FILE 001 - CvGameCoreDLL/CvPlayerAI.cpp - 32,289 lines - REVIEWED / NEW FINDINGS

F001 - provisional KI#330 - supported-config master/vassal tech-contact power-bias exemption depends on an unrelated toggle
Status: NEW CURRENT SAS DEFECT ROOT, static evidence strong.
Evidence:
- AI_doDiplo initializes bVassalsOrMaster=false.
- That relation flag is set only inside SAS_AI_DO_DIPLO_TECH_TRADE_MASTER_FROM_TO_VASSAL_CONTACT_PERCENT_OPTIMIZE.
- The separately configurable SAS_AI_DO_DIPLO_POWER_TECH_TRADE_OPTIMIZE later skips its power modifier only when !bVassalsOrMaster is false.
- XML documentation for the power-bias option explicitly promises that the bias is not applied between members of a master-vassal(s) locus, and both options are independently enable/disable toggles.
Consequence:
- With preferential master/vassal contact disabled but power-based tech contact enabled, master/vassal or sibling-vassal contacts are incorrectly treated as ordinary rivals for the power modifier.
- Defaults mask the problem because both options are enabled.
Repair direction:
- Compute the master/vassal-locus relationship independently of the preferential-contact feature toggle; use that relation both for the contact bonus and for the power-bias exemption as appropriate.

F002 - provisional KI#331 - restrictive AI canScrap policy silently vetoes callers that assume CvUnit::scrap succeeded
Status: NEW CURRENT SAS CONTRACT DEFECT ROOT, cross-file; first proven from CvPlayerAI.cpp/CvUnit.cpp/CvTeam.cpp. CvUnitAI.cpp may broaden the caller set during file 002.
Root contract:
- CvUnit::scrap() returns void and silently returns when canScrap()==false.
- SAS canScrap now rejects most AI units, especially in recommended obsolete-only mode.
- Several inherited callers were written when canScrap was essentially only a fighting-state gate and therefore treat the call as if removal succeeded.
Confirmed consequence A - AI_doMilitary financial disband quota:
- Candidate collection uses AI_disbandValue and does not filter canScrap().
- The loop calls scrap(), calls doDelayedDeath(), then increments iDisbandCount regardless of whether scrap() actually set delayed death.
- A rejected candidate therefore consumes the per-turn disband quota and can prevent a later genuinely eligible obsolete unit from being considered.
- Current data makes this practical: a unit can already be cheapened by AI_disbandValue when its ObsoleteTech is known while canScrap still rejects it until the configured obsolete-era delay has elapsed.
Confirmed consequence B - AI_doTurnUnitsPost upgrade cleanup:
- The cleanup path calls scrap(), doDelayedDeath(), then unconditionally sets bKilled=true.
- If canScrap rejects the unit, doDelayedDeath is a no-op because scrap() never set delayed death, but the still-living unit is treated as killed and skips the later ordinary upgrade path.
Confirmed consequence C - capitulation nuke disarm:
- CvTeam::setVassal inherited advc.143b disarms a capitulating team by calling scrap() on every nuke.
- Recommended SAS obsolete-only AI scrapping rejects units with ObsoleteTech=NONE before later UnitAI exceptions are reached.
- Current UNIT_TACTICAL_NUKE and UNIT_ICBM both use UNITAI_ICBM and ObsoleteTech=NONE, so AI capitulation can retain nukes while a human capitulation still removes them because the restrictive gates are AI-only.
Repair direction:
- Routine optional disband/cleanup callers should prefilter canScrap and only consume quota / mark killed after successful removal (or the API could explicitly return success).
- Mandatory game-rule actions such as capitulation disarm should use a forced/internal removal path that is not blocked by routine AI preservation policy.
- Do not globally weaken canScrap merely to satisfy mandatory callers.

F003 - KI#312 broadened - cluster-first tech redirect is bypassed by the independent high-research-progress gold-purchase fallback
Status: EXISTING KNOWN ROOT, not counted as a new defect.
Evidence:
- AI_doDiplo independently selects eBestReceiveTech and eBestProgressTech from the outsider partner.
- The SAS master/vassal cluster-first block can suppress eBestReceiveTech by setting it to NO_TECH when it believes a locus partner is an internal source.
- The later advc.550f fallback runs when eBestProgressTech != NO_TECH && eBestProgressTech != eBestReceiveTech and can immediately buy that progress tech from the same outsider for gold.
- Therefore setting eBestReceiveTech to NO_TECH can make the same outsider tech satisfy the fallback comparison rather than suppressing external acquisition consistently.
Classification:
- Keep this under KI#312 because that issue already covers the shared master/vassal internal-tech-supply predicate and the mismatch between merely-known / apparently-willing internal sources and legally/actually transferable techs.
- A centralized internal-supply/acquisition predicate should govern every external acquisition route that is intended to obey cluster-first policy.

F002 / provisional KI#331 - CvUnitAI caller expansion
Historical interaction:
- The important ancestry is a cross-commit contract change, not a changed caller. Base AdvCiv's canScrap was broadly permissive outside a narrow fighting-state restriction, so inherited `scrap(); return;` code effectively meant "remove this unit and stop."
- Practical 5945 introduced the recommended restrictive AI obsolete-only policy. Practical 5979 simplified that policy further, but the old CvUnitAI callers remained.
- This explains why the earlier commit-oriented archaeology could inspect practical 5945 locally and miss the later current-tree consequence: the defect lives in the interaction between a new callee contract and unchanged old callers.
- Current no-site Settler cleanup provides a useful internal contrast: `AI_scrapSettlerWithNoValidSite` deliberately uses `scrapInternal()` after its own guards because generic Settler scrap policy would otherwise block the mandatory cleanup.

Confirmed CvUnitAI consequences:
1. Worker self-cleanup no longer removes Workers under the recommended mode.
   - Current Worker unit variants use `UNITAI_WORKER` and `ObsoleteTech=NONE`.
   - The AI_workerMove cleanup branch can call `scrap(); return;` after productive actions fail.
   - canScrap rejects these Workers, so the call can only burn that Worker's turn; it can also prevent later stranded/safety fallback in that update.

2. Excess land Explorers can enter a repeated no-progress branch.
   - In inherited AI_exploreMove, an excess explorer that has positive ATTACK value converts to UNITAI_ATTACK; otherwise it calls `scrap(); return;`.
   - Current Scout/Explorer-style units explicitly disallow UNITAI_ATTACK, so the conversion fallback can be unavailable.
   - Scout/Explorer/Airship can remain blocked until their configured obsolete delay permits scrapping.
   - The current Robotic Recon Drone is stronger proof: it is an EXPLORE land unit, disallows ATTACK, has a positive production cost, and has `ObsoleteTech=NONE`. Once it reaches the excess-explorer cleanup with no earlier action, generic scrap has no future eligibility escape, so the inherited branch can repeatedly no-op and return.

3. Escort Sea cleanup can be impossible exactly when invoked.
   - The inherited inferior-escort cleanup branch is reached while `getCargo()>0`.
   - If role conversion fails, it executes `scrap(); return;`.
   - Recommended canScrap rejects units carrying cargo, so this removal is categorically vetoed at that call site.

4. Explore Sea cleanup can repeatedly no-op for cargo-capable ships.
   - Inherited obsolete-role and excess-explorer cleanup branches call `scrap(); return;` after failed role-conversion paths.
   - Recommended policy rejects any cargo-capable unit even when empty because `cargoSpace()>0`.
   - A cargo-capable ship assigned EXPLORE_SEA can therefore satisfy the caller's cleanup decision while being categorically ineligible for generic scrap.

5. Barbarian trapped-ship cleanup can no-op.
   - Inherited AI_barbAttackSeaMove periodically detects a ship that cannot generate a useful path and calls `scrap(); return;`.
   - A cargo-capable Barbarian ship such as a Galley is categorically rejected by the new policy, turning the intended cleanup into a lost turn whenever that branch fires.

6. Settler transport retirement after unloading can never use generic scrap in recommended mode.
   - Inherited AI_settlerSeaMove can unload a now-inferior old transport and then call `scrap(); return;`.
   - The transport remains cargo-capable even when empty, so canScrap rejects it solely from cargo capacity.

7. Doomed-city capturable-unit scorched-earth cleanup can report success without removing the unit.
   - AI_retreatToCity's inherited evacuation fallback can probabilistically scrap a capturable unit in an evacuating own city and immediately `return true`.
   - Settlers/Workers are representative capturable units and have no ObsoleteTech.
   - Recommended AI canScrap therefore rejects the removal, yet the caller reports that it handled the unit; the civilian can remain in the doomed city and be captured.

Together with file 001's financial-disband quota, upgrade-cleanup, and capitulation-nuke cases, these are one contract root rather than separate KIs.

Suggested root repair remains:
- Optional cleanup: check `canScrap()` before treating the decision as completed, and only consume quotas / return success / mark killed after actual removal.
- Mandatory removal: use an explicit forced/context-specific internal removal path.
- If practical, make the public scrap operation return success so callers cannot silently confuse a veto with removal.
- Do not weaken global canScrap just to accommodate mandatory callers.

F004 - provisional KI#332 - leader hover callers pass literal civilization 1, so trait help can resolve another civilization's units/buildings
Status: NEW CURRENT SAS UI DEFECT ROOT, current-data proof strong.
Native contract:
- CvDLLWidgetData::parseLeaderHelp treats widget data2, when not -1, as a CivilizationTypes value and passes it to CvGameTextMgr::parseLeaderTraits.
- parseLeaderTraits forwards that civilization to parseTraits.
- parseTraits uses the supplied civilization to resolve trait fast-production UnitClass/BuildingClass entries to civilization-specific UnitTypes/BuildingTypes.
Current caller problem:
- Multiple Python WIDGET_PEDIA_JUMP_TO_LEADER callers pass literal data2=1 rather than the leader/player's civilization.
- Current civilization index 1 is CIVILIZATION_ARABIA, so those hovers resolve fast-production classes through Arabia regardless of the displayed leader.
- Current literal-1 callers occur in Scoreboard, Hall of Fame, Info Screen, Military Advisor, and several Sevopedia pages (Religion, Civic, Music).
Concrete current consequence:
- Shaka is Aggressive and Zulu; the Zulu Barracks-class replacement is the Ikhanda, and that class receives the Aggressive double-production trait effect.
- Arabia has no Barracks-class replacement. A literal-Arabia context therefore makes Shaka's trait hover identify ordinary Barracks instead of his actual Ikhanda for that fast-production line.
- Equivalent wrong-resolution opportunities exist for leaders whose civilization replaces other trait-affected classes.
Ancestry/accounting:
- The old commit-oriented archaeology had already source-proven this interaction while examining a later caller, but deliberately held it unnumbered pending firmer ancestry.
- Local Base AdvCiv 1.14 retains literal data2=1 in its inherited BUG Scoreboard and Sevopedia Index leader widgets under the same DLL contract. Practical 4732 is an early SAS-lineage occurrence, not the upstream birth of the convention. Current SAS inherited the Scoreboard case, independently replaced/corrected the Index path and later broadened the wrong convention across additional screens.
Repair direction:
- Callers that know the real player/civilization should pass that CivilizationTypes value.
- Generic leader contexts should reuse the same canonical leader-to-civilization selection used by the main Sevopedia Leader path.
- Do not blindly substitute -1: parseLeaderHelp currently uses data2==-1 to suppress leader hover help entirely.

F005 - KI#21 reopened/broadened - live-unit Cannot Enter help ignores current passable-tech state
Status: EXISTING KNOWN ROOT, not counted as a new defect.
Evidence:
- The actual-unit setUnitHelp path lists pUnit->getTerrainImpassable / getFeatureImpassable directly when building the map-view Cannot Enter line. These are raw unit-info restrictions.
- The detailed/basic Sevopedia unit help separately understands TerrainPassableTech / FeaturePassableTech and can render an Until Tech relationship.
- CvUnit::canMoveInto permits a raw impassable terrain/feature once the unit's team owns the configured passable tech; own-team cultural-border exceptions can also make the raw flag insufficient by itself.
Concrete current data:
- UNIT_WORKBOAT has TERRAIN_OCEAN marked impassable and TECH_ASTRONOMY as its TerrainPassableTech.
- After the Work Boat's team researches Astronomy, the engine allows Ocean movement while the live-unit hover can still print Cannot Enter: Ocean from the raw flag.
Classification:
- This is an incomplete dynamic-state part of KI#21's map-view Cannot Enter display feature, not a distinct root.
- An older archaeology note had concluded no current raw-impassable + pass-tech contradiction existed; current Work Boat XML disproves that old data conclusion and should supersede it.
Repair direction:
- Build live-unit restriction text from effective current movement state, or at minimum account for the team's passable tech before printing the raw restriction. Preserve the cultural-border nuance as applicable.

FALSE POSITIVES / NON-FINDINGS RECORDED FOR FILE 003
- The source contains `static const SpecialistTypes eDefaultSpecialist = (SpecialistTypes)eDefaultSpecialist;` instead of GC.getDEFAULT_SPECIALIST(). Static zero-initialization makes it enum 0; current enum 0 is SPECIALIST_CITIZEN, which is also the current default specialist. The typo is presently exactly masked and was already recorded by the old archaeology; not promoted again.
- The custom building-power help contains an explicit clean-or-dirty uncertainty branch. Engine tracing resolves the rule: area clean power overrides dirty-power health classification. Current XML has only Three Gorges Dam with area clean power, and it is not itself dirty, so the uncertain simultaneous area-clean+dirty branch is unreachable in current data. No new root.
- Static Coal Plant-style health prose can be context-approximate when a city is already area-clean-powered, but this is inherited/static-help behavior and no SAS-created correctness root was established.
- `buildBRepeatString` is coherent with repeatable-tech ownership semantics; current Tech XML contains no bRepeat=1 entries anyway.
- Civilopedia suppression of generic obsolete/replacement/requirement prose was checked against the dedicated Sevopedia panels; the corresponding relationships remain represented, including Manhattan Project project requirements.
- Worker-speed summary uses the same player-wide accumulator fed by current Tech/Civic/Building worker-speed modifiers; no category mismatch established.
- Zero-base collateral capability rows remain intentional KI#324/KI#324.2 behavior and were not reopened.
- Promotion icon-size plumbing, null guards, billboard colors, score/trade/event/espionage helpers, and numerous small changed methods are presentation/caching/hardening changes with no new semantic defect found.
- No additional file-scope SAS helper layer exists in CvGameTextMgr.cpp that would sit outside the member-function comparison.


FILE 004 - CvGameCoreDLL/CvPlayer.cpp - 20,672 lines - REVIEWED / CLEAN CURRENT

RESULT
- No new current defect root was established in CvPlayer.cpp.
- All 104 member-function pairs with executable/token-level differences versus AdvCiv 1.14 were classified; the only genuinely new CvPlayer member is the key-early-strategic-bonus helper.
- CvPlayer.h was compared at token level after stripping comments/formatting. Its only semantic addition versus AdvCiv 1.14 is the declaration of that helper; the many apparent header differences are signature collapsing/comment movement.
- File-scope additions were audited separately. They are the SASGameRecord diplomacy text/logging helpers and do not alter gameplay state.
- The file therefore closes as a useful clean result rather than manufacturing a finding from tuning, caching, logging, or unsupported old-save compatibility.

CROSS-CONTRACT / DATA CHECKS
1. Save read/write cleanup is internally paired for the supported current format.
   - CvPlayer::write still writes uiFlag 22.
   - Current CvPlayer::read consumes the same current-format fields that AdvCiv 1.14 read when uiFlag==22; removed branches are old migration/legacy representations.
   - Same-version serialization therefore remains paired. The project does not promise cross-version save compatibility, so accepting an older save without migration is not promoted as a supported-current defect.

2. Static GlobalDefine caching does not currently freeze a dynamically changed CvPlayer value.
   - A mechanical scan collected the GlobalDefine names read by current CvPlayer.cpp and compared them against current runtime setDefineINT/setDefineFLOAT/setDefineSTRING usage in C++ and Python/map scripts.
   - No overlap was found for the 103 define names used by this file.
   - The static-local caches therefore preserve current runtime semantics under today's code/configuration.

3. New key-early-strategic-bonus helper is explicit tuning, not a hidden contract mismatch.
   - getNumAvailableBonusesHaveAnyKeyEarlyStrategicBonuses checks Copper, Iron, Horse, Camel and Elephants through getNumAvailableBonuses.
   - All five define-resolved current BONUS_* names exist in CIV4BonusInfos.xml and the helper guards NO_BONUS.
   - getNumAvailableBonuses is capital-plot-group scoped; comments on both declaration and implementation explicitly call out that capital-network-only approximation.
   - Current callers use the helper to relax early siege composition/production caps. The approximation can make an isolated city's local options differ from the player-level signal, but that limitation is documented and intentional tuning rather than a newly proven correctness failure.

4. TechAcquisitionCause arguments added at CvPlayer call sites are bookkeeping context only.
   - Current CvTeam research helpers forward the cause to setHasTech when progress completes a technology.
   - CvTeam::setHasTech forwards it to CvEventReporter::techAcquired; CvEventReporter uses it for SASGameRecord provenance while preserving the original Python tech-acquired event signature.
   - No research/ownership legality branch depends on the cause enum.

5. Diplomacy trade-row art additions resolve against current data.
   - INTERFACE_TECH_VASSAL, INTERFACE_TECH_OPENBORDERS, INTERFACE_TECH_DEFENSIVEPACT and INTERFACE_TECH_PERMALLIANCE all exist in current CIV4ArtDefines_Interface.xml.
   - Target-team leader icons retain the pre-existing valid-team assumption and guard NO_PLAYER/dead leaders before dereferencing the player/leader art.

6. SASGameRecord hooks inspected from this file are observational.
   - updateSASGameRecordPlayerTurnState increments only GameRecord accounting arrays.
   - plot-change hooks buffer/log old-vs-new plot state after the gameplay mutation; they do not mutate the plot.
   - diplomacy helpers format already-supplied event context and call the logger.
   - technology acquisition source plumbing is likewise observational as described above.

FILE 005 - CvGameCoreDLL/CvCityAI.cpp - 19,615 lines - REVIEWED / EXISTING ROOTS BROADENED OR REOPENED

RESULT
- No independent new defect root was established in CvCityAI.cpp; this file instead exposed several incomplete existing fixes/contracts that are better kept under their original KI families.
- Existing KI#213 is materially broadened from a Worker-demand reporting/cache mismatch into production, transport, relocation and evacuation consequences.
- Existing KI#48 should gain a focused follow-up (recommended KI#48.3): the building-value prefilter's growth signal subtracts unhealthiness twice and does not perform its own documented final zero clamp.
- Existing KI#306 is reopened/broadened: the government-center maintenance cache was fixed, but the sibling top-production-city cache retains the same insufficient within-turn invalidation model and can feed hard Wonder eligibility gates.
- Existing KI#70 is reopened: the forced-specialist sanitizer checks current assigned-specialist validity before deciding whether to trim an invalid forced count, allowing the historical invalid Artist/assert state to remain reachable.
- Existing KI#74 is reopened/broadened: AI_bestSpreadUnit still returns an enum comparison against NULL, so NO_UNIT (-1) is reported as Boolean success; caller guards mask much of the damage but do not repair the helper contract.
- Existing KI#11 is broadened: the current debug-map Actual/Standard Build Values display still calls AI_getImprovementValue, which now deliberately returns zero for every land plot.
- The large production, building, citizen, Worker, Settler, military, hurry/draft, process/project, improvement, helper and serialization surfaces otherwise survived the current-data/cross-contract review without another correctness root being manufactured.

F006 - KI#213 broadened - split Worker-demand architecture can now suppress or evacuate Workers, not merely undercount demand
Status: EXISTING KNOWN ROOT, materially stronger current consequences.
Root recap:
- Current land Worker planning is intentionally handled by CvUnitAI::AI_bestCityBuild rather than the old city-side land evaluator/cache.
- AI_updateWorkersHaveAndNeeded nevertheless still derives important demand signals from the old city-side best-build state plus partial compensating heuristics.
- A worked, unimproved no-bonus Flood Plains tile is a concrete current job that the custom Worker policy can improve but the city-side demand workaround can miss, yielding AI_neededWorkers(area)==0 despite useful land work remaining.
Newly proven consequences in the current tree:
1. Concrete CvCityAI::AI_chooseUnit can treat AI_neededWorkers(area)==0 as an area-usefulness veto and suppress Worker production.
2. CvUnitAI transport/relocation logic can use that same false zero to reject ferrying or unloading a Worker into the area.
3. Existing Workers in the area can be classified as belonging to a completed area and become candidates for the KI#192 completed-area evacuation machinery even though the custom land evaluator still sees useful work there.
Classification / repair direction:
- Keep one KI#213 root. The right repair is a shared authoritative Worker-job/demand predicate (or demand source) that sees the same land jobs as the custom Worker planner; patching each consumer independently would preserve the architectural split.

F007 - KI#48 broadened; recommended KI#48.3 - AI_buildingValue effective-food signal double-counts unhealthy-food loss
Status: EXISTING BUILDING-VALUE FAMILY, current implementation defect confirmed.
Current code:
- AI_buildingValue obtains `iFoodDifference = foodDifference(false, true)` and `iHealthLevel = goodHealth() - badHealth() + ...`.
- It then computes `iEffectiveFood = max(0, iFoodDifference) - max(0, -iHealthLevel)` and only later forces zero for nonpositive happiness surplus.
Engine contract:
- CvCity::foodDifference(..., true) subtracts foodConsumption().
- foodConsumption() already incorporates healthRate(), so unhealthy population has already reduced iFoodDifference before AI_buildingValue receives it.
- Subtracting max(0,-iHealthLevel) therefore charges the same unhealthiness a second time.
- The nearby worked example for a starving/unhealthy city explicitly says the result should be clamped to zero conceptually, but the implementation has no final `max(0, ...)` after the health subtraction and can leave iEffectiveFood negative.
Consequence:
- iEffectiveFood is not merely descriptive; it feeds hard force/skip gates for food-kept and health-building decisions. Thresholds can therefore flip because a mathematically wrong growth signal is used before the ordinary detailed building valuation runs.
Ancestry / classification:
- This logic was introduced in practical 4950 as part of the broad KI#48 building-value prefilter family. Treating it as KI#48.3 is cleaner than assigning a new root number.
Repair direction:
- Do not subtract current unhealthiness again from foodDifference when bIgnoreFood=false / food consumption is included. Derive the intended growth signal from one authoritative net-food quantity, then apply the documented nonnegative clamp and happiness gate.

F008 - KI#306 reopened/broadened - sibling top-hammer city cache can go stale during sequential city turns
Status: EXISTING CACHE-INVALIDATION ROOT; earlier repair was partial.
Current cache:
- AI_buildingValue keeps static per-player best/second/third base-production values keyed only by game turn and number of cities.
- Those rankings feed exact hard Wonder filters, including top-2/top-3 and +5-hammer boundary tests.
Concrete within-turn invalidation:
- Building completion can change another city's getBaseYieldRate(YIELD_PRODUCTION) without changing the turn or city count.
- Current Angkor Wat and Cristo Redentor each grant +1 production to Priest specialists through SpecialistYieldChanges.
- CvPlayer building processing applies those effects through changeSpecialistExtraYield(); that calls updateExtraSpecialistYield() across the player's cities, and CvCity::updateExtraSpecialistYield changes each affected city's base yield rate immediately when Priests are assigned.
- City turns/production choices are sequential. A top-HPT cache populated before such a completion can therefore be consumed later in the same player turn after one or more city production values have changed.
Consequence:
- Even a one-hammer stale ranking can cross the prefilter's exact top-city/+5 thresholds and change whether a later city is allowed to consider a Wonder.
Ancestry / classification:
- KI#306 fixed the same practical-5281 invalidation pattern for the government-center maintenance ranking cache while explicitly leaving the separate top-production cache in place. Reopen/broaden KI#306 rather than create a new cache root.
Repair direction:
- Avoid a turn+city-count cache for values that can change during city processing, or invalidate/recompute on relevant yield changes. A generation/revision counter for player city production state would be more precise than adding more ad hoc conditions.

F009 - KI#70 reopened - forced-specialist sanitizer can skip an already-invalid forced count
Status: EXISTING BASE/ADVCIV CITIZEN ROOT, current failure remains reachable.
Current sanitizer shape:
- AI_assignWorkingPlots enters its repair block only when `!isSpecialistValid(eSpecialist)`.
- Inside that block it trims assigned specialists above max capacity and separately trims forced specialists above max capacity.
Contract mismatch:
- isSpecialistValid(e, 0) tests whether the city's current assigned count can remain valid; it does not mean the independent forced-specialist target is <= capacity.
Concrete state:
- Artist assigned=0, max=0, forced=1: current assignment is valid, so the outer sanitizer condition is false and forced=1 is not trimmed.
- The later forced-specialist assignment pass sees the forced target and can assign the Artist anyway, after which the historical KI#70 debug assertion is reached because assigned=1 exceeds capacity=0.
- In non-assert builds this is not purely diagnostic: the invalid forced assignment can exist transiently.
Classification / repair direction:
- Reopen KI#70. Sanitize assigned count and forced target against capacity independently; do not gate forced-count repair on validity of the current assigned count.

F010 - KI#74 reopened/broadened - AI_bestSpreadUnit Boolean return still treats NO_UNIT as success
Status: EXISTING BASE/ADVCIV HELPER-CONTRACT ROOT, caller guards only mask it.
Current code:
- AI_bestSpreadUnit ends with `return (*eBestSpreadUnit != NULL);`.
- UnitTypes uses NO_UNIT=-1; NULL is integer/pointer zero in this comparison context.
- Therefore a caller that initializes `eBestSpreadUnit=NO_UNIT` and receives no candidate gets `(-1 != 0) == true`.
Current consequences / masking:
- Current production and governor callers contain assertions and explicit `eBestSpreadUnit != NO_UNIT` guards before actually trying to choose the unit, preventing the worst queue/action consequences.
- Those guards explain why the issue can look addressed, but the helper's Boolean contract itself remains false and the corresponding debug assertion remains reachable when no unit is selected but the helper reports success.
Classification / repair direction:
- Reopen KI#74 rather than add a new finding. The root fix is to compare against NO_UNIT (or return `iBestValue > 0` / an explicit success state consistently), then keep caller guards as defense in depth.

F011 - KI#11 broadened - land Actual/Standard Build Values debug help is structurally empty under the custom Worker evaluator split
Status: EXISTING DEBUG-MAP DISPLAY ROOT, no gameplay effect.
Evidence:
- Current CvCityAI::AI_getImprovementValue explicitly returns 0 (and NO_BUILD when requested) immediately for every non-water plot so the custom CvUnitAI land Worker policy has exclusive control.
- CvGameTextMgr's debug-map `Actual Build Values` and `Standard Build Values` paths still call AI_getImprovementValue for the working city and candidate improvements.
Consequence:
- On land, those diagnostics no longer describe the values driving the real Worker AI and can show empty/zero build-value information even when the custom Worker planner has a strong valid job.
Classification / repair direction:
- Keep under KI#11's existing inaccurate/weird debug-map display umbrella. If these diagnostics are retained, point them at the authoritative custom land evaluator (or clearly label the legacy water-only evaluator) rather than reviving the old land decision path.

AUDIT NOTE - direct Artist culture cache has an invalidation mismatch but is not promoted
- SAS_AI_directArtistCultureValuePercent caches one result per turn/player/city.
- AI citizen assignment can run before city turns and then again after that city's doCulture/doPlotCulture work in the same game turn, so the second call can reuse a pre-culture control/contestation verdict.
- A city's own culture update moves the relevant control tests toward more local control, so the demonstrated stale direction chiefly delays the intended Artist dampening until the next turn.
- This is a real cache-granularity/one-turn-lag observation, but no hard invariant or sufficiently serious current consequence was established. Keep as an audit note unless empirical evidence shows larger impact.

F012 - provisional KI#333 - emergency Port priority can preempt the documented stronger emergency-defense branch
Status: NEW CURRENT SAS AI-PRODUCTION PRIORITY DEFECT ROOT, birth/history/current-data evidence strong.
Birth and documented contract:
- Practical 5919 / bd2153482f introduced the mature-coastal emergency water-hammer / Port branch.
- The same birth commit's GlobalDefines documentation states that defense emergencies still run earlier in CvCity::doTurn so Castle/defense can go before Port when needed.
- The same practical's Main Changes Guide likewise says immediate danger / defense logic still runs earlier, allowing Walls/Castle to go first when needed.
Current control flow:
- CvCity::doTurn actually runs the emergency Harbor block, then the practical-5919 Port/water-hammer block, and only afterward enters the Walls/Castle emergency-defense block.
- The Port branch is gated by `!bDanger`, but not by `!bAtWar`, `!bEnemyStrong`, or the broader defense predicate.
- On success SASTryEmergencyBuilding pushes Port and sets `bEmergencyBuilding=true`.
- The later defense section is inside `if (!bEmergencyBuilding && !bHuman)`, so a successfully forced Port suppresses the entire defense branch for that turn/queue choice.
Concrete supported state:
- Mature ocean-coastal AI city, Navigation known, Port absent and constructible, sufficiently low production or food surplus, not locally flagged by AI_isDanger(), while its team is at war with a significantly stronger enemy (or otherwise satisfies the broader at-war defense predicate).
- Port passes its local gate and is forced first.
- The defense predicate would have requested Walls/Castle through bEnemyStrong / bAtWar, but is never evaluated because Port has already set bEmergencyBuilding.
- Current Port is a normal positive-cost Navigation building and Walls remains independently constructible in overlapping eras, so this is not a theoretical unsupported XML state.
Why the old commit-oriented pass missed it:
- The earlier archaeology inspected practical 5919 locally and correctly found the Port legality helper itself safe for mature ocean-coastal, non-danger cities.
- The defect is an insertion-order interaction with an older neighboring branch, exactly the kind of cross-commit/current-file interaction this second file-oriented pass is intended to expose.
Repair direction:
- Make the actual priority match the documented contract. The cleanest structure is to evaluate/force the broader emergency defense branch before Port, while retaining the explicit Harbor-first policy if that remains intentional; alternatively gate Port on the full defense-emergency predicate being false.
- Preserve the existing local-danger exclusion, but do not treat `!AI_isDanger()` as equivalent to `no strategic defense emergency`, because the defense branch deliberately also covers stronger enemies and ongoing wars without immediate local danger.

EXISTING ROOTS / CURRENT BEHAVIOR CONFIRMED IN FILE 006
- KI#51: the hard no-production safety net remains the intended workaround. Current fallback detection excludes a valid Process and is limited to genuinely empty or invalid queues.
- KI#310: current water-heavy fallback is repaired: if Harbor/Port/Lighthouse attempts all fail, control continues into the same restricted military fallback instead of ending without production.
- KI#313: the human convenience BFC Artist helper still lacks provenance for the forced-Artist increment; its cleanup can decrement a human-forced Artist that was not created by the helper. This is existing KI#313, not a new root.
- KI#44.5: the debug assertion in changeSpecialistCount fires on every positive default-Citizen addition, including legitimate inherited UI paths. This is intentionally broad instrumentation around the already-documented Citizen assignment/fallback family and is not counted again.
- KI#162 / KI#162.2 / KI#162.3: getProductionBarPercentages contains the expected owner/registration, active-player and typed-order hardening. The current typed production calculation is consistent with the inherited production-bar semantics; no new crash root was established.

FALSE POSITIVES / NON-FINDINGS RECORDED FOR FILE 006

In theory a future define retuned to a production-cost -1 class could make the helper clear the queue, fail to push, and still return true. All five current configured emergency classes map to ordinary positive-cost buildings for supported civilizations (Harbor, Port, Lighthouse, Walls, Castle; Barbarian Port maps to NONE), so no current supported defect is established. Popping an invalid old head before push does not create a current canTrain failure path for the already selected unit.

The logging helpers mutate only GameRecord aggregates/log output, and their placement preserves inherited unit/building/project gameplay-event order. Logging a produced air unit before possible no-capacity destruction is intentional production accounting.

Removal of legacy uiFlag branches therefore does not create a current same-version save mismatch; cross-version save compatibility remains intentionally unsupported.

F013 - provisional KI#334 - Great-General death notifications can reveal the killer civilization to observers that know only the victim; SAS Timeline persistence preserves the leak
Status: NEW CURRENT HIDDEN-INFORMATION DEFECT ROOT; inherited transient leak, SAS persistence amplification; source/visibility contract evidence strong.
Inherited notification contract failure:
- CvUnit::kill handles a Great-General death by notifying the owner, killer and eligible third parties.
- For a third-party observer, the branch checks only whether the observer has met the victim owner's team (or is a spectator).
- The actual TXT_KEY_MISC_GENERAL_KILLED text names both the victim civilization and the killer civilization: the General "has died in combat against %s3_Civ".
- There is no corresponding check that the observer has met the killer team before GET_PLAYER(ePlayer).getCivilizationShortDescription() is inserted.
- This victim-only gate and fully identified killer text are inherited from AdvCiv 1.14, so the transient on-screen leak predates SAS.
Normal diplomacy-event contrast:
- Comparable two-party public announcements use both-participant visibility. For example, CvTeam::announceWar suppresses the third-party announcement unless the observer has met both the aggressor and the target; permanent-alliance announcements follow the same principle.
- The Great-General branch therefore violates the surrounding information-visibility convention rather than merely choosing a different cosmetic message.
SAS persistence amplification:
- Practical 6123 / 574e50ba53, while fixing KI#204 Great-General AI/UI issues, added a coordinate-less REPLAY_MESSAGE_MAJOR_EVENT containing the same fully identified victim + killer text.
- The birth comment assumes the original notification already exposes the event safely to met observers, but that inherited notification itself has the victim-only visibility flaw above.
- Current Info Screen Timeline spoiler filtering can evaluate the replay message player (the victim) and optional plot visibility. This entry has the victim as iPlayer and no coordinates, and it carries no killer-team visibility metadata.
- Therefore an observer who has met the victim but not the killer can retain/read the killer civilization identity in replay/Timeline history as well as potentially receiving the transient notification.
Repair direction:
- Gate the fully identified third-party transient message on the observer knowing both victim and killer, or sanitize the unknown participant rather than naming it.
- Do not persist a fully identified killer under a victim-only replay record unless Timeline/replay has enough metadata to apply both-participant visibility. A sanitized replay variant or explicit visibility-aware metadata would be safer than relying on the victim player ID alone.
- Preserve spectator/debug behavior explicitly rather than weakening normal observer privacy.

F014 - provisional KI#335 - Military Advisor Battles vassal perspective exposes the vassal's hidden battle-location map knowledge to the human/master observer
Status: NEW CURRENT SAS ADVISOR VISIBILITY DEFECT ROOT; separate feature/fix surface from KI#224 and KI#232; current default configuration exposes it.
Shared advisor contract:
- SASUtils' advisor-perspective helper explicitly allows a master's vassal as a selectable perspective outside debug mode while promising that this does not reveal unrelated players or hidden map data.
- The Main Changes Guide states the same user-facing privacy intent for vassal advisor perspectives.
Birth/lineage:
- Practical 5757 / 62ceac98ee introduced the persistent Battles table with exact stored battle coordinates, plot-row camera targeting and debug logging.
- Practical 5758 / 4481a5877f then enabled the shared debug/vassal perspective selector for the Battles page (`bAllowVassalPerspective=True`) without adding an observer-team reveal filter to the pre-existing battle-location data.
- Later plot-context work additionally stores/renders terrain, feature, hill/peak and city context, amplifying the same location-information root.
Current behavior:
- drawBattleHistory reads SASBattleHistory entries for `self.iActivePlayer`, which can legitimately be a selected vassal rather than the real active human.
- Battle entries persist exact X/Y from the battle plot. Current plot-context display is enabled by default and derives/renders terrain, feature, hill/peak and city context for the stored location.
- The Plot column caches the exact coordinates; clicking it hides the advisor and calls CyCamera().JustLookAtPlot(CyMap().plot(iX,iY)) with no reveal test against the actual human observer team.
- The Battles LOG button is enabled by default and prints the stored location/context without an active-observer reveal check.
Reachability / scope correction:
- Master-vassal teams can possess different revealed-plot knowledge; vassalization does not merge the vassal's revealed map into the master's map. A vassal can therefore have fought on a plot that the human master has never revealed.
- CvTeam::setVassal DOES synchronize diplomatic `hasMet` relationships between master and vassal. Therefore this finding should NOT claim that selecting a vassal reveals an otherwise-unmet opponent civilization; the live defect is hidden map/location/context knowledge.
Classification relative to existing KIs:
- KI#224 covers the World Advisor's vassal-perspective hidden map/resource leak.
- KI#232 separately covers the Policy Advisor's corresponding hidden Holy City/HQ subject-vs-observer leak.
- Battles is another independently implemented advisor feature with its own persisted coordinates, render path, log path and camera action, so keep it as a separate root rather than stretching KI#224's World-Advisor fix surface.
Repair direction:
- Keep the selected vassal as the battle-history SUBJECT, but outside debug mode use the actual active human's team as the OBSERVER for map information.
- For battle plots the human observer has not revealed, hide/sanitize exact X/Y and terrain/feature/hill/city context, suppress those fields from LOG output, and disable the Plot camera action.
- Debug/spectator modes can retain unrestricted inspection explicitly.

KI#331 - CvUnit callee-side confirmation / robustness note
- Current CvUnit::scrap() remains a void wrapper: `if (!canScrap()) return; scrapInternal();`.
- The SAS obsolete-only AI policy contains early reject/allow paths before the inherited final plot-fighting guard. This is awkward defensive ordering, but no supported live caller was proved able to exploit that ordering as an independent gameplay defect: human manual deletion bypasses the AI policy, normal AI financial scrapping occurs in pre-turn processing, ordinary UnitAI cleanup runs through normal group updates, and delayed death itself will not complete while the unit is fighting.
- Keep the guard-placement cleanup as robustness debt when repairing KI#331, not as another KI. The substantive current root remains callers assuming a silent-veto void API succeeded.

FALSE POSITIVES / CLEAN TAIL CHECKS FROM FILE 007
- Battle History initially looked as if vassal perspective could expose an opponent civilization unknown to the human master. This was rejected after tracing CvTeam::setVassal: master/vassal `hasMet` relations are synchronized. KI#335 is deliberately limited to hidden map/location/context knowledge.
- Battle History funnels the DLL's `COMBAT_LIMIT` and true `WITHDRAWAL` non-lethal endings into one Retreated outcome. That would be semantically lossy if partial-lethal combat units existed, but current CIV4UnitInfos has zero combat-capable units with `0 < iCombatLimit < 100`: fighting units use combat limit 100, while 0 belongs to nonfighters. Current data therefore makes the COMBAT_LIMIT branch unreachable; future/mod-mod robustness only, no KI.
- canScrap's AI-policy early return before the inherited plot-fighting guard was traced through current callers and delayed-death scheduling; no supported current exploit was established. Keep as robustness debt under KI#331.
- canUpgrade's `bUpgradeCityKnown` extension is used with upgrade types established by getUpgradeCity and preserves the ordinary gold/ROI/other gates; this matches the KI#188.3.3 fix rather than creating a second upgrade-legality hole.
- getUpgradeCity's optional chosen-upgrade output is initialized to NO_UNIT and set on successful direct/best-city results; no uninitialized output path found.
- Current espionage GameRecord snapshots were compared with CvPlayer::doEspionageMission's actual calculations. Production destroyed, stolen gold, inserted culture, poison/unhappy/revolt values, player-anarchy duration and counterespionage duration use the corresponding engine calculations/game-speed scalers; no logging-value mismatch established.
- Air strike/interception, bombard/pillage, blockade, nuke, range/flanking/collateral combat, Great-Person actions, upgrade, move/setXY and combat-message changes are logging/accounting hooks, enum renames or cached constants after contract review; no independent gameplay mutation survived.
- maxCombatStr changes are the already-fixed KI#211/KI#212 lineage; no new combat-strength root established.
- Current read/write serialization was mechanically paired at 86 corresponding operations. Removal of old uiFlag branches is unsupported legacy-save cleanup, not a current same-version mismatch.
- All cached GlobalDefines used by changed CvUnit paths were checked against current runtime define mutation; no behavior-sensitive overlap was found.
- Header changes (testSpyIntercepted phase label, canUpgrade bUpgradeCityKnown, getUpgradeCity output, impassability wrappers, scrapInternal exposure) match their implementations/callers; no ABI/contract mismatch established.
- Whole-file sentinel/null/static/lifetime scan found no extra enum-vs-NULL defect, stale mutable cache or post-kill dereference in the changed surface.


FILE 008 - CvGameCoreDLL/CvGame.cpp - 10,841 lines - REVIEWED / TEN NEW FINDINGS

RESULT
- CvGame.cpp is closed after both a current-vs-AdvCiv semantic-diff pass and an independent whole-file inherited-code/invariant pass. The second layer was especially productive: several defects live in untouched inherited code and therefore were not natural targets for the older commit-oriented archaeology.
- Ten new current roots survived full reachability/history/data checks: F015-F024 (provisional KI#336-KI#345).
- The findings split into one SAS lifecycle/configuration defect (F015), one AdvCiv-replay x SAS-Timeline visibility interaction (F016), seven inherited starting/score/global-warming/vote/victory defects from AdvCiv/BtS lineage (F017-F022/F024), and one inherited BtS Lock Mods defect (F023).
- Current-format CvGame serialization was mechanically paired at 88 corresponding read/write operations in the same order; no same-version stream mismatch was found.
- Multiple attractive false positives were explicitly killed so they are not rediscovered later.

F015 - provisional KI#336 - dynamic map-heaviness fallback is computed before starting plots exist and therefore caches a neutral result
Status: NEW CURRENT SAS LIFECYCLE DEFECT ROOT; current default/support configuration reaches it.
Intended contract:
- SAS map-heaviness logic first classifies scripts listed explicitly as land-heavy or naval-heavy.
- Scripts intentionally absent from both lists fall back to a dynamic start-area classifier that counts major-civilization starting plots and classifies the map from those areas.
- Current XML deliberately leaves Big_and_Small, Hub, Medium_and_Small, RandomScriptMap, Shuffle and not_too_Big_or_Small unspecified so this dynamic fallback can decide them.
Current lifecycle failure:
- CvGame::initDiplomacy explicitly documents that the EXE invokes it after map generation but before assigning starting plots. recomputeMapnameHeaviness() is called at the end of that function.
- regenerateMap likewise clears/has no starts, generates the map, calls recomputeMapnameHeaviness(), and only later reaches setInitialItems()/starting-plot assignment.
- The dynamic branch therefore normally sees no major-civilization starting plots. With iMajorCivs <= 1 it leaves both heaviness flags false.
- Those booleans are then cached and serialized correctly, so the problem is computation timing rather than persistence corruption.
Lineage:
- Practical 5032 introduced the script-name cache/call sites.
- Practical 5402 added the start-area dynamic fallback and persistence without moving the computation to a post-start phase.
Consequence:
- Supported scripts intentionally delegated to dynamic classification normally remain neutral instead of receiving their intended land/naval heuristic classification. AI logic consuming these flags can therefore miss its map-specific tuning.
Repair direction:
- Split script-name classification from the start-area-dependent phase, or explicitly recompute the dynamic fallback after starting plots have been assigned (including scenario/regeneration lifecycle considerations).

F016 - provisional KI#337 - AP/UN passed-resolution replay entries bypass the live voting-member audience when surfaced through Timeline
Status: NEW CURRENT HIDDEN-INFORMATION INTERACTION; AdvCiv replay record plus SAS Timeline visibility model.
Live audience contract:
- CvGame::doVoteResults sends the detailed vote result only to voting members (with explicit spectator handling).
- Passed non-Secretary-General resolutions are also persisted as coordinate-less REPLAY_MESSAGE_MAJOR_EVENT entries with NO_PLAYER.
- The replay text can contain the vote source, Secretary-General team identity, vote totals and target civilization/city/resolution details.
Timeline interaction:
- CvReplayInfo::createInfo(activePlayer) copies the replay-message stream; its player argument does not filter individual messages by their original live audience.
- Current Info Screen Timeline spoiler filtering can test replay-player diplomacy, plot reveal, plot owner and the special unknown-player color.
- This entry has NO_PLAYER and no coordinates/owner metadata, so none of those generic gates can reconstruct the voting-member-only audience.
Consequence:
- A civilization that was not a voting member and therefore did not receive the live detailed result can later inspect that detailed record in Timeline.
Scope/control:
- This is not a blanket NO_PLAYER replay defect. The coordinate-less global-warming activation replay is deliberately broadcast live to all major civilizations, so global Timeline visibility is consistent there.
- F013/KI#334 Great-General replay leakage has a related metadata limitation but a different observer contract (met killer vs voting membership); keep roots separate unless a generalized replay-visibility architecture deliberately repairs both.
Repair direction:
- Persist enough audience/visibility metadata for vote-result replay entries, omit/sanitize private detail in the globally visible replay text, or teach Timeline a vote-source membership rule that can be evaluated for the observer.

F017 - provisional KI#338 - starting-location volatility safeguard tests the human's old start instead of the sorted site they are about to receive
Status: NEW CURRENT INHERITED ADVCIV DEFECT ROOT; refactor regression proved by history.
Evidence:
- applyStartingLocHandicaps sorts actual starting sites into apStartingSitesByValue and ultimately assigns player at slot i to apStartingSitesByValue[i].
- The human volatility safeguard instead sets pStart from GET_PLAYER(ePlayer).getStartingPlot(), i.e. that player's pre-reassignment/original start.
- Its neighboring alternative/swap tests correctly inspect apStartingSitesByValue[iSwapPlayerIndex].
Lineage:
- Before practical 2708's starting-site refactor, the safeguard tested startPlots[i], the actual site associated with the slot being evaluated.
- The refactor changed storage/ordering but accidentally changed this one check to the player's old plot.
Consequence:
- A human can be assigned a volatile site without the safeguard noticing because their old site was safe, or can be swapped unnecessarily because their old site was volatile even though the new assigned slot is safe.
Repair direction:
- Use apStartingSitesByValue[i] for pStart so the safeguard evaluates the site the human is actually about to receive.

F018 - provisional KI#339 - starting-handicap collision randomization computes a random rotation and then never applies it
Status: NEW CURRENT INHERITED ADVCIV DEFECT ROOT; practical tie/collision bias.
Evidence:
- sortByStartingLocHandicap separately processes human and AI agents, draws a random ordinal, walks to that agent and stores its actual source index in iRandOffset.
- The placement pass then iterates the source list from index 0 without using iRandOffset at all.
- The random draw therefore consumes map RNG but has no effect on ordering.
Lineage:
- Pre-refactor starting-site code used the random offset to rotate traversal, preventing fixed player-slot priority when equal/colliding StartingLocationPercent values mapped agents to the same positions.
- The extracted/refactored helper retained calculation of the offset but dropped the rotated traversal.
Consequence:
- Players/teams sharing the same starting-location handicap can resolve collisions deterministically according to input/player ordering rather than the intended randomized order.
Repair direction:
- Restore a rotation over the eligible source-agent order using the computed starting index (or directly shuffle/rotate the eligible list in an equivalent deterministic-map-RNG-safe way).

F019 - provisional KI#340 - team-start fallback uses compact alive-team count as an offset in the absolute team-ID space
Status: NEW CURRENT INHERITED BTS DEFECT ROOT; sparse/high team IDs make the intended random priority non-uniform.
Evidence:
- assignStartingPlots draws iRandOffset from MapRandNum(countCivTeamsAlive()).
- It then rotates through (i + iRandOffset) % MAX_CIV_TEAMS, which is the absolute team-ID domain rather than the compact list of alive teams.
Concrete example:
- If the two alive teams are IDs 5 and 20, countCivTeamsAlive()==2, so the only offsets are 0 and 1.
- Scanning absolute IDs from either 0 or 1 reaches team 5 before team 20; team 20 can never receive first-choice priority.
Reachability:
- Sparse/high IDs are supported in scenarios and current SAS code, and StartingPositionIteration deliberately falls back to this inherited assignment path for supported cases such as sufficiently large team games.
Repair direction:
- Build the actual alive-team list and randomize/rotate within that compact list; never mix a compact count with absolute team IDs.

F020 - provisional KI#341 - initScoreCalculation passes a civilization index to isCivilizationFreeTechs where the technology index is required
Status: NEW CURRENT INHERITED ADVCIV DEFECT ROOT; practical 2011 enum-refactor regression, current-data consequence proved.
Evidence:
- initScoreCalculation loops over technologies and then playable civilizations to determine whether eLoopTech is a possible civilization free technology.
- Current code calls GC.getInfo(eLoopCivilization).isCivilizationFreeTechs(eLoopCivilization), passing the civilization enum as the tech argument.
- BtS/Civ4CE lineage passes the technology-loop index as intended.
Current-data proof:
- Current playable civilization/free-tech data contains no accidental civ-index/free-tech-index coincidence that rescues this predicate; the erroneous branch is false for every playable civilization.
Consequence:
- On an Ancient start, m_iInitTech receives 0 from this free-start-tech normalization instead of counting the current five distinct possible civilization starting technologies (each currently worth one technology-score point).
- This defeats the routine's explicit intent to normalize the immediate-retirement score for free starting technologies. Later start eras mask much of the effect because earlier-era technologies are counted through the separate era branch.
Repair direction:
- Pass eLoopTech to isCivilizationFreeTechs.

F021 - provisional KI#342 - global-warming target selection can accept the last rejected water/peak plot after exhausting its retry loop
Status: NEW CURRENT INHERITED ADVCIV DEFECT ROOT; K-Mod control shows the lost exhaustion guard.
Evidence:
- getRandGWPlot tries up to 25 random plots for each pool candidate and accepts Ice or ordinary non-peak land.
- Unsuitable open water/peaks are simply retried. If all 25 random draws are non-null but unsuitable, pTestPlot still points to the final rejected plot when the loop exits.
- Current post-loop guard tests only pTestPlot == NULL, so the rejected plot proceeds into scoring/selection.
- eTerrain is declared outside the outer candidate-pool loop and can therefore also carry the previous candidate's terrain value into this exhausted candidate.
Lineage:
- K-Mod retained an explicit exhaustion test in addition to the null test.
- AdvCiv practical 1631 removed the loop-count guard on the theory that resetting pTestPlot made it redundant; resetting before a successful-but-unsuitable draw does not make it null after the final retry.
Consequence:
- On water/peak-heavy maps, a global-warming strike can be wasted on an unsuitable open-water/peak target, and stale eTerrain can mis-rank an exhausted candidate.
Repair direction:
- Track whether a suitable plot was actually found (or test retry exhaustion explicitly), and reset candidate-specific terrain state inside the outer pool iteration.

F022 - provisional KI#343 - multiple missing AP/UN voters receive staggered one-turn grace periods based on player iteration order
Status: NEW CURRENT INHERITED BTS MULTIPLAYER DEFECT ROOT; source contract explicit.
Evidence:
- doVoteResults comments that a player who has not voted should receive one more turn to submit a vote.
- On the first NO_PLAYER_VOTE it changes only that voter to NO_PLAYER_VOTE_CHECKED, sets bAllVoted=false and immediately breaks from the voter loop.
- On the next game turn, that first checked voter defaults to abstain, then the next still-missing voter receives the checked state; additional missing voters repeat the sequence.
Consequence:
- When several eligible voting members miss the same deadline, they do not receive the same one-turn grace period. Later players in iteration order can receive successively later deadlines.
Repair direction:
- In the first incomplete-vote pass, mark every currently missing eligible voter NO_PLAYER_VOTE_CHECKED, remember that voting is incomplete, and postpone processing until the next turn. Then all still-checked missing votes can default consistently.

F023 - provisional KI#344 - Lock Modified Assets can randomly generate an empty admin password and fail to lock WorldBuilder
Status: NEW CURRENT INHERITED BTS DEFECT ROOT; low-probability but direct option-contract failure.
Evidence:
- With GAMEOPTION_LOCK_MODS, CvGame::init generates seven arbitrary random char values from a range that includes zero, terminates the buffer, and supplies it as the admin password.
- If the first generated byte is NUL, the C string passed to setAdminPassword is empty.
- Current isDebugToolsAllowed(true) explicitly permits single-player WorldBuilder when the admin password is empty.
Consequence:
- A game started with Lock Modified Assets can therefore retain WorldBuilder/debug-tool access despite the option's stated purpose of assigning a random password to prevent it.
- Under the normal Windows/MSVC signed-char range (0..127), first-byte NUL is approximately a 1/128 event per locked game. A later NUL merely shortens the password; the first-byte case is the concrete contract failure.
Repair direction:
- Generate from a non-NUL printable alphabet/range or encode a random integer into a guaranteed nonempty textual password.

F024 - provisional KI#345 - victory scan clears the cumulative End Score flag on every victory/team test
Status: NEW CURRENT INHERITED BTS DEFECT ROOT; concrete Time-Victory tie behavior contradicts the outer scan's cumulative use.
Contract failure:
- CvGame::testVictory(VictoryTypes, TeamTypes, bool*) begins by assigning *pbEndScore=false on every call, then sets it true only for that individual victory if appropriate.
- The outer testVictory() passes the same bEndScore pointer through every team/victory pair, but later treats the final value as the cumulative question "does this game have an End Score victory?".
Current-data consequence:
- VICTORY_TIME has End Score enabled but is followed in XML iteration by non-End-Score Conquest, Domination, Cultural, Space and Diplomatic victories. Those later calls reset bEndScore back to false.
- Concrete trigger: when the Time Victory deadline is reached with the top teams tied, Time Victory correctly refuses to select a winner; the later non-End-Score calls then clear bEndScore, allowing the post-loop max-turn fallback to end the game anyway. The outer guard's intended cumulative meaning is therefore defeated.
Lineage:
- Local Civ4CE BtS 3.19 contains the same helper, per-call reset and outer reuse. The defect is therefore inherited from BtS itself rather than introduced by K-Mod; tracked K-Mod and AdvCiv retain it.
Repair direction:
- Preserve the helper's per-call output contract and OR each result into a separate cumulative flag in the outer caller, or change the helper to set-only semantics explicitly.

FALSE POSITIVES / CLEAN TAIL CHECKS FROM FILE 008
- Vote-source city coordinate leak candidate rejected: getVoteSourceCity(..., bDebug=true) still requires the observer to have revealed the city unless the game itself is actually in debug mode; the flag is not an unconditional reveal bypass.
- sortByStartingLocHandicap's all-human/all-AI zero-agent pass calls MapRandNum(0), but CvRandom explicitly defines zero as legal: it returns 0 without advancing RNG. No separate zero-range random defect.
- skipDuplicateNormalizationBonus appears able to divide by zero because the city-radius iterator includes the candidate plot, but all live callers invoke it while that candidate is bonus-free; the self plot therefore cannot pass the same-bonus test before the division.
- The two perAreaUnowned.find(...)->second dereferences are safe under the local construction contract: the map is populated for every current CvArea immediately before the consuming plot scan.
- randomBarbarianUnit's optional OR-resource/NO_TECH handling looked under-defensive, but current unit XML has no reachable unit configuration exercising the problematic path. Future/mod-mod robustness only.
- Global-warming ordinary probability/defence denominators and current XML values were checked; no second denominator/configuration root survived beside F021.
- clearSecretaryGeneral's apparently underinitialized target state does not escape through the negative processVote path; the relevant removal branch returns before target fields can be consumed.
- goodyHutEffectFactor can only reach its questionable denominator if the relevant GlobalDefine is retuned outside current supported/default values; record as tuning robustness debt, not a current KI.
- updateStartingPlotRange's denominator/range paths have current caller/data guards; no additional starting-distance arithmetic root established.
- Current-format CvGame serialization was mechanically paired at 88 corresponding read/write operations in the same order. Variable-length string vectors and conditional SPAH, Rise & Fall and event-trigger blocks are symmetric.
- Modern read() hard-coded option remap numbers were checked against the current enum: 17 New Random Seed, 18 Lock Mods, 19 Rise & Fall, 20 True Starts, 23 No Vassals and 27 No Espionage are all correct.
- Map-heaviness members are reset and serialized correctly. F015 is specifically a lifecycle/computation-order failure, not stale persistence.
- Cached CvGame gameplay defines were cross-checked against current runtime define mutation. The dynamically changed river-generation, camera/FOV and minimap values do not overlap these caches.
- Global warming's coordinate-less NO_PLAYER replay message is intentionally broadcast live to all major civilizations; unlike F016, its Timeline visibility matches its live audience.
- replaceCorporation iterator deletion, timer/active-player accesses, reviveActivePlayer, deal-iteration diagnostics and remaining FAssert(false) sites were traced to established iterator/UI/EXE contracts; no additional current defect survived.
- calculateSyncChecksum concerns map to already-known/fixed KI#326 lineage; no new synchronization root from this file.
- Constructor/reset/header/member ownership was checked: persistent enum maps/state have matching reset/read/write lifecycles, transient UI/cache state is reset rather than serialized, replay storage is cleared, and BarbarianWeightMap ownership/activity reset is coherent.
- Post-C008 commits touching CvGame.cpp were checked: practical 6216 only adjusts/comment-documents starting-distance work; practical 6221 is KI#325 past-city-name signature/locale work. Neither repairs or invalidates F015-F024.
- Final assertion/sentinel/container/iterator/enum/division scan found no additional live root. The remaining FAssert(false) sites are synchronization diagnostics or switch-default impossibilities under current enums.


FILE 009 - CvGameCoreDLL/CvPlot.cpp - 8,705 lines - REVIEWED / SEVEN NEW FINDINGS

RESULT
- CvPlot.cpp is closed after a changed-surface pass, an independent inherited whole-file pass, and final owner/team-sentinel, fixed-array, iterator/container, constructor/header, serialization, supported-caller and known-KI collision sweeps.
- Seven new current defect roots survive as F025-F031 / provisional KI#346-KI#352.
- Current CvPlot read/write serialization was mechanically revalidated at 60 corresponding operations in the same structural order.
- No F032 was established. Several genuine source defects remain recorded below as dormant/robustness debt because no supported current caller reaches the broken branch.

F025 - provisional KI#346 - dynamic feature passability changes leave PASSABLE_AREAS and plot-group connectivity stale
Status: NEW CURRENT DEFECT ROOT, inherited/AdvCiv topology interaction; supported global-warming trigger.
Evidence:
- AdvCiv's PASSABLE_AREAS behavior deliberately makes impassable shelf Ice part of water-area topology and performs an additional area calculation after Ice placement.
- CvPlot::setFeatureType() changes the feature, calls updateImpassable() and updateYield(), but does not recalculate/reassign CvArea topology and does not rebuild trade/resource plot-group connectivity.
- Ordinary current global warming can remove FEATURE_ICE through setFeatureType(NO_FEATURE).
- After the removal, the plot's direct passability is updated immediately while its old CvArea identity and existing plot groups can still describe the Ice barrier.
- isTradeNetwork() itself treats impassable feature state as relevant to connectivity, so the stale state is not merely diagnostic metadata.
Consequence:
- A melted channel can be traversable at plot level while naval/area-scoped AI and resource/trade-network logic still reason from the pre-melt disconnected topology.
- CvArea::canBeEntered, water-area unit/resource/city accounting and several naval/worker/assault decisions deliberately consume these area divisions.
Repair direction:
- Treat a feature change that changes effective passability as a topology invalidation and perform a safe deferred area/plot-group refresh after the mutation batch.
- Do not naively call wholesale recalculateAreas() inside every setFeatureType(); current area rebuild code has live-city bookkeeping caveats.
- If an incremental processArea(-1/+1) repair is considered, first fix or bypass the dormant processArea defects recorded below.

F026 - provisional KI#347 - lazy getFoundValue ignores a successful Python city-found-value override
Status: NEW CURRENT SUPPORTED-CONFIGURATION API DEFECT, inherited from BtS.
Evidence:
- CvPlot::getFoundValue() calls CvPythonCaller::AI_foundValue().
- If Python returns -1, the DLL computes and stores its own value in m_aiFoundValue.
- If Python returns a real override, the function never copies iValue into m_aiFoundValue; the cache remains -1.
- It then tests/returns the still--1 cache entry, fails to update the area's best-found value from the Python result, and repeats the callback on the next lazy read.
- Current SAS still registers/configures getCityFoundValue as a supported BUG/GameUtils callback; default is -1, but enabling the callback is an explicit supported configuration.
- Local Civ4CE BtS 3.19 source has the same lazy callback path and missing non--1 cache assignment; K-Mod and AdvCiv retain it through later wrappers/refactors.
Consequence:
- A valid Python override can be ignored specifically through lazy CvPlot::getFoundValue(), producing -1 instead of the supplied city-site value and leaving associated caches inconsistent.
Repair direction:
- In the non--1 branch, store iValue into m_aiFoundValue before area-best comparison and return.

F027 - provisional KI#348 - foreign friendly air units inherit the host city's enhanced air capacity
Status: NEW CURRENT GAMEPLAY DEFECT, inherited from BtS.
Evidence:
- CvPlot::airUnitSpaceAvailable(TeamTypes eTeam) correctly counts aircraft belonging to the requested eTeam.
- On a city plot it asks pCity->getAirUnitCapacity(getTeam()), where getTeam() is the plot/city owner's team, not eTeam.
- CvCity::getAirUnitCapacity(eTeam) is intentionally team-sensitive: the city team receives building-enhanced capacity, while a different friendly team receives only the base capacity.
- Current gameplay permits peaceful foreign-team air basing/rebasing, and ordinary callers query airUnitSpaceAvailable for the visiting team.
- Local Civ4CE BtS 3.19 source contains both the wrong plot-team argument and the team-sensitive city-capacity contract; K-Mod and AdvCiv retain them.
Consequence:
- Visiting allied/vassal/open-borders aircraft can be admitted using the host owner's Airport-enhanced capacity even though the underlying city API intentionally denies that expansion to foreign teams.
Repair direction:
- Pass the requested team through: pCity->getAirUnitCapacity(eTeam).

F028 - provisional KI#349 - CvPlot::debugStr returns a dangling c_str pointer
Status: NEW CURRENT DIAGNOSTIC/UNDEFINED-BEHAVIOR DEFECT, introduced by AdvCiv.
Evidence:
- debugStr() returns wchar const* and uses a static std::wostringstream.
- Every return expression is out.str().c_str().
- std::wostringstream::str() returns a temporary std::wstring; c_str() therefore points into a temporary destroyed at the end of the return expression.
- The static lifetime of the stream does not extend the lifetime of that temporary string.
- Current found-value/BBAI diagnostic paths consume debugStr when those logs are enabled.
Consequence:
- Diagnostic logging reads a dangling pointer and can produce stale/garbled text or, under undefined behavior, worse failures.
Repair direction:
- Prefer returning CvWString/std::wstring by value, or assign out.str() to storage whose lifetime survives the call before returning c_str().

F029 - provisional KI#350 - nuke effect reporting reveals the true owner of hidden-nationality units
Status: NEW CURRENT HIDDEN-INFORMATION DEFECT, inherited AdvCiv reporting behavior.
Evidence:
- nukeExplosion() records damaged/killed units in per-real-owner vectors and later formats observer messages with GET_PLAYER(eUnitOwner).getCivilizationAdjectiveKey().
- The observer gate checks whether each affected plot is visible, but does not apply the unit's observer-relative visual owner before naming the civilization.
- Current UNIT_PRIVATEER is hidden-nationality and always-hostile, is not nuke-immune, and getVisualOwner(observerTeam) intentionally conceals its real civilization under ordinary visibility.
- Such a unit can legitimately be damaged/killed by a nuclear blast.
Consequence:
- An observer who sees only an anonymous/Barbarian-looking Privateer can receive a nuke message naming its real civilization (for example, "Dutch unit destroyed"), revealing information the unit itself is designed to conceal.
Repair direction:
- Group/name affected units using observer-relative visual/combat ownership, or sanitize hidden-nationality entries rather than formatting them with the real owner's civilization adjective.
- Keep this separate from the Great-General identity leak: the feature, data path and repair surface are different.

F030 - provisional KI#351 - post-start river-edge edits leave several derived systems stale
Status: NEW CURRENT SUPPORTED-API DEFECT, inherited from BtS.
Evidence:
- The source immediately around fresh-water logic explicitly notes that changing river state requires updateIrrigated and CvCity::updateFreshWaterHealth.
- CvPlot::setNOfRiver() and setWOfRiver() can dynamically change that state, but neither performs those updates.
- The setters update river crossings, yields and river-edge area counts, and adjust plot-group bonus counts, but do not rebuild trade/resource plot-group connectivity even though river edges can create/remove network connections.
- Both setters remain publicly exposed through current CyPlot Python APIs; current SAS logging also explicitly supports rare post-start WorldBuilder/script/event river-edge edits.
- Initial map generation is not the problematic case because later initialization rebuilds derived state; the defect concerns post-start mutation.
Consequence:
- A runtime river edit can leave irrigation propagation/yields, city fresh-water health and river-based resource/trade connectivity inconsistent with the visible river.
Repair direction:
- When an edge actually changes after initialization, invalidate/recompute irrigation around the affected edge, update fresh-water health for affected cities and refresh plot-group connectivity for relevant players/teams.

F031 - provisional KI#352 - doCultureDecay reads one element past its city-radius array for Barbarian culture
Status: NEW CURRENT MEMORY-SAFETY/LOGIC DEFECT, inherited AdvCiv practical 1289.
Evidence:
- doCultureDecay() allocates bool abInRadius[MAX_CIV_PLAYERS].
- In the 48-civ DLL, valid array indices are civilization-player slots 0..47; BARBARIAN_PLAYER is 48 and MAX_PLAYERS is 49.
- The later FOR_EACH_ENUM(Player) loop spans the full PlayerTypes domain through MAX_PLAYERS and reads abInRadius[eLoopPlayer].
- Therefore a positive Barbarian-culture entry reaches abInRadius[48], one element past the stack array.
- Reachability is normal current gameplay, not a malformed save: Barbarian cities/adjacent plots receive ordinary plot culture, legal Barbarian/civilization city spacing permits overlapping/contested radius plots, and the decay branch can process such a plot.
- Historical intent around practical 1289 explicitly discussed excluding Barbarian tiles from the extra city-radius decay, so the mismatch is especially likely to be an array-domain implementation error.
Consequence:
- Release builds have undefined behavior. The likely visible symptom is nondeterministic application of accelerated culture decay to Barbarian culture on contested plots; the one-past-end read is also a memory-safety defect in its own right.
Repair direction:
- Either size the state to the full PlayerTypes domain (MAX_PLAYERS / PlayerMap<bool>) or explicitly skip BARBARIAN_PLAYER before indexing if the historical Barbarian exemption remains intended.

DORMANT / ROBUSTNESS DEFECTS AND KILLED CANDIDATES FROM FILE 009
- CvPlot::processArea contains two genuine source mistakes in a currently dormant negative/live-city path:
  - `pWaterArea->getCitiesPerPlayer(getOwner()) > 0, true` uses the comma operator and is always true. The intended expression is evidently `pWaterArea->getCitiesPerPlayer(getOwner(), true) > 0`.
  - The refactored building-area loop skips `iTotalChange <= 0`, suppressing removal effects; historical behavior indicates that only zero should be skipped.
  Current supported area-changing paths do not reach those malformed negative branches with a living city still needing that bookkeeping. Do not promote them as present gameplay roots, but fix/avoid them before using incremental processArea(-1) as part of F025.
- exclusiveRadius(ePlayer) can overwrite an inner-ring result (1) with a later same-owner outer-ring result (2) when city radii overlap. Its only logic consumer is currently dead/commented, so this is a dormant helper bug rather than a current KI.
- Deprecated isCityExternal(bool, TeamTypes) compares `eForTeam == NULL`; NO_TEAM is -1, so its old parameterized ActsAsCity path would dereference GET_TEAM(NO_TEAM). Current supported callers use the ordinary no-parameter city check and the function/header explicitly treat the parameterized path as deprecated/EXE compatibility, so this remains robustness debt.
- getNonObsoleteBonusType has a header default of NO_TEAM while its implementation asserts and dereferences a real team. No current zero-argument C++ caller was found and the Python wrapper requires the team argument; no live root established.
- changeBuildProgress and shouldDisplayBridge also assume valid player arguments, but all supported engine/Python call contracts found supply a real player; no sentinel bug was promoted from hypothetical invalid external input.
- The setFeatureType fresh-water facet is incomplete in general, but current dynamically changing feature data does not provide a supported post-start AddsFreshWater creation/removal path; it remains future/mod-mod robustness separate from F025.
- Hidden-resource random-event eligibility is intentional for the one matching current event ("A Man Named Jed", Oil), whose design is to discover Oil; no information-leak root.
- Culture-flip civilization names follow AdvCiv's established hasSeen/revealed-owner contract and do not require hasMet; no leak established.
- The hidden-nationality visibility contact path acts on visual owner and does not identify the concealed real owner; no defect there.
- getYieldWithBuild unowned-owner concern was killed by caller contracts: current callers operate on WorkablePlotIter/best-build plots with an owned/same-owner working city.
- canHavePotentialIrrigation(...NO_TEAM...) safely propagates the sentinel to code that handles it; no invalid team dereference.
- getPlotGroup(ePlayer) requires a real player, and current callers establish that contract.
- Sight-recursion pointer lifetime/NULL concern was killed: reaching the recursive branch establishes the candidate plot.
- Static GlobalDefine caches were cross-checked against current runtime define mutation; no cached CvPlot gameplay value overlaps the currently mutable river-generation/UI/camera/minimap define set.
- Fixed-array/domain scan found no second MAX_CIV_PLAYERS/MAX_CIV_TEAMS local-array mismatch beyond F031.
- Iterator erase patterns, constructor/member initialization, enum/container bounds and final division/sentinel scans produced no additional supported current root.

- Observations in later files are not counted until that file reaches its turn. File 010 is now active.

CURRENT FILE-010 RESULT SO FAR
