- Provisional KI aliases: KI#353-KI#363.
- The file remains OPEN; these findings are checkpointed so that chat/thread/network interruption cannot lose them.
- Most roots are inherited BBAI/K-Mod/AdvCiv survivors. The current SAS-specific semantic surface checked so far (tech-locus KI#312 repair, victory-denial peace hook, Barbarian AreaAI option, war diagnostics/logging, serialization cleanup) has not produced another new SAS regression.

F032 - provisional KI#353 - AI_diploVoteCounterCandidate compares vote count with team ID
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT AI LOGIC DEFECT.
Evidence:
- AI_diploVoteCounterCandidate() tracks first/second vote totals, but a branch compares `iFirstMostVotes` (vote count) with `getID()` (TeamTypes numeric ID).
- Those values are unrelated domains.
- Concrete normal case: our team ID 0 has 100 votes, rival team 1 has 60, third team 2 has 20. When team 1 becomes the second-place candidate, the test `100 == 0` fails and the helper can return NO_TEAM rather than team 1 as the obvious counter-candidate.
- The result feeds diplomacy-victory strategy progress and UWAI military-victory utility.
Ancestry:
- AdvCiv practical 1832 introduced the comparison in this form.
- Later practicals 2579 and 2633 explicitly fixed neighboring diplomatic-victory vote calculations but left this domain mismatch intact.
Repair direction:
- Compare the proper candidate/team state rather than a vote total with a team ID; reconstruct the original intended first/second candidate logic explicitly.

F033 - provisional KI#354 - AI_haveSeenCities(eTeam) iterates our own team instead of target team
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT / CONFIGURATION-REACHABLE WAR-PLANNING DEFECT.
Evidence:
- Helper contract is to determine whether cities belonging to `eTeam` have been seen/deduced.
- Current implementation uses `MemberIter(getID())`; the target `eTeam` argument is not used to select the city owners.
- K-Mod's original explicit loop correctly filtered players by `getTeam() == eTeam`.
- AdvCiv practical 1842 ("Rename WPAI to UWAI; revise loops in CvTeamAI") replaced the target population with the own-team iterator.
- A sibling practical-1842 own-team/target-team substitution in AI_isLandTarget() was later repaired by practical 1941 (`MemberIter(getID())` -> `MemberIter(eTarget)`), strongly corroborating this refactor class.
Reachability:
- Default UWAI masks the path for ordinary major-civ war selection.
- It is live through supported Aggressive AI / K-Mod behavior, `USE_KMOD_AI_NONAGGRESSIVE`, `DISABLE_UWAI`, and related background-UWAI modes.
Consequence:
- Once an AI has seen/deduced one of its own cities, total/limited/dogpile gates can act as though a proposed enemy city has been seen even when no target city is known.
Repair direction:
- Iterate `MemberIter(eTeam)` (or the exact equivalent target-team population) and preserve the original visibility/deduction semantics.

F034 - provisional KI#355 - AI_getRivalAirPower computes the rival-average denominator once per air class
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT AI VALUATION DEFECT, inherited K-Mod.
Evidence:
- Routine promises enemy air power plus average rival air power.
- During K-Mod practical 199's rewrite from unit types to unit classes, `iTeamCount++` was placed inside the outer air-unit-class loop.
- Current SAS has five qualifying default air classes (Fighter, Jet Fighter, Bomber, Stealth Bomber, Guided Missile).
- With two eligible rival teams, the denominator can therefore become about 10 instead of 2.
- AdvCiv's later "avoid war / friendly rival" filtering can further aggravate the same root: skipped teams contribute no numerator while the denominator population can still include them.
Consequence:
- The average-rival-air component is systematically understated, approximately 5x in the straightforward current-data case.
- This feeds interceptor unit valuation, defensive-air production and air-defense building valuation.
Repair direction:
- Count eligible rival teams once per rival, with exactly the same population used by the numerator; accumulate unit-class air power inside that rival loop (or track unique included teams separately).

F035 - provisional KI#356 - AI_doWar subtracts total Dagger teammates from independent teammate populations
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT MULTI-MEMBER-TEAM COUNTING DEFECT, inherited K-Mod/BBAI logic.
Evidence:
- Financial-trouble gate intends to reason about financially troubled NON-DAGGER teammates, but computes `iFinancialTroubleCount - iDaggerCount`.
- Dagger strategy and financial trouble are independent sets; a healthy Dagger member can cancel a different member's genuine financial trouble.
- The same invalid set-subtraction pattern is used for `GET_BETTER_UNITS`: `iGetBetterUnitsCount - iDaggerCount`, even though Dagger and GET_BETTER_UNITS are independently computed strategies and can coexist arbitrarily.
- Nearby comment says "half the non-Dagger teammates", but the threshold uses `getNumMembers()/2`, i.e. half of all teammates.
Concrete case:
- Two-member team: member A is healthy + Dagger; member B is financially troubled + non-Dagger. Intended troubled-non-Dagger count is 1/1; subtraction yields 1 - 1 = 0 and removes the intended opposition.
Repair direction:
- Count the actual intersections directly (`financialTrouble && !Dagger`, `GET_BETTER_UNITS && !Dagger`) and compare against the actual non-Dagger population.

F036 - provisional KI#357 - AI_warCommitmentCost passes accumulated output cost as raw war weariness
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT REFACTOR REGRESSION.
Evidence:
- War-weariness section computes a raw `iTotalWW`.
- Current code calls `getModifiedWarWearinessPercentAnger(iWWCost)`, where `iWWCost` is the accumulating result/cost output, not raw war weariness.
- K-Mod practical 887 explicitly fixed this same routine to pass `iTotalWw`.
- AdvCiv practical 1842 changed the argument to `iWWCost` during iterator/refactor cleanup.
Consequence:
- First team member begins with `iWWCost == 0`, so its anger estimate can ignore nonzero actual war weariness.
- Later members are fed a feedback value measured in AI-cost units rather than war-weariness units.
- Commitment penalty can become detached from the actual war weariness that triggered the branch.
Repair direction:
- Restore the intended raw war-weariness argument (`iTotalWW` / exact current equivalent); keep the accumulated cost only as the output accumulator.

F037 - provisional KI#358 - AI_preDeclareWar changes "war despite tribute" memory audience to the wrong knowledge set
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT PRACTICAL-1842 ITERATOR REGRESSION.
Evidence:
- Pre-refactor AdvCiv/K-Mod logic awarded the memory to AI players whose team had met the tribute-paying victim, subject to the master/vassal-locus exclusion.
- Practical 1842 replaced this with an iterator population based on knowledge/potential-enemy relationship to the attacker.
- These populations are not equivalent.
- The original advc.130o logic and XML frame this memory as knowledge that the VICTIM had paid tribute before being attacked.
Consequence:
- An AI that knows the attacker but has never met the tribute-paying victim can receive the memory.
- An AI that knows the victim but has not met the attacker can be omitted.
Repair direction:
- Restore the victim-contact population (plus intended master/vassal exclusions) explicitly; do not substitute the attacker's potential-enemy iterator.

F038 - provisional KI#359 - AI_acceptSurrender reverses the war-duration side of "might capitulate to another rival"
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT PRACTICAL-1842 COMPARATOR/CONTROL REGRESSION.
Evidence:
- Before the refactor, code did:
  `if (atWarCounter < threshold) continue;`
  then tested overwhelming rival war success.
- Therefore the rival had to have been at war LONG ENOUGH before being treated as a plausible alternative capitulation master.
- Practical 1842 collapsed the logic to a positive condition using:
  `atWarCounter < threshold && overwhelmingWarSuccess`
- This reverses the duration side.
Consequence:
- `bMightCapToOther` can become true during an EARLY war, then cease becoming true once the exact duration threshold intended to make capitulation plausible has been reached.
- This feeds several later decisions about whether to accept surrender before another enemy does.
Repair direction:
- Restore the `>= threshold` meaning (or retain the original `continue if < threshold` structure for clarity).

F039 - provisional KI#360 - AI_knownTechValModifier includes capitulated teams only in the denominator
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT PRACTICAL-1842 POPULATION MISMATCH.
Evidence:
- Before practical 1842, capitulated teams were excluded from BOTH `iCivsMet` and `iTechCivs`.
- After the refactor, `iCivsMet++` became unconditional while the numerator still excludes capitulated teams.
Consequence:
- Every known capitulated team increases the denominator but can never increase the tech-owning numerator.
- Technologies therefore look artificially rarer / more monopolized and more valuable.
- Modifier feeds ordinary AI technology-trade valuation and Great-Person bulb/discover valuation.
Repair direction:
- Use the same eligible civilization/team population for numerator and denominator, matching the pre-1842 logic.

F040 - provisional KI#361 - AI_getEnemyPowerPercent double-counts shared Defensive Pact allies
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT INHERITED COALITION-POWER DEFECT.
Evidence:
- AI can hold several chosen-war plans simultaneously.
- Each prospective target's `getDefensivePower()` independently includes its Defensive Pact allies.
- If targets B and C both share pact ally D, summing target defensive power can effectively produce `(B + D) + (C + D)`.
- AdvCiv practical 1366 explicitly noticed this and left a FIXME; no later repair found.
Consequence:
- Shared allies can be counted multiple times in the "enemy power" total.
- AI_getEnemyPowerPercent feeds hard war thresholds, military-need logic, production pressure and newer SAS danger-sensitive weighting.
Repair direction:
- Build the union of hostile/committed defending teams once and sum each team exactly once, rather than summing overlapping per-target coalition totals.

F041 - provisional KI#362 - AI_randomCounterChange callers calculate accumulated relationship ceilings but cap only the 0-2 single-turn roll
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT HELPER/CALLER CONTRACT DEFECT; wording corrected before durable C011.
Evidence:
- CvTeamAI Open Borders caller computes a long-term `iMax = 2 * OpenBordersAttitudeDivisor + 10`.
- CvPlayerAI Bonus Trade caller similarly computes a long-term cap from the Bonus Trade attitude divisor/change limit.
- Both pass this as `AI_randomCounterChange(iUpperCap, rProb)`.
- The helper applies `iUpperCap` only to the per-turn binomial increment, whose natural maximum is already 2.
- Therefore those meaningful long-term caps are effectively no-ops.
Important correction:
- Normal Open Borders deal start/end resets the OB counter to zero; the earlier working-note claim that excess OB state survives cancellation and decays afterward was WRONG and must not be repeated in C011.
Current live consequence:
- Bonus Trade does provide the strong supported consequence.
- Current default divisor 50 and +2 limit imply a calculated ceiling around 125.
- Sustained valuable imports can push the counter above that intended ceiling.
- When imports disappear, Bonus Trade counter decays rather than being immediately reset; excess hidden state can keep the capped +2 resource-trade attitude for extra turns.
Repair direction:
- Enforce a ceiling on the ACCUMULATED counter, e.g. cap the result after increment or constrain the increment to `max(0, ceiling - currentCounter)`.
- Keep Open Borders recorded as the same ineffective-cap caller contract without overstating its current post-cancellation impact.

F042 - provisional KI#363 - freeing a vassal fails to restore cached war-plan counts against the newly independent team
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT CACHED-STATE LIFECYCLE DEFECT.
Evidence:
- War-plan-count optimization excludes vassal targets from `m_aiWarPlanCounts`.
- `CvTeam::setVassal(..., false, ...)` explicitly tries to re-add existing plans targeting the team being freed:
  conceptually `AI_updateWarPlanCounts(target, NO_WARPLAN, existingPlan)`.
- It performs that restoration BEFORE clearing the target's current vassal/master status.
- `AI_updateWarPlanCounts()` sees `target.isAVassal()` and immediately returns, making the intended restoration a no-op.
Reachable lifecycle:
- While V is a vassal, enemy E can be dragged into war with V and hold `WARPLAN_ATTACKED_RECENT`; the plan is intentionally excluded from E's cache during vassalage.
- If V is freed while the war continues, the now-countable existing plan is not restored.
- When the plan later transitions `ATTACKED_RECENT -> ATTACKED`, normal cache transition code can decrement the old type that was never incremented, producing a negative per-plan count while incrementing the new type.
Consequence:
- `AI_isAnyWarPlan()` and per-plan cached strategy queries can temporarily disagree with live plans.
- Negative cached counts can persist during the live session until reconstruction/repair.
Ancestry:
- AdvCiv practical 1677 introduced the war-plan-count optimization and this pre-status-change setVassal adjustment together.
- Later storage/serialization descendants did not correct the ordering.
Repair direction:
- Restore/reconstruct target war-plan counts only AFTER the team is no longer considered a vassal, or make the transition helper explicitly operate on the intended future status rather than consulting stale current state.

PRACTICAL-1842 FOCUSED REFRACTOR AUDIT - CLOSED AS A SUBPASS
- Practical 1842 generated five surviving current regressions in this file: F033, F036, F037, F038, F039.
- Its other iterator substitutions were expanded back to their old predicates and checked.
- AI_calculateAreaAIType's shift to KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF does not lose a reachable pre-contact chosen-war target: normal `canEventuallyDeclareWar()` war-plan creation requires contact, including the relevant Always War path.
- Peace, worst-enemy, forgiveness, counter and timeout iterator conversions examined are population-equivalent or documented semantic changes.
- `AI_isLandTarget()` provides sibling evidence: practical 1842 also changed its target-player loop to our own team, and practical 1941 explicitly repaired that one while F033's analogous error remained.

- AI_mayAttack TeamTypes overload assumes a real defender team; normal plot/unit wrappers establish ownership/team first, and Always Hostile combat-owner logic explicitly does not support NO_TEAM.

- Known `AI_getEnemyPowerPercent()` Defensive Pact FIXME is F040 itself, not a second root.
- `nextIndex()` denominator scan so far: nearly all averages use explicit max(1,...) or have nonempty team-member contracts; the exceptional wrong-population averages already map to F034/F039.
- Public sentinel/default/assert/fixed-array sweep has not yet produced F043.

- AI_doCounter increments AtPeaceCounter even before teams have met.
- This initially looked as though relationship history might begin too early.
- AdvCiv's attitude logic explicitly accounts for this inherited counter semantic by using:
  `min(AI_getAtPeaceCounter(...), AI_getHasMetCounter(...))`
  when computing the peace-attitude contribution.
- Other consumers checked either require an already-known/previous-war rival or intentionally use elapsed non-war time.
- Therefore AtPeace-before-contact is intentional inherited state semantics, not a bug.

NEW CURRENT ROOT AFTER WIP2

F043 - provisional KI#364 - practical 1842 stopped clearing old arrogant-demand memories toward the declaring team's own master/vassal locus
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT PRACTICAL-1842 POPULATION REGRESSION.

Original contract / ancestry:
- Practical 1260 (`ccf5d7b95...`) explicitly states in its log:
  "AI civs now forget all 'arrogant demands' when they declare any war. [advc.130o]"
- Pre-1842 declare-war logic iterated all living major players and, for a primary declaration, cleared each declaring member's `MEMORY_MADE_DEMAND` toward every such player.
- Practical 1842 replaced the broad player loop with:
  `PlayerIter<MAJOR_CIV, KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF>(getID())`
- `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF` requires contact and excludes players in the declaring team's same-master locus (master, vassals, sibling vassals).
- This silently narrowed a deliberately all-player memory reset.

Why excluded memories are not already guaranteed gone:
- `CvTeam::setVassal()` does clear `MEMORY_MADE_DEMAND` when a team becomes a vassal, but it clears the NEW VASSAL'S OWN memories toward others.
- It does not symmetrically clear a master's existing `MEMORY_MADE_DEMAND` about that new vassal.

Concrete supported sequence:
1. AI A accepts an arrogant demand from human H, so A stores `MEMORY_MADE_DEMAND(H)`.
2. H later becomes A's VOLUNTARY vassal.
3. A later declares an unrelated war on C.
4. The practical-1260 contract says A should now forget all old arrogant-demand memories.
5. Current practical-1842-era loop excludes H because H is in A's master/vassal locus.
6. A therefore retains the old negative demand memory.

Why the voluntary-vassal example is useful:
- Capitulation has special forced-attitude behavior that could temporarily mask some visible consequences.
- Voluntary vassalage gives the cleanest demonstration that this remains meaningful AI state.

Current consequence:
- `MEMORY_MADE_DEMAND` has real current negative attitude/decay values in leader data.
- UWAI / demand-war reasoning also consumes this memory count.
- The stale memory therefore remains behaviorally meaningful; it is not dead bookkeeping.

Collision:
- No existing reviewed KI / Album root with the same all-demand-reset population regression was found.

Repair direction:
- Separate the deliberately all-major-player `MEMORY_MADE_DEMAND` reset from the narrower declaration-observer loop.
- Do not rely on `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF` for the all-player forget operation.

F044 - provisional KI#365 - practical 1842 suppresses valid war-on-friend memories for observers inside the aggressor's master/vassal locus
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT PRACTICAL-1842 OBSERVER-POPULATION REGRESSION.

Current structure:
- `AI_preDeclareWar()` now loops over:
  `PlayerIter<MAJOR_CIV, KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF>(getID())`
  before asking:
  `kPlayer.AI_disapprovesOfDoW(getID(), eTarget)`
  and applying `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR_ON_FRIEND`.
- `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF` excludes players sharing the aggressor's current master locus.

Pre-1842 semantics:
- The old declare-war loop considered all living major third-party players.
- For the war-on-friend memory, it excluded the aggressor's OWN TEAM.
- It did NOT generally exclude observers merely because they shared the aggressor's master/vassal locus.
- It did reject cases where the VICTIM shared the observer's master locus, which is a different relationship.

Current helper confirms the broader intended semantic population:
- `CvPlayerAI::AI_disapprovesOfDoW(eAggressor, eVictim)` rejects:
  - self/aggressor/victim invalid cases,
  - capitulated victim,
  - observer not meeting victim,
  - observer already at war with victim,
  - victim sharing the observer's master locus,
  - and other specific partner/memory exceptions.
- It does NOT reject an observer merely for sharing a master with the AGGRESSOR.
- It deliberately does not require the observer to have met the aggressor, matching inherited BtS behavior.

Why the DoW popup's narrower audience does not make the memory change intentional:
- Practical 1416's declaration-warning popup excludes same-master observers for presentation.
- While a vassal relation is active, the vassal/master forced-attitude logic can make listing that observer as a currently disapproving "third party" misleading.
- That UI choice does not erase the underlying historical memory semantics.
- `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR_ON_FRIEND` used to be stored and can matter later if the vassal relationship ends.

Concrete supported lifecycle:
1. AI B is a voluntary vassal / same-master-locus member of aggressor A.
2. B knows and likes outside civilization C.
3. A declares war on C.
4. Pre-1842 code could record `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR_ON_FRIEND` in B about A.
5. Practical 1842's `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF(A)` prefilter excludes B before `AI_disapprovesOfDoW` is even asked.
6. The memory is therefore never created.
7. If B later becomes independent, `CvTeam::setVassal(...false)` does not clear this memory type; under the old behavior it could then contribute normally to B's attitude / war utility toward A.

Current consequence:
- Current leader data assigns real negative attitude weights to `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR_ON_FRIEND` (often -100, stronger for some leaders) with decay.
- UWAI / WarUtility-related logic reads this memory as well.
- Thus suppressing it can alter post-vassalization diplomacy and strategic memory.

Collision:
- No same-root current KI / Album finding found.

Repair direction:
- Do not use the aggressor-potential-enemy population as the audience for war-on-friend memory.
- Restore all living major third-party observers and let `AI_disapprovesOfDoW` perform its intended semantic filtering, or build an iterator predicate exactly equivalent to that helper's requirements.
- Keep F044 separate from:
  F037 = tribute-memory observer visibility population,
  F043 = all-player arrogant-demand reset population.
  They originate from one overly broad loop replacement but break distinct contracts and have distinct consequences.

RENEWED PRACTICAL-1842 AUDIT - IMPORTANT CORRECTION

F045 - provisional KI#366 - incumbent worst enemy can remain inside the same master/vassal locus even though such teams are excluded from all new candidates
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT WORST-ENEMY ELIGIBILITY / LIFECYCLE DEFECT.

Current structure:
- `AI_updateWorstEnemy()` treats the incumbent and replacement candidates differently.
- Existing `m_eWorstEnemy` is evaluated directly through `AI_enmityValue(m_eWorstEnemy)`.
- New/replacement candidates are searched through:
  `TeamIter<MAJOR_CIV, KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF>(getID())`
- `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF` excludes teams sharing the same current master/vassal locus.
- `AI_enmityValue()` itself does not equivalently reject a voluntary vassal/master-locus incumbent.

History-dependent inconsistency:
1. Team A legitimately has team B as worst enemy while both are independent.
2. B later becomes A's VOLUNTARY vassal (or otherwise enters A's same-master locus).
3. Vassalization triggers broad attitude / worst-enemy refreshes.
4. `AI_updateWorstEnemy()` evaluates incumbent B directly, outside the candidate iterator's master-locus exclusion.
5. If B's retained enmity remains large enough, B can stay `m_eWorstEnemy`.
6. Starting from scratch in the exact same post-vassal state, B could never be chosen as a new worst enemy because the normal candidate population excludes it.

Concrete gameplay consequence:
- `AI_techTrade()` has an unconditional permission when WE are the recipient's vassal, but not the reverse master->vassal direction.
- It subsequently checks whether `AI_getWorstEnemy() == eToTeam` and may return `DENIAL_WORST_ENEMY`.
- Therefore an AI master can potentially refuse technology trade to its own voluntary vassal because that vassal remains a grandfathered stale worst enemy.
- Other worst-enemy consumers can inherit the same impossible-by-fresh-selection state.

Relation to existing KI#208:
- KI#208 is a different root: the previously repaired sticky-worst-enemy flag/order problem where later enemy-trade-memory processing could overwrite the need to recalculate worst enemy.
- F045 is candidate-eligibility asymmetry after a master/vassal relationship transition:
  incumbent evaluation bypasses the population restriction imposed on all replacements.
- Keep F045 separate from KI#208.

Ancestry:
- Practical 1842 moved the replacement scan to `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF` during the AgentIterator refactor.
- The incumbent remained evaluated outside that iterator population.
- No descendant repair for this incumbent/candidate asymmetry found so far.
- Later AdvCiv work continues to treat master/vassal status as relevant to worst-enemy semantics, reinforcing that same-locus teams should not normally be eligible.

Repair direction:
- Apply the same eligibility predicate to the incumbent before retaining it.
- If current `m_eWorstEnemy` is no longer a valid `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF` candidate (or exact intended equivalent), discard/recalculate it rather than grandfathering it.
- Prefer centralizing candidate eligibility so incumbent and replacement paths cannot diverge again.

F037 / provisional KI#358 - WORDING BROADENING / CORRECTION
- Root remains the practical-1842 replacement of the original tribute-memory observer population with the broad outer:
  `PlayerIter<MAJOR_CIV, KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF>(getID())`
  keyed to the AGGRESSOR.
- Pre-1842 logic for the "war despite received tribute" memory explicitly required the OBSERVER to have met the tribute-paying VICTIM, plus its separate master/vassal exclusions.
- `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF(aggressor)` instead:
  - requires observer contact with the AGGRESSOR,
  - excludes observer if in aggressor's same-master locus,
  - does NOT itself guarantee observer contact with the tribute-paying victim.
- Therefore the refactor can both:
  1. suppress a formerly valid observer who knows the victim but has not met the aggressor, and
  2. admit an observer who knows the aggressor but not the victim, subject to the remaining inner checks.
- Keep this as F037: one observer-population regression in the tribute-memory contract.
- Repair should reconstruct the ORIGINAL victim-knowledge population explicitly rather than reuse the aggressor-potential-enemy iterator.

F044 / provisional KI#365 - WORDING BROADENING
- Root remains the practical-1842 outer `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF(aggressor)` filter applied before `AI_disapprovesOfDoW`.
- Earlier WIP wording emphasized observers in the aggressor's master/vassal locus.
- Current helper trace proves the regression is broader:
  `CvPlayerAI::AI_disapprovesOfDoW(eAggressor,eVictim)` deliberately does NOT require the observer to have met the aggressor:
  `//!kOurTeam.isHasMet(eAggressor) // Not checked by BtS either`
- The helper DOES require the observer to know the victim and applies the actual victim/friend/master-locus semantic filters.
- Pre-1842 all-major-player iteration therefore allowed an outside observer who knows/likes the victim but has not yet met the aggressor to store `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR_ON_FRIEND`.
- Practical 1842 silently adds an aggressor-contact requirement and same-aggressor-locus exclusion before the helper gets a chance to decide.
- This can suppress historical diplomatic memory that was intended to become meaningful later once contact/status changes.
- Keep same-master-locus and no-aggressor-contact examples under the single F044 observer-population root.
- Repair should restore the broad third-party observer population and let `AI_disapprovesOfDoW` enforce its own semantic eligibility.

F046 - provisional KI#367 - practical 1842 can suppress direct victim `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR` on cascading declarations before first contact
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT PRACTICAL-1842 CONTACT/ORDERING REGRESSION.

Current declaration ordering:
- `CvTeam::declareWar(eTarget,...)` calls:
  1. `AI().AI_preDeclareWar(eTarget,...)`
  2. sets both teams' at-war flags
  3. performs other immediate war-state work
  4. only later calls `meet(eTarget, false)`
  5. later triggers Defensive Pact and vassal-alignment cascading wars.
- Thus AI_preDeclareWar executes with the PRE-WAR contact graph.

Why primary wars usually mask the defect:
- Normal voluntary war-plan / `canEventuallyDeclareWar()` logic requires `isHasMet(eTarget)`.
- Therefore the primary aggressor/victim pair is normally already known and passes `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF`.

Why cascading wars are different:
- Defensive Pact and vassal-alignment reactions queue new war declarations between teams that need not already have met each other.
- `triggerDefensivePacts` can queue e.g. third-party defender C -> original attacker A with `bPrimary=false`.
- `triggerWars` drains the queue by directly invoking `GET_TEAM(attacker).declareWar(defender,...)`; there is no separate pre-contact meeting before AI_preDeclareWar.
- The nested declaration itself only establishes contact later through `meet`.

Concrete supported example:
1. A declares war on B.
2. B has a Defensive Pact with C.
3. C has never met A.
4. The pact reaction queues C declaring war on A.
5. C's `declareWar(A)` enters `AI_preDeclareWar(A,...)` before C/A meet.
6. Practical-1842 outer iterator:
   `PlayerIter<MAJOR_CIV, KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF>(C)`
   excludes A because A has not met C yet.
7. Therefore the inner direct-victim branch:
   `if (kPlayer.getTeam() == eTarget) ... MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR ...`
   never sees A's members.
8. The declaration then establishes C/A contact, but no later code replays the missed victim memory.

Pre-1842 control:
- The original all-living-major-player loop did not require prior aggressor/victim contact before reaching the direct-victim `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR` branch.
- Thus the victim correctly remembered that the newly encountered team had declared war on it.

Current consequence:
- `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR` has current leader-memory XML values and real attitude/decay behavior.
- UWAI / WarUtility and UWAIAgent logic also read this memory family.
- Missing it on secondary/cascading declarations can therefore alter post-war attitude and strategic memory.

Scope:
- Distinct from F044:
  - F044 = third-party `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR_ON_FRIEND` observer audience.
  - F046 = the DIRECT VICTIM's own `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR`.
- Distinct from F037:
  - F037 = war-despite-tribute observer population.
- Distinct from F043:
  - F043 = all-player arrogant-demand reset narrowed by the same broad iterator.

Ancestry:
- Practical 1842 replaced the old broad per-player declaration-memory loop with `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF(getID())`.
- The outer filter now imposes a contact requirement before the declaration's own later `meet()` call can establish that contact.
- No descendant repair found so far.

Repair direction:
- Do not prefilter direct victim memory through an aggressor-contact iterator.
- The direct target/victim branch should explicitly process the target team's members regardless of prior contact when a valid declaration occurs.
- More generally, split AI_preDeclareWar's multiple distinct memory contracts into separately correct populations rather than driving all of them through one broad iterator.

OPEN UNNUMBERED CANDIDATE - possible F047
Title candidate:
- Legacy AI may store a chosen/preparing war plan on a VOLUNTARY VASSAL team while query/readiness APIs redirect the same target to its MASTER team.

Why it looks suspicious:
- Legacy `AI_doWar()` explicitly considers voluntary vassals as war targets.
- Target selection stores:
  `eBestTarget = eTarget` (the vassal team itself)
  and later calls:
  `AI_setWarPlan(eBestTarget, WARPLAN_PREPARING_TOTAL/LIMITED/DOGPILE...)`.
- `AI_isOkayVassalTarget(eTeam)` exists specifically to permit some voluntary-vassal targets while rejecting capitulated/inappropriate ones.
- There is no obvious target->master normalization at the selection call site.

But multiple query APIs redirect vassal targets to their master:
- `AI_isChosenWar(eIndex)` reads:
  `AI_getWarPlan(GET_TEAM(eIndex).getMasterTeam())`.
- `AI_isSneakAttackPreparing(eIndex)` likewise queries the target's master plan.
- `AI_mayAttack(eDefender)` gets defender master and requires an imminent plan against that master.
- `AI_updateWarPlanCounts()` intentionally ignores war plans whose target is currently a vassal.
- `AI_isAnyWarPlan()` and per-plan cache queries therefore do not count a vassal-stored plan.

Potential mismatch:
- Write key = vassal team.
- Several readiness/query/cache keys = master team.
- If no sibling/master plan is created elsewhere, legacy AI can select and store a vassal-target plan that later readiness/attack logic cannot observe through the normalized query APIs.

Reachability:
- Full UWAI returns before legacy AI_doWar selection.
- Legacy path remains reachable in supported configurations when UWAI is disabled / K-Mod-style or related background modes.
- Must still prove a complete supported behavioral failure before promotion.

Important reasons NOT to promote yet:
- `AI_doWar()`'s first live-plan loop reads raw `AI_getWarPlan(eLoopTeam)`, so the vassal-stored plan is not completely invisible.
- It may advance PREPARING -> active on the vassal team.
- Declaring war on the vassal can drag its master into war by game rules, so storing the raw plan on the vassal might be partially intentional.
- Need determine whether:
  1. a sibling plan is also created on the master,
  2. the vassal plan is later normalized,
  3. unit/city war-readiness consumers require the master-normalized APIs before actual declaration,
  4. current supported non-UWAI configuration actually reaches the bad combination.

Exact current source facts already established:
- `AI_doWar()` target selection:
  - iterates known potential enemy teams,
  - computes `eLoopMasterTeam = GET_TEAM(eTarget).getMasterTeam()`,
  - detects `bVassal`,
  - permits via `AI_isOkayVassalTarget(eTarget)`,
  - stores `eBestTarget = eTarget`,
  - calls `AI_setWarPlan(eBestTarget, ...)`.
- `AI_isOkayVassalTarget()`:
  - rejects capitulated target,
  - permits non-vassal directly,
  - for voluntary vassal evaluates area/adjacency relationship between vassal and master and can return true.
- Query redirects:
  - AI_isChosenWar(eIndex) -> target master
  - AI_isSneakAttackPreparing(eIndex) -> target master
  - AI_mayAttack(eDefender) -> defender master
- Cache:
  - AI_updateWarPlanCounts returns without counting vassal targets.
- Legacy `AI_doWar()` first plan-processing loop reads raw plans by actual team ID, so storage is still visible to that loop.

Exact proof tasks for possible F047
1. Trace every live caller of:
   - AI_isChosenWar
   - AI_isSneakAttackPreparing / Ready
   - AI_mayAttack
   - raw AI_getWarPlan
   in CvUnitAI / CvSelectionGroupAI / CvCityAI / CvTeamAI and related war execution.
2. Trace PREPARING_TOTAL/LIMITED stored on a vassal:
   - how/when it transitions,
   - what predicate starts actual attacks,
   - whether declaration ultimately targets vassal or master.
3. Search all `AI_setWarPlan` paths for automatic sibling/master plan creation.
4. Trace `CvTeam::declareWar` / vassal-war propagation to see whether plan reconciliation occurs.
5. Historical control:
   - compare K-Mod / pre-1842 / AdvCiv practical(s), especially `advc.104j`,
   - determine whether master redirects and vassal target selection were designed together or introduced separately.
6. Current configuration reachability:
   - prove supported legacy AI path can select a voluntary vassal while UWAI is disabled.
7. Collision search if confirmed.
8. Promote only if an actual current strategy/execution failure survives; otherwise record as intentional dual-key semantics or robustness debt.

F047 - provisional KI#368 - advc.104j master-normalized vassal war-plan queries but left legacy AI_doWar writing plans to the vassal team
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT CONFIGURATION-REACHABLE WAR-PLANNING DEFECT.

Root:
- K-Mod's original representation was internally consistent:
  - war target selection could choose a voluntary vassal,
  - `AI_setWarPlan(vassal, ...)` stored the plan on that vassal team,
  - `AI_isChosenWar(vassal)` / `AI_isSneakAttackPreparing(vassal)` read the same vassal key.
- AdvCiv practical 1366 (`82ed992626...`, change `advc.104j`) changed the READ/query side to normalize vassal targets to their master:
  - `AI_isChosenWar(eIndex)` -> `AI_getWarPlan(GET_TEAM(eIndex).getMasterTeam())`
  - `AI_isSneakAttackPreparing(eIndex)` -> master-keyed plan
  - pathing / tactical readiness was likewise changed to use the target master.
- Practical 1366's own comment says:
  "Since a war plan against a master implies a war plan against its vassal, I don't think this function is relevant anymore."
  about `AI_isOkayVassalTarget`.
- Its commit log explicitly says:
  "Fixed issues that had prevented the AI from starting a war by invading a vassal. [advc.104j]"
- However, practical 1366 did NOT normalize the legacy `AI_doWar()` target-selection/write side.

Current legacy selection/write path:
- Legacy AI_doWar still iterates possible major targets and can select a VOLUNTARY VASSAL:
  - `eTarget = it->getID()`
  - `eLoopMasterTeam = GET_TEAM(eTarget).getMasterTeam()`
  - `bVassal = (eLoopMasterTeam != eTarget)`
  - if vassal, `AI_isOkayVassalTarget(eTarget)` can return true.
- It intentionally rejects capitulated vassals, but some voluntary vassals remain valid targets.
- If selected:
  - `eBestTarget = eTarget` remains the VASSAL team ID,
  - `AI_setWarPlan(eBestTarget, WARPLAN_PREPARING_TOTAL/LIMITED or DOGPILE...)`
    stores the plan on the VASSAL key.
- `AI_startWarVal(eTarget,...)` knows the target's master for coalition valuation but does not normalize the stored target.
- Search of current AI_setWarPlan paths found no automatic creation of a sibling plan on the target MASTER when legacy AI_doWar selects the vassal.
- AI_setWarPlan's own vassal propagation concerns OUR AI vassals inheriting our target; it does not normalize the ENEMY target to its master.

Why the plan is partly visible but tactically unusable:
- AI_doWar's first plan-maintenance loop reads raw:
  `AI_getWarPlan(eLoopTeam)`.
- Therefore a vassal-stored PREPARING plan can age and transition to WARPLAN_LIMITED / WARPLAN_TOTAL.
- But the APIs that permit/execute an undeclared attack against that target normalize to the target master:
  - `AI_isChosenWar(vassal)` -> master plan
  - `AI_isSneakAttackPreparing(vassal)` -> master plan
  - `AI_isSneakAttackReady(vassal)` -> master-keyed chosen/preparing tests
  - `AI_mayAttack(vassal)` -> defender master plan
  - `CvSelectionGroupAI::AI_isDeclareWar(vassal plot)` -> target master plan for limited-war semantics
  - GroupPathFinder `MOVE_DECLARE_WAR` handling -> AI_isSneakAttackReady(target)
  - `CvUnit::canMoveInto(..., bDeclareWar=true)` for AI requires AI_isSneakAttackReady(plot team)
  - `CvUnitAI::AI_considerDOW()` only declares when AI_isSneakAttackReady(plot team) is true.
- With only a vassal-keyed plan, all of those master-normalized readiness checks see NO_WARPLAN.
- The AI therefore cannot use the normal tactical border-crossing / path-DOW mechanism to execute the planned undeclared war.

Timeout consequence:
- After a PREPARING vassal-keyed plan matures to LIMITED/TOTAL, AI_doWar's raw plan loop still sees it.
- `AI_abandonWarPlanIfTimedOut()` checks whether any member has an enemy-target mission against the vassal team.
- Because master-keyed readiness prevents normal mission/path declaration setup, the plan can remain inactive.
- Once the state counter exceeds the normal abandon threshold, the vassal-keyed plan is cleared.
- Thus the legacy planner can select, prepare and then abandon a war against a voluntary vassal without the tactical layer ever recognizing it as an imminent war.

Current reachability:
- Full UWAI returns before the legacy target-selection half of AI_doWar.
- Legacy K-Mod/BBAI war planning remains a supported path when UWAI is disabled / legacy K-Mod-style configurations are used (the same configuration family relevant to F033).
- `canEventuallyDeclareWar(vassal)` deliberately calls `canChangeWarPeace(eTeam, true)`, so an outside team may declare war on a voluntary vassal; target reachability is not blocked by the core diplomacy API.
- AI_isOkayVassalTarget exists and remains called precisely to allow suitable voluntary-vassal targets.

Ancestry classification:
- K-Mod target storage/query was same-key and therefore did not have this particular split.
- AdvCiv practical 1366 / advc.104j introduced master normalization on the tactical/query side while leaving K-Mod's vassal-target selection/write side intact.
- This is best classified as an AdvCiv incomplete-fix / cross-contract regression surviving SAS.

Collision:
- Semantic searches for `AI_isOkayVassalTarget`, `advc.104j`, vassal war-plan/invasion/sneak-plan wording found no same-root current Album or reviewed Known-Issues entry.

Repair direction:
Preferred architecture:
1. Normalize the LEGACY target to the master before storing a chosen/preparing war plan:
   - if selection evaluates a voluntary vassal's geography/value, retain that as the tactical subtarget if useful,
   - but strategic war-plan key should be the master team, matching advc.104j's query/tactical contract.
2. Or centralize a canonical `warPlanTargetTeam(eTeam)` helper and use it consistently for BOTH writes and reads.
3. Reevaluate/remove `AI_isOkayVassalTarget()` from legacy strategic plan-key selection; practical 1366 already comments that the function became conceptually irrelevant once plans are master-keyed.
4. Preserve the intended ability to invade a vassal when that is the attractive front of a war against its master; do not simply ban vassal invasion.

F048 - provisional KI#369 - Permanent Alliance merge discards absorbed team's SharedWarSuccess toward third parties
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT CROSS-FILE DIPLOMACY-STATE MERGE DEFECT, inherited AdvCiv.

Feature semantics:
- `advc.130m` introduced `SharedWarSuccess` as the actual-contribution component of the lasting shared-war relationship bonus.
- Current `CvPlayerAI::AI_getShareWarAttitude` consumes:
  - `AI_getShareWarCounter(eTeam)` for duration/cap, and
  - `AI_getSharedWarSuccess(eTeam)` for the other side's actual military contribution.
- The value therefore has current attitude and diplomacy consequences.

Permanent Alliance state merge:
- `CvTeam::addTeam(eTeam)` permanently absorbs `eTeam` into `getID()`.
- `CvTeam::shareCounters(eTeam)` merges the absorbed team's OWN relationship state toward every third-party team.
- It explicitly merges, among others:
  - WarWeariness
  - StolenVisibilityTimer
  - AtWarCounter
  - AtPeaceCounter
  - TurnsAtPeace
  - HasMetCounter
  - OpenBordersCounter
  - DefensivePactCounter
  - ShareWarCounter
  - WarSuccess
  - EnemyPeacetimeTradeValue
  - EnemyPeacetimeGrantValue
- For these own-side counters, the established `shareCounters` policy is generally to retain the larger/stronger of the two team values.
- `SharedWarSuccess` is absent from this merge.

Opposite-direction evidence makes the omission particularly clear:
- Later in the same `CvTeam::addTeam`, every third-party team `kOther` has its state TOWARD the merged team recomputed.
- That code explicitly includes:
  `kOther.AI_setSharedWarSuccess(getID(),
      (iOriginalTeamSize * kOther.AI_getSharedWarSuccess(getID()) +
       iSecondTeamSize * kOther.AI_getSharedWarSuccess(eTeam)) /
       getNumMembers());`
- Thus the merge handles SharedWarSuccess in the third-party -> merged-team direction,
  while failing to merge the merged-team -> third-party direction.
- After A absorbs B:
  - outsider C's memory of A+B's contribution is combined,
  - merged A's memory of C's contribution retains only old A's value,
  - B's accumulated history of C's shared-war contribution is silently discarded.

Historical evidence:
- AdvCiv `advc.130m` feature lineage introduced SharedWarSuccess and explicitly added the third-party-side Permanent Alliance merge.
- No corresponding addition to `shareCounters` was made.
- This is consistent with a field added after the older K-Mod counter-merging routine and omitted from one side of the merge contract.
- No descendant repair found.

Current reachability:
- `GAMEOPTION_PERMANENT_ALLIANCES` remains a visible supported game option.
- Current `CIV4TechInfos.xml` contains a live tech with `<bPermanentAllianceTrading>1`.
- Therefore `CvTeam::addTeam` / Permanent Alliance state merging remains supported current gameplay, not dormant compatibility code.

Current consequence:
- The absorbed alliance partner's accumulated shared-war contribution history toward third parties is lost at merge.
- `AI_getShareWarAttitude()` thereafter uses the surviving merged-team SharedWarSuccess, so the resulting relationship bonus can be lower than the state carried by the absorbed team.
- The relationship becomes directionally asymmetric despite explicit reverse-direction merging.

Collision:
- Current reviewed Known Issues / Album searches found no existing same-root Permanent Alliance + SharedWarSuccess merge issue.

Repair direction:
- Add `AI_getSharedWarSuccess` / `AI_setSharedWarSuccess` to `CvTeam::shareCounters`.
- The conservative policy should follow that function's established OWN-side merge semantics (normally retain the stronger/max state), while preserving the existing team-size-weighted reverse direction.
- The correctness requirement is that the absorbed team's SharedWarSuccess not be silently discarded; exact merge weighting can be reviewed alongside the surrounding Permanent Alliance policy.

- A transient post-WIP9 thought suggested practical 1842 might further hide voluntary-vassal plans from legacy `AI_doWar()` maintenance by excluding the foreign vassal from `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF(ourTeam)`.

- A FOREIGN voluntary vassal belonging to another master remains an outside-locus potential enemy and can still be iterated.
- Therefore practical 1842 should NOT be cited as aggravating F047 by making the raw vassal-keyed plan invisible to AI_doWar's maintenance loop.
- The correct F047 root remains exactly the original WIP7 analysis:
  - legacy selection/write can store chosen/preparing plan on the voluntary VASSAL key,
  - advc.104j query/tactical APIs normalize that target to the MASTER key,
  - raw planner maintenance can still see/age the vassal-keyed plan,
  - tactical readiness/path/DOW consumers looking under the master key can fail to recognize it.

OPEN UNNUMBERED CANDIDATE - possible F049
Candidate title:
- Permanent Alliance merge can discard the OUTSIDER'S Open Borders relationship counter toward the absorbed team.

Current source structure:
- `CvTeam::addTeam(eTeam)` permanently absorbs team B (`eTeam`) into surviving team A (`getID()`).
- Before the final counter merge, relationship propagation can create A<->C Open Borders when B already had Open Borders with third-party C.
- `CvTeam::setOpenBorders` resets the counter for the relationship endpoint whose status changes:
  `AI().AI_setOpenBordersCounter(eIndex, 0)`.
- Thus when A did not previously have Open Borders with C but B did:
  - creation of A->C Open Borders resets new A->C counter to 0,
  - creation of C->A Open Borders resets new C->A counter to 0,
  - old B->C and C->B counters remain attached to B until merge cleanup.

Own-side merge:
- `CvTeam::shareCounters(B)` explicitly merges B->C into surviving A->C using the routine's established stronger/max counter semantics.
- It includes `AI_getOpenBordersCounter` / `AI_setOpenBordersCounter`.
- Therefore B's own Open Borders history toward C is preserved on the surviving team's side.

Reverse third-party merge:
- Later in `CvTeam::addTeam`, each outsider `kOther` has many values TOWARD the merged team combined:
  - AtWarCounter
  - AtPeaceCounter
  - HasMetCounter
  - WarWeariness
  - DefensivePactCounter
  - ShareWarCounter
  - WarSuccess
  - SharedWarSuccess
  - espionage / peace / enemy-trade/grant state and related values.
- This reverse-direction block does NOT merge `kOther.AI_getOpenBordersCounter(...)`.
- Therefore C->A remains the newly reset 0 and old C->B relationship history is discarded.

Concrete supported sequence under investigation:
1. B and C have long-standing Open Borders, with a positive C->B OpenBordersCounter.
2. A and C do not have Open Borders.
3. A permanently allies with / absorbs B.
4. The merged alliance inherits B-C Open Borders.
5. A->C can preserve B's old counter through `shareCounters(B)`.
6. C->A is newly reset to 0 and no reverse merge restores old C->B.
7. C therefore loses accumulated Open Borders relationship history toward the absorbed alliance partner.

Current behavioral consumer to finish proving:
- `CvPlayerAI::AI_getOpenBordersAttitude` reads the team's OpenBordersCounter toward the other team, divides by the leader's OpenBordersAttitudeDivisor and clamps to the change limit.
- Current leader XML is believed to have nonzero Open Borders attitude divisors/change limits.
- Exact consumer/XML line proof still required before promotion.

Ancestry evidence already found:
- K-Mod's initial `CvTeam::shareCounters` includes OWN-side OpenBordersCounter merging.
- K-Mod's initial reverse `CvTeam::addTeam` third-party merge includes several relationship counters but omits OpenBordersCounter.
- Therefore this likely predates AdvCiv as a K-Mod Permanent-Alliance merge omission rather than a new SAS defect.
- Need check later K-Mod/AdvCiv team-merge commits for deliberate semantic change or repair.

Why this is probably distinct from existing roots:
- F041 = counter ceiling/growth helper contract.
- This candidate = Permanent Alliance transition discarding directional relationship history.
- F048 = SharedWarSuccess omitted on merged team's own side.
- Candidate concerns the opposite-direction Open Borders history and appears to have different ancestry.

Proof tasks before F049 promotion:
1. Confirm exact current `AI_getOpenBordersAttitude` use and current leader-data reachability.
2. Confirm exact `setOpenBorders` counter-reset ordering in the Permanent Alliance sequence.
3. Inspect later K-Mod / AdvCiv team-merge history for intended omission/repair.
4. Search Album + reviewed Known Issues for same-root Permanent Alliance/OpenBordersCounter issue.
5. Determine exact repair semantics:
   - adding a late reverse merge may still use newly reset A-side data,
   - preserving/cache old C->A/C->B values before relationship propagation may be required for exact member-weighted semantics.
6. Promote only if all checks survive.

F049 - provisional KI#370 - Permanent Alliance reverse merge omits outsider OpenBordersCounter toward absorbed team
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT K-MOD PERMANENT-ALLIANCE MERGE DEFECT.

Current relationship semantics:
- `CvPlayerAI::AI_getOpenBordersAttitude(ePlayer)` reads the directional TEAM counter:
  `GET_TEAM(getTeam()).AI_getOpenBordersCounter(TEAMID(ePlayer))`
  divided by the leader's OpenBordersAttitudeDivisor and clamped to the OpenBordersAttitudeChangeLimit.
- Current ordinary leader defaults use divisor 25 and change limit +2.
- Therefore OpenBordersCounter is active directional diplomatic relationship state, not diagnostic bookkeeping.

Permanent Alliance transition:
- `CvTeam::addTeam(eTeam)` permanently absorbs team B (`eTeam`) into surviving team A (`getID()`).
- During relationship propagation, if B has Open Borders with outsider C while A does not, the merge establishes:
  - A -> C Open Borders
  - C -> A Open Borders.
- `CvTeam::setOpenBorders(eIndex, true)` resets the directional OpenBordersCounter when the status changes.
- Thus the newly created A/C directions begin from zero while old B/C directional counters still exist on B until the later merge cleanup.

Own-side merge is present:
- `CvTeam::shareCounters(B)` explicitly includes:
  `AI_getOpenBordersCounter` / `AI_setOpenBordersCounter`.
- Consequently B -> C's old Open Borders history is preserved into surviving A -> C using the routine's established stronger/max own-side merge policy.

Reverse third-party merge is missing OpenBordersCounter:
- Later in `CvTeam::addTeam`, every outsider `kOther` has its relationship state TOWARD the merged team combined.
- This block merges:
  - AtWarCounter
  - AtPeaceCounter
  - HasMetCounter
  - WarWeariness
  - DefensivePactCounter
  - ShareWarCounter
  - WarSuccess
  - SharedWarSuccess
  - TurnsAtPeace
  - espionage and enemy peacetime trade/grant state
  - other neighboring relationship values.
- It does NOT merge `kOther.AI_getOpenBordersCounter(...)`.

Concrete supported consequence:
1. B and C have had Open Borders for 50+ turns; C -> B OpenBordersCounter is therefore positive and can contribute +1/+2 attitude.
2. A and C either:
   a. do not currently have Open Borders, or
   b. have a different, shorter Open Borders history.
3. A absorbs B through Permanent Alliance.
4. The new merged alliance inherits B-C Open Borders.
5. B -> C history is merged into A -> C by `shareCounters`.
6. C -> B history is never combined into C -> A.
   - If A lacked Open Borders, C -> A remains the newly reset zero.
   - If A already had Open Borders, only A's prior C -> A history survives instead of the team-size-weighted A/B history.
7. C can therefore abruptly lose part or all of its positive Open Borders attitude toward the newly merged alliance.

Historical evidence / ancestry:
- K-Mod practical 941 (`94d44a04a0...`, "Made some changes to how data is merged when two teams merge") deliberately reworked Permanent-Alliance state combining.
- It retained OpenBordersCounter in the absorbed-team -> outsider `shareCounters` merge.
- Its reverse outsider -> merged-team averaging block combines neighboring relationship counters but omits OpenBordersCounter.
- K-Mod practicals 943/944 and later AdvCiv team-merge descendants did not add the missing reverse merge.
- Best classification: inherited K-Mod directional Permanent-Alliance merge omission surviving AdvCiv/SAS.

Why this is distinct:
- F041 = relationship-counter growth/ceiling helper contract.
- F048 = SharedWarSuccess omitted on merged team's OWN side while reverse side is present.
- F049 = OpenBordersCounter omitted on the OUTSIDER -> merged-team side while own side is present.
- These are distinct state fields, directions and ancestry contracts.

Field-symmetry evidence:
- Comparing durable directional relationship fields confirms the Permanent Alliance merge is generally deliberate rather than broadly incomplete.
- `shareCounters` own-side relationship fields and reverse `addTeam` fields correspond closely.
- OpenBordersCounter is the conspicuous reverse-side omission.
- SharedWarSuccess is the converse own-side omission already owned by F048.
- Other unmerged state is transient/derived/tactical (e.g. StrengthMemory, recent nuke target memory) or has separate recomputation/merge machinery.

Collision:
- Current repository Album and reviewed README_Known_Issues contain no same-root Permanent Alliance + OpenBordersCounter issue.

Repair direction:
- Add reverse team-size-weighted OpenBordersCounter merging in `CvTeam::addTeam`, alongside HasMet/DefensivePact/ShareWar and the other directional relationship counters:
  conceptually:
  `kOther.AI_setOpenBordersCounter(getID(),
      (iOriginalTeamSize * kOther.AI_getOpenBordersCounter(getID()) +
       iSecondTeamSize * kOther.AI_getOpenBordersCounter(eTeam)) /
      getNumMembers());`
- No extra pre-reset cache appears required:
  - if A did not already have OB with C, A's prior directional counter was already zero by ordinary status-reset semantics;
  - if A already had OB, `setOpenBorders(true)` is a no-op and its old counter remains available;
  - B's old counter remains accessible until the merge block.
- Exact arithmetic should follow the existing reverse-block team-size-weighted policy.

POST-WIP12 ADVERSARIAL CERTIFICATION PROGRESS

1. F046 re-proved against exact current declaration ordering
Current `CvTeam::declareWar` ordering is:
- `AI_preDeclareWar(...)`
- set both teams at war
- establish first contact with `meet(...)`
- post-declaration processing
- trigger Defensive Pact / vassal cascading wars.

Cascading declarations still enter the normal `declareWar()` path.
Therefore a Defensive-Pact or vassal-alignment team that has never met the eventual victim can enter
`AI_preDeclareWar()` BEFORE first contact is created.

This re-proves F046:
- practical 1842's outer `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF(getID())` requires pre-existing contact,
- so the direct victim can be filtered out during a first-contact cascading declaration,
- the later `meet()` call does not replay the omitted `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR`.
No later restoration path was found.

2. F047 re-proved through tactical DOW execution
Legacy `AI_doWar()` can still write a chosen/preparing plan on a voluntary VASSAL team ID.
The following current query/execution APIs normalize that target to its MASTER:
- `AI_isChosenWar(vassal)`
- `AI_isSneakAttackPreparing(vassal)`
- `AI_isSneakAttackReady(vassal)`
- `AI_mayAttack(vassal)`
- AI group/path `MOVE_DECLARE_WAR` readiness
- `CvUnit::canMoveInto(..., bDeclareWar=true)` AI readiness
- `CvUnitAI::AI_considerDOW()` readiness.

Direct AI declaration search found no general strategic bypass that repairs the mismatch.
A separate opportunistic "poach undefended units" declaration path exists, but it is independent no-plan behavior
and does not make the vassal-keyed strategic preparation visible to the master-normalized tactical layer.

Important wording correction retained:
- practical 1842 does NOT exclude a FOREIGN voluntary vassal from raw AI_doWar maintenance merely because it is a vassal.
- F047 remains the advc.104j / practical-1366 read-vs-write key split, not an 1842 foreign-vassal exclusion.

3. F048/F049 Permanent Alliance pair re-proved as conspicuous directional omissions
Exact source reconstruction confirms:
- `shareCounters()` deliberately contains OpenBordersCounter on absorbed-team -> outsider direction.
- reverse outsider -> merged-team block omits OpenBordersCounter = F049.
- reverse block explicitly contains SharedWarSuccess.
- `shareCounters()` omits SharedWarSuccess = F048.
Neighboring durable directional relationship state is substantially merged/recomputed, making these two omissions
stand out rather than reflecting an intentionally lossy merge design.

4. F042 re-proved against exact `setVassal` ordering
- `setVassal(..., false, ...)` attempts to re-add existing plans against the newly freed target while the target's
  `m_eMaster` / vassal status is still old.
- `AI_updateWarPlanCounts()` explicitly returns for current vassal targets.
- The intended restoration is therefore blocked by stale transition state exactly as F042 claims.

5. F045 re-proved as incumbent/replacement eligibility divergence
- `setVassal` DOES trigger broad attitude / worst-enemy refresh.
- Therefore F045 is not a missing-refresh bug.
- `AI_updateWorstEnemy()` evaluates the incumbent directly through `AI_enmityValue`,
  while replacement candidates pass the same-master-locus exclusion of `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF`.
- A voluntary master/vassal relation does not force the master's attitude toward the vassal to Friendly.
- A formerly valid worst enemy can therefore remain grandfathered despite being impossible to choose from scratch
  in the same post-vassal state.
- Repair should centralize worst-enemy eligibility for incumbent and replacements.

6. Recent-nuke-memory timing concern closed clean
- Birth history explicitly introduced recent nuke-target memory for same-turn repeated-nuke valuation.
- It is intentionally remembered only for one game turn.
- Its sole current consumer is the second/repeated-strike nuke valuation path.
- No persistence/merge defect established.

- Remaining iterator/control substitutions not already owned resolve as:
  - equivalent populations,
  - intentional no-cheating / master-locus behavior,
  - unused helper changes,
  - or heuristics below correctness threshold.

  it is a prospective heuristic and no hard source contract proves that a subordinate observer's current displeasure
  must be included.
- Do not promote it without stronger evidence.

F047 / provisional KI#368 - BROADENED
Revised root:
- `advc.104j` / practical 1366 established the conceptual rule:
  "a war plan against a master implies a war plan against its vassal."
- It canonicalized several important query/tactical paths to the target MASTER,
  but did not make war-plan target canonicalization universal.
- Current code therefore contains BOTH directions of key inconsistency:
  A) some writers/raw-maintenance paths use a VASSAL key while tactical/query readers normalize to MASTER;
  B) some normal master-keyed preparations are invisible to raw per-vassal area/enemy helper reads.

Manifestation A - legacy planner vassal-key write vs master-normalized tactical read
- Legacy `AI_doWar()` can select a suitable VOLUNTARY VASSAL as `eBestTarget`.
- It writes PREPARING/LIMITED/TOTAL/DOGPILE state on that vassal team ID.
- `AI_isChosenWar(vassal)`, `AI_isSneakAttackPreparing/Ready(vassal)`,
  `AI_mayAttack(vassal)` and AI border/path DOW execution normalize the target to the MASTER.
- No automatic sibling plan is created on the enemy master by that legacy selection/write path.
- Raw AI_doWar plan maintenance can still see/age the vassal-keyed plan;
  do NOT claim practical 1842 hides foreign vassals from that raw loop.
- The tactical layer can nevertheless fail to recognize the plan under the master key,
  leading to prepare/age/abandon behavior without normal strategic DOW execution.
- Reachable when UWAI is disabled / legacy K-Mod-style war planning is active.

Manifestation B - canonical master-key preparation omitted by raw vassal area/enemy reads
- This one is reachable even with normal/default UWAI-style strategic planning.
- A normal plan against independent master M is stored on M.
- `AI_setWarPlan(M, PREPARING_...)` propagates preparations to OUR AI vassals;
  it does NOT create plan entries on M's target vassals.
- Before actual war, therefore:
  `AI_getWarPlan(M) != NO_WARPLAN`
  but for target vassal V:
  `AI_getWarPlan(V) == NO_WARPLAN`.
- Current raw helper/AreaAI code still uses those per-team keys:
  - `AI_countEnemyPowerByArea` loops enemy players and includes area power only when
    `AI_getWarPlan(kEnemy.getTeam()) != NO_WARPLAN`.
  - `AI_countEnemyCitiesByArea` uses the same raw team-plan test.
  - `AI_countEnemyPopulationByArea` also has the same raw test (currently unused).
  - `AI_calculateAreaAIType`'s target and relative-enemy loops similarly skip teams whose raw
    `AI_getWarPlan(team) == NO_WARPLAN`.
- Consequently M's vassal forces/cities can be omitted during PREPARATION even though they will
  necessarily become enemies when war with M begins.

Concrete current consequences of manifestation B
- `AI_calculateAreaAIType` uses `AI_countEnemyPowerByArea` to choose defensive/massing/offensive
  posture and compares own area power against planned-enemy power.
- It also derives target/enemy-city presence from raw per-team plan loops.
- `CvPlayerAI::AI_getTotalFloatingDefendersNeeded` uses
  `AI_countEnemyCitiesByArea` while focusing on land war / sneak preparation.
- Several `CvUnitAI` naval/worker/transport decisions use `AI_countEnemyPowerByArea`.
- During preparation against a master with a substantial vassal presence in the area,
  the AI can therefore underestimate future enemy area power/city presence and underprepare
  defenses or choose the wrong AreaAI posture.
- Once the war is actually declared, `CvTeam::declareWar` cascades war to target vassals and
  `AI_postDeclareWar` creates raw war plans for those declarations; the omission is primarily
  a PRE-WAR planning-state defect.

Why this is the same root, not F050
- Both manifestations come from incomplete canonicalization after practical 1366 / advc.104j:
  some code treats the master as the canonical strategic war target while other reads/writes
  still use raw team IDs.
- Splitting by direction would duplicate the same invariant:
  every logical war-plan lookup/write should consistently map a vassal target to its master
  unless the code explicitly needs a tactical subtarget.
- F047 should therefore be renamed/reframed around inconsistent master/vassal war-plan keying,
  with both manifestations documented.

Historical control
- K-Mod was internally same-key for the relevant vassal target queries/writes.
- Practical 1366 changed `AI_isChosenWar` and `AI_isSneakAttackPreparing` to read the target master
  and modified tactical/path behavior under the explicit "master plan implies vassal plan" idea.
- The same practical left `AI_isOkayVassalTarget` in place and even commented that it no longer
  seemed conceptually relevant.
- Area enemy-count helpers and several raw plan loops were not comprehensively canonicalized.
- This supports classifying F047 as an AdvCiv incomplete-fix / cross-contract regression.

Revised repair direction
1. Introduce one canonical helper for strategic war-plan target keys, e.g. conceptually:
   `canonicalWarPlanTarget(eTeam) = GET_TEAM(eTeam).getMasterTeam()`
   where appropriate.
2. Use the canonical key consistently for strategic plan WRITES and READS.
3. Where tactical code intentionally wants a particular vassal front/subtarget, keep that separate
   from the strategic plan key rather than overloading `m_aeWarPlan`.
4. Audit area/enemy helper loops so a master-key plan includes the master's vassal members/cities/power
   exactly once during preparation.
5. Reassess/remove `AI_isOkayVassalTarget` from strategic key selection; practical 1366 already notes
   that a master-key plan should make direct vassal strategic targeting conceptually unnecessary.

F050 - provisional KI#371 - K-Mod war-success coalition formulas exclude vassal battle results while including vassal power
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT INHERITED K-MOD COALITION-AGGREGATION DEFECT.

Core state contract:
- Ordinary WarSuccess is stored directionally by ACTUAL team ID.
- Combat/city results call `AI_changeWarSuccess` on the actual attacker/defender/city-owner team:
  - defender-side success against actual attacker team,
  - attacker-side success against actual defender team,
  - city capture against actual city team.
- `CvTeam::setVassal()` does NOT transfer ordinary `AI_getWarSuccess` between vassal and master.
- Current `AI_countEnemyWarSuccess()` provides a useful internal control:
  it iterates `CIV_ALIVE, ENEMY_OF`, i.e. all alive enemy teams including vassals, and sums their
  actual WarSuccess against us.

K-Mod `AI_getWarSuccessRating()` population mismatch:
- Current function loops:
  `TeamAIIter<FREE_MAJOR_CIV, ENEMY_OF>`
  and therefore excludes all enemy vassal team IDs.
- For each free enemy master it reads only:
  - `AI_getWarSuccess(master)`
  - `master.AI_getWarSuccess(getID())`.
- It does not sum that master's vassal teams' WarSuccess.
- Yet its power terms use:
  - `iThisTeamPower = getPower(true)`
  - `iOtherTeamPower = master.getPower(true)`.
- `CvTeam::getPower(true)` explicitly adds all alive vassal players' power.
- Therefore the formula measures military POWER over master+vassal coalitions while measuring
  BATTLE RESULTS only over the free-master team IDs.

Symmetric own-side inconsistency:
- When this team is a free master, `getPower(true)` includes OUR vassals.
- WarSuccess earned by our vassal teams remains keyed to those vassal team IDs and is not folded into
  our own `AI_getWarSuccess(masterEnemy)` value.
- Thus combat by either side's vassals can disappear from the success term even while those units
  remain included in coalition power.

Concrete supported case:
1. Free team A is at war with free master M and M's vassal V.
2. Most/all actual fighting occurs between A and V.
3. A destroys many V units; V scores little or nothing.
4. The victories are recorded in `A.AI_getWarSuccess(V)`.
5. `AI_getWarSuccessRating()` never visits V because V is not FREE_MAJOR_CIV.
6. No ordinary WarSuccess transfer moves `A->V` into `A->M`.
7. M's `getPower(true)` nevertheless includes V's military power.
8. The rating can remain neutral/negative based largely on coalition power despite A actually
   winning the battles that the function contract says it is supposed to reflect.
- The reverse loss case is likewise ignored if V is the one defeating A.

Function contract / historical evidence:
- K-Mod practical 116 (`47f56c9c4e...`) explicitly rewrote the BBAI ratio and says:
  - it should "scale properly for wars of different sizes in different eras",
  - +99 means "we are soundly defeating our enemies",
  - zero is neutral.
- That rewrite explicitly filtered enemy teams with `!kLoopTeam.isAVassal()`
  while simultaneously using `getPower(true)`.
- K-Mod practical 455 later renamed the function to `AI_getWarSuccessRating`,
  explicitly stating that it "has nothing to do with capitulation" and is a general war-success rating.
- AdvCiv later refactors preserve the free-enemy population (`FREE_MAJOR_CIV, ENEMY_OF`);
  scaled WarSuccess conversion also preserves it.
- No descendant aggregation of vassal WarSuccess into the master was found.

Force-Peace sibling manifestation:
- K-Mod practical 86 rewrote Force-Peace vote decision similarly:
  - explicitly excludes vassal enemy teams,
  - uses free enemy master's `getPower(true)`,
  - reads only direct `ePeaceTeam <-> free master` WarSuccess.
- Thus the same coalition-population mismatch also exists in that K-Mod force-peace evaluator.
- Current AdvCiv comment notes UWAI can overwrite most K-Mod Force-Peace results when enabled,
  so F050 should not rely solely on this path for current impact.
- Treat it as a sibling manifestation of the same K-Mod aggregation design.

Current impact:
- `AI_getWarSuccessRating()` remains widely consumed by:
  - CvCityAI production / panic / wonder / unit decisions,
  - CvPlayerAI military/economic strategy,
  - CvUnitAI behavior,
  - UWAICache / UWAIAgent,
  - WarUtilityAspect,
  - CvTeamAI war decisions,
  - UI/debug help.
- Therefore omitted vassal battle results can affect default/current AI behavior even when full UWAI is enabled.

Why this is one root:
- K-Mod deliberately tried to avoid treating each vassal as an independent war by iterating only free teams.
- But it canonicalized only the POWER side of the coalition through `getPower(true)`;
  it never canonicalized/aggregated WarSuccess state to the same master coalition.
- The repair invariant is population consistency: coalition power and coalition battle success must cover
  the same master/vassal membership.

Collision:
- Semantic searches of current repository Album and reviewed README_Known_Issues found no same-root
  "vassal WarSuccess omitted from free-master coalition rating / Force Peace" issue.

Repair direction:
1. For each free enemy coalition master, aggregate directional WarSuccess across the master and all its vassals
   before applying coalition power.
2. If the rating concept includes our own vassal power through `getPower(true)`, likewise aggregate the
   WarSuccess earned/suffered by our vassal teams so numerator/state population matches own coalition power.
3. Prefer a centralized coalition/master-locus WarSuccess helper so Force-Peace and `AI_getWarSuccessRating`
   cannot independently repeat the same free-team/raw-team mismatch.
4. Avoid simply re-including every vassal as a separate top-level enemy while retaining `master.getPower(true)`,
   which would double-count coalition power; aggregate vassal battle results under the free master instead.

POST-WIP14 F047 BROADENING / CALLER VERIFICATION
- F047 remains ONE incomplete master/vassal war-plan canonicalization root, not F050:
  - legacy vassal-key strategic writes can be missed by master-normalized tactical/read APIs,
  - canonical master-key preparations can be missed by raw per-vassal area/enemy helpers.
- Additional current consequence confirmed:
  `AI_updateWarPlanCounts()` deliberately ignores plans whose RAW target is currently a vassal.
  Therefore manifestation A's legacy vassal-keyed plan may also leave:
  - `m_bAnyWarPlan == false`
  - per-plan cached counts == 0
  even though the raw planner contains a real preparation/war plan.
- Many production/strategy helpers consume `AI_isAnyWarPlan()`, so manifestation A is not limited to
  tactical border-crossing readiness.
- Conversely, master-key preparations make the global cache correct but still leave raw target-vassal
  AreaAI/enemy aggregators blind during the preparation phase.
- This reinforces one canonical-key invariant rather than creating extra roots.

POST-WIP15 FINAL-SCAN PROGRESS

1. Quantitative claims revalidated against current practical-6239 XML/data
- F034:
  current XML has exactly five default air UnitClasses with DOMAIN_AIR and positive iAirCombat:
  Fighter, Jet Fighter, Bomber, Stealth Bomber, Guided Missile.
  Therefore the simple current-data example in which the rival-team denominator is inflated by roughly 5x
  remains accurate.
- F041 / F049:
  ordinary leader defaults remain:
  - BonusTradeAttitudeDivisor = 50
  - BonusTradeAttitudeChangeLimit = +2
  - OpenBordersAttitudeDivisor = 25
  - OpenBordersAttitudeChangeLimit = +2
  The numeric examples remain current.

2. F035 independence assumption revalidated
- GET_BETTER_UNITS strategy is generated from military-unit quality/availability conditions.
- DAGGER is generated separately from an aggression/early-rush strategic score.
- financial trouble is separately computed state.
- No implication relation was found among these sets.
- Therefore subtracting total Dagger count from FinancialTrouble / GET_BETTER_UNITS counts remains invalid
  set arithmetic, and F035 survives unchanged.

3. F041 birth history revalidated
- Practical 1386 introduced the randomized relationship-counter helper and the large Open Borders / Bonus Trade
  "cap" arguments in the same change.
- Helper semantics cap only the returned 0-2 per-turn binomial increment.
- The large caller caps were therefore ineffective from birth; this is not a later semantic drift.
- Preserve the corrected consequence:
  - Open Borders counter resets on agreement status changes;
  - do NOT claim OB excess survives cancellation;
  - Bonus Trade excess + later decay remains the strong current effect.

4. Division / modulo sweep
- Every syntactically unguarded division/modulo site in CvTeamAI was enumerated and checked against live caller/data invariants.
- Representative `AI_refuseToTalkWarThreshold()` member-count divisor is only reached for a live player's
  at-war team and is therefore nonzero.
- Demographic/yield/team-attitude averages have nonempty team or explicit positive denominator invariants.
- Variable percentage denominators are guarded.
- No F051 from divide-by-zero or denominator reachability.

5. Tiny-army `AI_getWarSuccessRating()` scale - held below threshold
- `militaryUnits * WAR_SUCCESS_ATTACKING / 5` can become very small/zero for tiny armies.
- K-Mod deliberately scaled WarSuccess sensitivity by army size and guards the eventual divisor with max(1,...).
- No source-backed correctness threshold says tiny wars must be smoothed differently.
- Keep as tuning territory, not a finding.

6. Sentinel / type-domain sweep
- Raw NO_TEAM / enum-map callers rechecked.
- APIs using NO_TEAM as "any team" branch before raw indexing.
- Integer-vs-team/player/enum comparisons were scanned again.
- F032 remains the unique confirmed incompatible vote-count/team-ID comparison.
- No new current sentinel/type root.

7. FAssert / default-switch sweep
- Live attitude/yield switches cover current valid enum domains.
- Missing UNITAI_ATTACK_CITY_LEMMING arm in tech-monopoly switch remains dormant because current XML has no
  Lemming `<DefaultUnitAI>`.
- Other default/assert branches are unreachable for supported current enum/data.
- No F051.

8. Unused-parameter / ignored-argument scan
- F033 remains the meaningful ignored target-team parameter caused by own-team iteration.
- `AI_forgiveEnemy(..., bFreed)` is explicitly documented in AdvCiv as unused/not needed.
- `AI_reportSharedWarSuccess(..., eEnemy, ...)` matches the aggregated-distress design; enemy identity is not
  currently semantically required.
- `AI_teamCloseness(..., bConsiderLandTarget, ...)` remains historical API residue after the effect moved to
  AI_playerCloseness.
- No new dropped-behavior root.

9. Comment debt / FIXME / TODO inventory
- Concrete Defensive Pact double-count FIXME = F040.
- Remaining XXX/TODO comments concern strategic approximations, old replaced code, or possible tuning improvements.
- No unowned explicit correctness FIXME remains.

10. Persistent member / reset-read-write-state pass
- CvTeamAI persistent member storage was re-enumerated against constructor/reset/read/write and major diplomacy transitions.
- Apart from already-owned lifecycle/merge roots:
  - F042 war-plan-count restoration,
  - F045 worst-enemy incumbent eligibility,
  - F048 SharedWarSuccess own-side PA merge,
  - F049 OpenBordersCounter reverse PA merge,
  remaining persistent state examined is symmetric, reconstructed, derived, or intentionally transient.
- Current read/write structure remains paired.
- `AI_finalizeInit()` not initially forcing `m_bAnyWarPlan=false` remains a robustness improvement only; no
  independent supported stale-true/no-plan lifecycle was established.

- F050 was the surviving coalition-population defect:
  WarSuccessRating / Force-Peace free-master loops omit vassal battle results while power uses getPower(true).

  surrender code contains explicit design comments about how vassal WarSuccess should affect master's
  capitulation chances, so that subsystem has separate intentional semantics and is below the current defect threshold.
- No additional coalition-aggregation root established after F050.
