F049 / provisional KI#370 - ANCESTRY CORRECTION
Correct classification:
- F049 is NOT introduced by K-Mod practical 941.
- The reverse Permanent-Alliance OpenBordersCounter omission is inherited from original Firaxis team-merge code.

Historical control:
- Civ4 / Warlords / BtS historical source snapshots already show the same directional asymmetry:
  - absorbed-team -> outsider `shareCounters()` preserves OpenBordersCounter,
  - outsider -> merged-team reverse averaging combines neighboring counters but omits OpenBordersCounter.
- K-Mod practical 941 ("Made some changes to how data is merged when two teams merge") refactored team-merge logic
  but preserved the pre-existing omission.
- K-Mod, AdvCiv and SAS therefore inherit this root from Firaxis/Civ4 lineage.

Current defect statement remains unchanged:
- when survivor A absorbs B and B has Open Borders history with outsider C,
  B->C can be preserved into A->C by `shareCounters`,
  while C->B is not merged into C->A;
- if A lacked Open Borders with C, creation of the inherited relationship resets C->A to zero and no reverse merge restores B's history;
- if A already had OB, only A's prior history survives instead of the combined team history.
- Current Open Borders attitude consumes that directional counter, so the outsider can lose relationship attitude history.

Repair remains:
- add reverse team-size-weighted OpenBordersCounter merging in `CvTeam::addTeam`,
  following the existing reverse relationship-counter policy.

F047 / provisional KI#368 - REPAIR SCOPE NARROWED
- Do NOT globally replace every raw `AI_getWarPlan(team)` with master-key lookup.
- After war begins, cascading declarations create genuine per-vassal raw war plans, and some code intentionally needs actual-team active-war state.
- The broken invariant is chiefly PRE-WAR / STRATEGIC canonicalization:
  - strategic plan writes/reads disagree on vassal vs master key,
  - `AI_isAnyWarPlan()` cache ignores raw vassal-key plans,
  - master-key preparations can leave target-vassal area/enemy helpers blind until actual war declaration.
- Preferred repair architecture:
  1. canonicalize strategic/preparation war-plan identity to the master team;
  2. keep tactical subtarget/front selection separate from the strategic key;
  3. preserve actual per-team active-war plans once war is declared;
  4. audit area/enemy preparation helpers to include the master's vassal coalition exactly once.

F050 / provisional KI#371 - SCOPE CHECK
- Additional FREE_MAJOR_CIV / ENEMY_OF loops were inspected.
- Most are correctly asking an independent-master question (e.g. which coalition master would enter/leave war).
- F050 remains distinct because its formulas mix populations:
  - WarSuccess from free-master team IDs only,
  - power through `getPower(true)` including vassals.
- Surrender/capitulation code has explicit separate comments/policies for vassal WarSuccess; do not broaden F050 into that subsystem without a direct contradiction.
- Current best root remains coalition-population consistency in `AI_getWarSuccessRating` plus the Force-Peace sibling.

POST-WIP17 PROTECTED PROGRESS

F047 / provisional KI#368 - additional same-root raw-plan callers
The broadened incomplete master/vassal strategic war-plan canonicalization root now has additional
pre-war readers that expose the same mismatch:

1. `AI_isHasPathToEnemyCity()`
- Raw per-team `AI_getWarPlan(eLoopTeam)` filtering can omit cities belonging to a target master's
  vassal during preparation when the canonical strategic plan exists only on the master.
- This can make path-to-enemy-city reasoning understate the future coalition before declaration.

2. `AI_getRivalAirPower()`
- Enemy-air treatment checks raw `AI_getWarPlan(itRival->getID())`.
- During preparation against a master, air units belonging to the master's vassal can remain outside
  the "enemy" component because no raw plan exists on that vassal yet.
- They may still contribute to the broader rival average, but planned-coalition enemy weighting is incomplete.

3. `AI_getTechMonopolyValue(eTech,eTeam)`
- Military-tech monopoly valuation sets war-plan context through raw `AI_getWarPlan(eTeam)`.
- If `eTeam` is a foreign vassal of the planned master, the strategic coalition is logically part of the
  future war but the raw vassal key can report NO_WARPLAN during preparation.

These are NOT separate findings. They reinforce the same repair invariant:
- canonicalize strategic/preparation coalition identity,
- preserve true per-team active-war state after declaration,
- audit raw plan reads only where they are intended to represent preparation rather than actual team-local war state.

F050 / provisional KI#371 - additional population precision
- `AI_getWarSuccessRating()` mixes populations in one more term:
  - `iThisTeamPower = getPower(true)` includes our vassals,
  - but `iSuccessScale` is based on `getNumMilitaryUnits()` for this team itself.
- Therefore even the WarSuccess smoothing/normalization scale is narrower than the coalition whose power is
  used in the same formula.
- Keep this under F050; it is the same coalition-population consistency defect, not a separate root.
- The key repair remains to define one master/vassal coalition population and use it consistently for:
  WarSuccess earned/suffered, military scale and power.

OPEN UNNUMBERED CANDIDATE - possible F051
Candidate:
- Legacy `AI_declareWarTradeValLegacy()` may underprice a declaration of war against a VOLUNTARY VASSAL
  by valuing the named vassal rather than the master coalition that necessarily enters the war.

Current wrapper:
- `AI_declareWarTradeVal(eTarget,eSponsor)`:
  - with UWAI enabled, explicitly canonicalizes:
    `GET_TEAM(eSponsor).uwai().declareWarTradeVal(GET_TEAM(eTarget).getMasterTeam(), getID())`
    and comments that the target can be a voluntary vassal;
  - without UWAI, calls:
    `AI_declareWarTradeValLegacy(eTarget,eSponsor)`
    with the raw target ID.

Legacy value terms use raw target:
- target city count
- target total population
- target nukes
- `GET_TEAM(eWarTeam).getPower(true)`.
For a voluntary vassal, `getPower(true)` includes that vassal's own vassals (if any) but NOT its master.

Coalition contrast:
- `CvTeam::getDefensivePower()` canonicalizes through `getMasterTeam()` and includes the full same-master coalition.
- `AI_declareWarTrade` denial/power logic can therefore see the full master/vassal coalition while the legacy
  price formula appears to value only the named vassal.

War-propagation fact already established:
- declaring war on a voluntary vassal queues vassal-alignment war against its master / same-locus partners.
- Therefore accepting the trade entails war with more than the named vassal.

Historical anchor:
- AdvCiv practical 1263 / advc.104o split the old K-Mod/BtS valuation into `AI_declareWarTradeValLegacy`.
- The new UWAI branch already normalized a voluntary-vassal target to its master and explicitly documented that
  the raw target can be a voluntary vassal.
- Legacy branch intentionally preserved old raw-target valuation.

DO NOT PROMOTE YET.
Proof still required:
1. Show a supported non-UWAI current trade path can actually request/price war against a voluntary vassal.
2. Show legacy valuation materially omits the master coalition and no later floor/adjustment reliably compensates.
3. Determine whether the legacy formula's raw-front valuation was intentionally preserved or is simply inherited
   K-Mod/BtS behavior made inconsistent with AdvCiv's master-normalized war semantics.
4. Search current Album / reviewed KI for same-root collision.
5. If confirmed, assign F051 / provisional KI#372; otherwise record as heuristic approximation.

F051 - provisional KI#372 - legacy AI joint-war proposal uses untouched -1 cached war price
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT CONFIGURATION-REACHABLE CROSS-FILE REGRESSION.
Root file: `CvPlayerAI.cpp`, discovered through `CvTeamAI::AI_declareWarTradeVal` caller tracing.

Current control flow:
- `CvPlayerAI::AI_proposeWarTrade(eHireling)` initializes:
  `int iBestTeamPrice = -1;`
- UWAI target-selection branch:
  - evaluates candidate joint-war utility,
  - calls `AI_declareWarTradeVal`,
  - assigns `iBestTeamPrice = iTheirPrice` when choosing a target.
- Legacy/K-Mod branch:
  - independently chooses a legal target,
  - never assigns `iBestTeamPrice`.
- After either branch:
  `int iWeReceive = iBestTeamPrice;`
  so the legacy path enters deal balancing with -1.

Concrete no-tech consequence:
1. UWAI disabled / Aggressive AI (Legacy) active.
2. AI sponsor is at war and finds a legal AI hireling + target.
3. Legacy branch chooses `eBestTarget`.
4. No tradable tech is available, so `iHirelingReceives = 0`.
5. `iWeReceive = -1`.
6. Gold-balancing branches do not repair the sentinel.
7. Final deal test:
   `4 * iHirelingReceives > 3 * iWeReceive`
   becomes `0 > -3`, true.
8. AI implements a deal in which the hireling gives `TRADE_WAR` and the sponsor can give nothing.

With a tradable tech:
- positive `iHirelingReceives > -1`,
- because legacy `iBestTargetValue` remains 0, the code can enter the branch that asks the HIRELING to pay gold back
  to the sponsor, even though the sponsor is the one asking for military help.

Ancestry:
- Before AdvCiv practical 1341, the common post-selection path always called:
  `ourTeam.AI_declareWarTradeVal(eBestTeam, civ.getTeam())`
  after either UWAI or legacy target selection.
- Practical 1341 / `a5724ff581...`, title "Some small AI changes":
  - introduced `iBestTeamPrice = -1`,
  - cached/assigned the price only in the UWAI branch,
  - replaced the old common `AI_declareWarTradeVal(...)` call with `iBestTeamPrice`.
- The legacy branch was left without an assignment.
- Later refactors (including extraction to `AI_proposeWarTrade`) preserve the sentinel and branch structure.
- No descendant repair found.

Current supported reachability:
- AdvCiv explicitly supports disabling UWAI through:
  - Custom Game "Aggressive AI (Legacy)" / K-Mod mode,
  - `DISABLE_UWAI`,
  - related legacy testing configurations.
- `AI_proposeWarTrade` is called for AI-AI diplomacy regardless; it has explicit UWAI vs legacy branches.
- Therefore this is not dormant debug code.

Collision:
- No current reviewed Known Issue / Album root for `iBestTeamPrice` or free/inverted legacy AI joint-war compensation found.

Repair direction:
- Restore a valid legacy price before common deal balancing.
- Minimal safe repair:
  after `eBestTarget` is selected, if UWAI is disabled:
  `iBestTeamPrice = kOurTeam.AI_declareWarTradeVal(eBestTarget, kHireling.getTeam());`
  (or assign the price when selecting the legacy target).
- Keep the UWAI cached-price optimization.
- Add/assert `iBestTeamPrice >= 0` once `eBestTarget != NO_TEAM` before monetary/tech balancing so this sentinel cannot silently reappear.

F052 - provisional KI#373 - intended UWAI voluntary-vassal war-trade targets are blocked by inherited BtS TRADE_WAR gate
Status: CONFIRMED CURRENT ADV-CIV INCOMPLETE FEATURE / CROSS-CONTRACT DEFECT.

Inherited game-rule gate:
- Current `CvPlayer::canTradeItem(... TRADE_WAR ...)` requires:
  `!GET_TEAM(eTargetTeam).isAVassal()`.
- Thus ALL vassal teams, including voluntary non-capitulated vassals, are rejected before AI denial/value logic.

Historical lineage:
- Original Civ4 TRADE_WAR game-rule code did not have the universal target-vassal exclusion.
- Warlords used a narrower vassal restriction.
- BtS changed the target rule to reject any `isAVassal()` team.
- AdvCiv inherited that BtS gate unchanged through current practical 6239.

AdvCiv `advc.104o` later introduces an explicit contradictory target contract:
- In `CvPlayerAI::AI_proposeWarTrade`, UWAI target-selection comment says:
  an unpopular VOLUNTARY vassal "should be a valid target even if the master would not be."
- The code itself rejects only `kTarget.isCapitulated()` before testing `canTradeItem`, showing voluntary vassals are intended to remain candidates.
- `CvTeamAI::AI_declareWarTradeVal` UWAI branch explicitly comments:
  "`eWarTeam` can be a (voluntary) vassal"
  and canonicalizes the valuation target to `GET_TEAM(eTarget).getMasterTeam()`.
- `CvTeam::canEventuallyDeclareWar(eTarget)` calls `canChangeWarPeace(eTarget, true)`,
  which permits declaration against a FOREIGN voluntary vassal.
- `CvTeam::declareWar` contains the vassal-alignment cascade that brings the target's master/locus into the war.
- Therefore the underlying war system supports the state that the trade-item gate suppresses.

Concrete lost behavior:
1. Sponsor A is at war with voluntary vassal V (and therefore V's master M).
2. Potential hireling H dislikes V enough to accept a war trade against V but likes M too much to accept the same trade when M is named.
3. AdvCiv UWAI code explicitly intends V to be a valid target in this case.
4. `H.canTradeItem(A, TRADE_WAR(V), ...)` reaches the inherited BtS game-rule gate.
5. `V.isAVassal()` makes the item invalid before `AI_declareWarTrade` / UWAI target valuation runs.
6. The intended target choice is therefore impossible.

User-facing side:
- The same `canTradeItem` contract also prevents ordinary trade UI / AI deal machinery from constructing a war-trade item whose named target is a voluntary vassal.
- The strongest source-backed design contradiction, however, is AdvCiv's own UWAI AI-AI target comment and master-normalized valuation path.

Collision:
- No current reviewed KI / Album entry for the voluntary-vassal `TRADE_WAR` game-rule gate versus advc.104o intent found.
- Distinct from F047:
  F047 = strategic war-plan key canonicalization.
  F052 = diplomatic TRADE_WAR target availability.

Repair direction:
Conservative UWAI-preserving option:
- Relax the target-vassal gate only for VOLUNTARY/non-capitulated vassals when the relevant supported UWAI war-trade path is active.
- Continue rejecting capitulated-vassal target items if that remains the desired user/game-rule policy.
General option:
- Replace universal `!isAVassal()` with a deliberate capitulation/voluntary distinction (e.g. reject only capitulated),
  then ensure both UWAI and legacy valuation/denial paths are coalition-correct.

IMPORTANT REPAIR HAZARD - dormant legacy valuation mismatch
- The earlier unnumbered candidate about `AI_declareWarTradeValLegacy(vassal, sponsor)` underpricing the target master is
  NOT independently current-reachable through normal TRADE_WAR item construction because F052's gate blocks all vassal targets.
- If F052 is fixed by enabling voluntary-vassal targets in LEGACY/K-Mod mode too, this latent mismatch becomes reachable:
  - legacy value uses raw vassal cities/population/nukes/power,
  - `getPower(true)` on a vassal does not include its master,
  - declaring war on the vassal necessarily aligns the master,
  - embargo floor is also raw-target based and does not reliably restore coalition valuation.
- Therefore either:
  1. enable voluntary-vassal war-trade targets only in UWAI mode (whose valuation already canonicalizes to master), or
  2. canonicalize `AI_declareWarTradeValLegacy` to the master coalition at the same time.
- Do NOT assign a separate current finding to the dormant raw legacy price under the present gate.

ROOT NORMALIZATION - F037 absorbs F043 / F044 / F046
Canonical root:
F037 / provisional KI#358
Practical-1842 `AI_preDeclareWar()` uses one attacker-relative `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF`
outer population for four inner memory contracts that originally had different populations.

Why one root:
- Pre-1842 code had one broad loop over every alive non-minor/major player.
- Each inner branch then applied its OWN semantic predicate:
  1. war-despite-tribute reputation:
     observer must know the tribute-paying VICTIM and satisfy separate master-locus conditions;
  2. `MEMORY_MADE_DEMAND` reset:
     declaring player forgets the memory toward ALL other living major players;
  3. direct victim `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR`:
     target-team members are processed whenever a valid declaration occurs, including a first-contact cascade;
  4. third-party `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR_ON_FRIEND`:
     `AI_disapprovesOfDoW()` itself defines the valid observer population and intentionally does not require
     prior contact with the aggressor.
- Practical 1842 replaced the common broad loop with:
  `PlayerIter<MAJOR_CIV, KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF>(getID())`
  keyed to the AGGRESSOR before any of those inner branches execute.
- That single population substitution imposes:
  - aggressor contact,
  - exclusion of aggressor same-master-locus players,
  - and an attacker-relative population,
  on all four logically different contracts.
- Restoring the original broad major-player population (or splitting the four operations into correctly scoped loops)
  repairs all four manifestations together.
- Therefore counting four separate bugs would overstate one refactor root.

Canonical F037 manifestations to preserve:
A. Former F037 / KI#358 manifestation - war despite received tribute
- Observer population should be based on knowledge of the tribute-paying VICTIM plus the original locus condition.
- 1842 instead keys outer eligibility to knowledge/locus relative to the AGGRESSOR.
- Can both omit a valid victim-knowing observer and admit an attacker-knowing observer that lacks the victim-contact
  condition intended by the original code.

B. Former F043 / provisional KI#364 - all arrogant-demand reset
- Practical 1260 explicitly promised that AI civs forget ALL "arrogant demands" when declaring any war.
- Pre-1842 broad loop cleared `MEMORY_MADE_DEMAND` toward all other living major players.
- 1842 only clears players in the aggressor's current potential-enemy iterator.
- Existing demand memories toward current master/vassal-locus players can survive contrary to the feature contract.
- `setVassal()` does not symmetrically clear the master's memories about the new vassal.

C. Former F044 / provisional KI#365 - war-on-friend observer memory
- Pre-1842 broad population let `AI_disapprovesOfDoW()` enforce its own semantics.
- That helper deliberately does NOT require the observer to have met the aggressor and does not simply reject an
  observer for sharing the aggressor's master locus.
- 1842 silently removes those observers before the helper is called.
- Historical `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR_ON_FRIEND` can therefore be omitted and later diplomacy/war utility altered.

D. Former F046 / provisional KI#367 - direct victim memory on first-contact cascading declaration
- `CvTeam::declareWar()` calls `AI_preDeclareWar()` BEFORE `meet()`.
- Defensive Pact / vassal-alignment cascades can declare war between teams that had not previously met.
- The 1842 outer contact filter can therefore remove the direct target's players before the
  `MEMORY_DECLARED_WAR` branch.
- Later contact establishment does not replay the missed memory.

Historical aliases / counting policy:
- F043, F044, F046 remain in WIP history for traceability but are RETIRED AS INDEPENDENT ROOTS.
- KI#364, KI#365, KI#367 are provisional merged aliases; do not create four separate KI entries from this root.
- Later F numbers and provisional KI aliases are NOT renumbered in WIP history, because the user/Codex have already
  received those identifiers.
- If reviewed Known-Issue numbering is deliberately compacted during implementation, do that explicitly and record
  the mapping rather than silently reusing provisional aliases.

Canonical repair direction for F037:
Preferred:
1. Do not drive all pre-declaration memory work through `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF(aggressor)`.
2. Restore/builder a broad living-major observer loop and keep each branch's own semantic predicate,
   matching the pre-1842 contract.
Or:
3. Split the four operations into dedicated loops/targeted team operations:
   - direct victim -> target-team members directly;
   - all-demand reset -> all other alive major players;
   - tribute reputation -> observers satisfying victim-contact/locus conditions;
   - war-on-friend -> broad observers, then `AI_disapprovesOfDoW`.
This is clearer and less likely to reintroduce cross-population coupling.

WHY OTHER CLUSTERS REMAIN SEPARATE

  both involve Permanent-Alliance merge completeness, but:
  - F048 = AdvCiv-added SharedWarSuccess omitted on merged team's own direction;
  - F049 = Firaxis-inherited OpenBordersCounter omitted on outsider->merged reverse direction.
  Different state fields, ancestry and merge statements; separate roots.
- F047 vs F050:
  both concern master/vassal coalitions, but:
  - F047 = strategic war-plan key canonicalization/read-write identity;
  - F050 = WarSuccess/military-scale/power population aggregation.
  Different state contracts and ancestry; separate roots.
- F051 vs F052:
  - F051 = practical-1341 legacy joint-war proposal leaves cached price at -1;
  - F052 = inherited BtS TRADE_WAR gate blocks AdvCiv's intended UWAI voluntary-vassal target.
  Different configuration paths and surgical fixes; separate roots.

F032 / KI#353 - ANCESTRY CORRECTION
- Earlier WIPs incorrectly described practical 1832 as the birth of the vote-count/team-ID comparison.
- Practical 1832 merely MOVED the already-buggy helper from `WarAndPeaceAgent` into `CvTeamAI` nearly verbatim.
- The deleted pre-move implementation already contains the same incompatible comparison:
  conceptually `if (firstMostVotes == agentId)`.
- The same bug is visible in the initial tracked AdvCiv/WPAI import practical 1253 (`f6ff6a182f`, "Initial commit (cont.)").
- Durable classification:
  present from the initial tracked AdvCiv/WPAI code imported into this ancestry; later moved to CvTeamAI by practical 1832.
- Do NOT say practical 1832 introduced F032.

F035 / KI#356 - UPSTREAM BIRTH CONFIRMED
- The invalid Dagger set-subtraction logic already exists in the large BBAI/K-Mod baseline import
  `KMod_3_2414225e64.diff`.
- The comments themselves say "half the non-dagger teammates" while the implementation subtracts independent
  Dagger totals from FinancialTrouble / GET_BETTER_UNITS totals.
- Later K-Mod / AdvCiv changes largely preserve/reformat it.
- Durable classification: inherited BBAI/K-Mod baseline design defect, not an AdvCiv regression.

F040 / KI#361 - ANCESTRY PRECISION
- Shared Defensive Pact double-counting predates AdvCiv practical 1366.
- Pre-1366 chosen-war code already summed each chosen target's `getDefensivePower(getID())`; overlapping DP
  allies could therefore be counted more than once.
- Practical 1366 added vassal de-duplication and explicitly left the note that DP allies can still be double-counted.
- Durable classification:
  inherited BBAI/K-Mod coalition-summation defect, explicitly acknowledged but left unfixed by AdvCiv 1366.
- Do NOT describe 1366 as the birth of the bug.

F045 / KI#366 - ANCESTRY / SCOPE NUANCE
- Pre-1842 `AI_enmityValue()` explicitly rejected `isVassal(eEnemy)`, i.e. OUR MASTER.
- Practical 1842 removed that local check while moving candidate eligibility into
  `KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF`, apparently relying on the iterator to enforce master-locus eligibility.
- Current `AI_updateWorstEnemy()` nevertheless evaluates the INCUMBENT directly through `AI_enmityValue`
  before iterating replacement candidates.
- Thus the historically strongest unambiguous regression case is:
  a former worst enemy later becomes OUR MASTER; the old value function would have rejected it, while the
  current incumbent path can bypass the iterator exclusion.
- Current candidate semantics are broader and exclude the whole same-master locus, so an own voluntary vassal
  can likewise become grandfathered after a relationship transition.
- Durable wording should focus on the CURRENT invariant:
  incumbent and replacement worst-enemy eligibility diverge.
- Use "our master" as the strongest historical-control example; do not overstate that pre-1842 code had exactly
  the same broad same-locus policy in every direction.

F048 / KI#369 - FEATURE-LINEAGE ANCHOR
- SharedWarSuccess is visible in the initial tracked AdvCiv/advc.130m implementation practical 1253.
- That implementation explicitly includes SharedWarSuccess in the outsider -> merged-team Permanent Alliance
  merge while the older own-side `shareCounters()` routine omits the new field.
- Durable classification:
  omission present in the initial tracked AdvCiv/advc.130m SharedWarSuccess implementation.
- Since the local tracked history begins around this import, avoid claiming practical 1253 is necessarily the
  first-ever external conception of the feature; it is the first tracked ancestry point and already contains the mismatch.

F050 / KI#371 - K-MOD BIRTH CONFIRMED
- K-Mod practical 116 (`47f56c9c4e...`) rewrote the BBAI war-success ratio to scale across war sizes/eras.
- The rewrite explicitly combines:
  - WarSuccess only from non-vassal/free enemy team IDs,
  - coalition power through `getPower(true)` including vassals.
- Later code preserves that population mismatch.
- Durable classification: K-Mod practical-116 coalition-aggregation design defect.

F051 / KI#372 - SUPPORTED LEGACY CONFIGURATION REACHABILITY CONFIRMED
Current option plumbing makes the legacy joint-war path an intentional supported game configuration:
- `CvGameAI::AI_sortOutUWAIOptions()` maps the normal Custom Game "Aggressive AI (Legacy)" option to
  `setUseLegacyAI(true)`.
- `GlobalDefines_devel.xml` documents:
  - `DISABLE_UWAI`
  - `UWAI_IN_BACKGROUND` (UWAI logs, K-Mod/legacy makes decisions).
- `GlobalDefines_advc.xml` exposes `USE_KMOD_AI_NONAGGRESSIVE`.
- UI text names the option "Aggressive AI (Legacy)".
- Therefore F051 is not developer-only or reachable only through unsupported source hacking.

Specific clean dispositions:
1. `AI_countVSNonMembers`
- Intentionally counts PLAYER-level nonmembers for the diplomatic-victory obstacle metric.
- Its callers use alive civilization player counts too.
- No team/player-domain bug.

2. `AI_isAnyCloseToReligiousVictory`
- Straight any-member wrapper.
- No population mismatch.

3. Circumnavigation / map-trade helper
- Team-wide "can any member train a suitable sea unit?" logic is intentional for team map-trade valuation/refusal.
- No first-member or wrong-team defect.

4. `AI_isMasterPlanningLandWar`
- Inherited BBAI/K-Mod comment "Master has no presence here" can be imprecise when the master's AreaAI is merely NEUTRAL.
- Behavior is a stochastic production heuristic, not a hard zero-city/presence contract.
- Retain as comment/heuristic debt only.

5. `AI_refuseToTalkWarThreshold`
- Member-count divisor has no explicit max(1), but live caller concerns a living at-war team and therefore has at least one alive member.
- No current divide-by-zero.

6. `AI_noWarProbAdjusted`
- Raw attitude enum zero means ATTITUDE_FURIOUS in the current enum domain.
- No enum/sentinel defect.

7. `AI_countEnemyDangerByArea`
- PUF data passes a leader player ID, but PUF_isPotentialEnemy converts the supplied player through TEAMID.
- No TeamTypes/PlayerTypes mismatch.

8. `AI_makePeaceTradeVal`
- Counter/attitude averaging and denominators remain guarded / positive under live contracts.
- No new root.

- Behavior matches its intended diplomacy-state contract.

F042 / KI#363 - TRANSITION DIRECTION REFINED
Exact `CvTeam::setVassal` ordering now proves a useful asymmetry:
- BECOMING a vassal:
  cache removal happens BEFORE `m_eMaster` is assigned.
  Target still appears independent, so `AI_updateWarPlanCounts` removes the old count correctly.
- BECOMING FREE:
  attempted cache restoration happens BEFORE `m_eMaster` is cleared.
  Target still appears a vassal, so `AI_updateWarPlanCounts` returns and the restoration is blocked.
- Therefore F042 is a clean one-direction transition-order bug, not a vague general vassal-cache lifecycle problem.

F048 / KI#369 - SEMANTIC REPROOF
- `AI_changeWarSuccess` reports contribution to allies through `AI_reportSharedWarSuccess`.
- `AI_reportSharedWarSuccess` stores directional, accumulating partner-contribution state with decay.
- `CvPlayerAI::AI_getShareWarAttitude` directly consumes
  `kOurTeam.AI_getSharedWarSuccess(eTeam)`.
- `CvTeam::addTeam` reverse outsider->merged-team merge explicitly combines SharedWarSuccess,
  while own-side `shareCounters` omits it.
- Thus the omitted state is unquestionably live relationship history after a Permanent Alliance merge.

F049 / KI#370 - SEMANTIC REPROOF
- `CvPlayerAI::AI_getOpenBordersAttitude` directly consumes:
  `GET_TEAM(getTeam()).AI_getOpenBordersCounter(TEAMID(ePlayer))`.
- own-side `shareCounters` preserves OpenBordersCounter;
  reverse outsider->merged-team `addTeam` merge omits it.
- Firaxis/Civ4 ancestry correction from WIP17 remains authoritative.

F050 / KI#371 - SEMANTIC REPROOF
- `CvTeam::getPower(true)` explicitly sums own members plus all vassal players.
- ordinary WarSuccess remains keyed to actual team IDs.
- `AI_getWarSuccessRating` reads only free enemy master-team WarSuccess while using coalition power.
- The population mismatch is therefore real and current.

F051 / KI#372 - EXACT BIRTH + CALLER REPROOF
- Practical 1341 / `a5724ff581...` exact diff was inspected again.
- Before the practical, the common post-target-selection path computed:
  `AI_declareWarTradeVal(eBestTeam, hirelingTeam)`
  after either UWAI or legacy selection.
- Practical 1341:
  - introduced `iBestTeamPrice = -1`,
  - assigned it only while selecting the UWAI target,
  - replaced the old common price call with that cached variable,
  - left the legacy target-selection branch without any assignment.
- Current `CvPlayerAI::AI_proposeWarTrade` preserves this.
- Supported reachability is explicit:
  - Custom Game "Aggressive AI (Legacy)" -> legacy AI,
  - DISABLE_UWAI,
  - UWAI_IN_BACKGROUND,
  - USE_KMOD_AI_NONAGGRESSIVE.
- The city-for-war fallback suspicion is KILLED:
  `kHireling.AI_cityTradeVal(city, kHireling.getID())` is the correct acquire-value contract when callee==recipient.

F052 / KI#373 - ADVERSARIAL FEATURE-CONTRACT REPROOF
Current game rule:
- `CvPlayer::canTradeItem(... TRADE_WAR ...)` universally rejects target teams with `isAVassal()`.

Current core-war negative control:
- An outside team CAN eventually declare war on a foreign voluntary vassal through
  `canEventuallyDeclareWar` / `canChangeWarPeace(..., true)`.
- Declaration then aligns the target's master/locus normally.
- So the intended voluntary-vassal target is a supported war state; only TRADE_WAR item validity blocks it.

Historical gate lineage:
- Original Civ4: no universal target-vassal TRADE_WAR exclusion.
- Warlords: narrower vassal restriction.
- BtS: universal `!target.isAVassal()` gate.
- AdvCiv inherits the BtS gate.

AdvCiv feature contradiction:
- Initial tracked AdvCiv/UWAI source practical 1253 already says an unpopular VOLUNTARY vassal
  should be a valid joint-war target even if the master would not be.
- The selection rejects only capitulated vassals before testing `canTradeItem`.
- Practical 1263 split legacy/UWAI valuation; UWAI path explicitly says `eWarTeam` can be a voluntary vassal and
  canonicalizes its war-trade value to the target MASTER.
- No later policy statement intentionally disabling this feature has been found.
- Therefore F052 remains a strong inherited BtS gate vs AdvCiv feature-contract mismatch.

Brokered-peace negative control:
- Peace brokerage intentionally uses the independent/master team as coalition representative.
- No mirror defect established.

EXPLICIT WHOLE-FILE COVERAGE CLOSURE
- A comment-stripped parser identifies 172 live out-of-line CvTeamAI definition bodies representing 169 unique method names; the only overload sets are `AI_isHasPathToEnemyCity`, `AI_isTerritoryAccessible`, and `AI_mayAttack`.
- The earlier rough WIP inventory was slightly inflated because it counted the three intentionally commented-out obsolete bodies `AI_calculateCapitalProximity`, `AI_calculatePlotWarValue`, and `AI_calculateBonusWarValue`. Their declarations remain in the header, but the source explicitly records that K-Mod obsoleted them and that their bodies were deleted in 2019. They are not live missing-definition defects because they are unused.
- `CvTeamAI.h` was cross-checked against the implementation. The remaining header-only AI methods are straightforward inline state/accessor helpers (`AI_getWarPlan`, counters, cached counts/flags, `AI_getTeam`, etc.) or the three obsolete declarations above.
- Reset/read/write symmetry was reread for all actual CvTeamAI state: war-plan counters, relation counters, WarSuccess, SharedWarSuccess, enemy trade/grant state, war-plan cache and raw plans, worst enemy, lonely flag, nuke-explosion memory, strength memory, and UWAI payload. No new serialization/reset asymmetry root was established.
- Tiny wrappers and helper families that were previously covered only implicitly were explicitly dispositioned, including initialization/turn hooks, demographic/yield estimators, area helpers, attitude/memory accessors, election/vote wrappers, tech-locus helpers, map/vassal/trade wrappers, relation counters, personality-average accessors, enemy-area helpers, peace/war valuation wrappers, no-war rolls, circumnavigation, master-planning helpers, religious-victory wrappers, plot defense, train-expectation, forgiveness/liberation, tech-monopoly value, and water-area relevance.
- A final independent sentinel/divisor/default/assert sweep did not establish another current defect. Apparent risky divisions are guarded directly, constructed from positive values, or protected by a live-team/game-data contract. Apparent enum/sentinel concerns similarly resolve to valid current domains.

PENDING ROOT REPROOF - F045 / provisional KI#366
Incumbent worst-enemy eligibility can be grandfathered after a master/vassal-locus transition.
- `AI_updateWorstEnemy` seeds the contest with `AI_enmityValue(m_eWorstEnemy)` before entering the candidate iterator.
- Replacement candidates come from `TeamIter<MAJOR_CIV,KNOWN_POTENTIAL_ENEMY_OF>`, whose predicate requires contact and `!isSameMaster(...)`.
- `AI_enmityValue` itself does not impose that same master/locus eligibility rule.
- Therefore the incumbent can remain eligible after it ceases to satisfy the population from which every replacement is drawn; inertia then makes the stale incumbent slightly harder to dislodge.
- Historical control strengthens the root: before the practical-1842 iterator refactor, the local worst-enemy valuation path explicitly rejected the relevant vassal/master relationship. Practical 1842 moved candidate eligibility into the iterator but left the incumbent pre-evaluation outside it.
- KI#208 is distinct: KI#208 fixes whether a second worst-enemy recomputation is requested after trade-memory decay; it does not restore incumbent/candidate population equivalence.
Repair direction:
- Evaluate the incumbent only if it still satisfies the same current worst-enemy eligibility predicate as replacement candidates, or centralize that eligibility into `AI_enmityValue`/a shared helper so incumbent and challenger cannot diverge.

PENDING ROOT REPROOF - F047 / provisional KI#368
Strategic/preparation war-plan master/vassal key identity is incomplete; this is NOT a claim that every raw `AI_getWarPlan` access is wrong.
- The raw accessor is intentionally raw: `AI_getWarPlan(eIndex)` simply reads `m_aeWarPlan[eIndex]`.
- Several strategic helpers explicitly canonicalize a requested defender to its master before reading the plan: notably `AI_isChosenWar`, `AI_isSneakAttackPreparing`, and `AI_mayAttack`.
- `AI_updateWarPlanCounts` deliberately excludes target vassals from cached strategic plan counts.
- However `AI_setWarPlan` stores exactly the raw target supplied and propagates that same raw target to our AI vassals; it does not canonicalize the target locus.
- Supported legacy `AI_doWar` can evaluate a voluntary vassal as the preferred target while separately reasoning about its master, then call `AI_setWarPlan(eBestTarget, ...)` on that raw vassal. The resulting preparation plan is stored on a key that strategic master-normalizing helpers and cached-plan counts do not recognize.
- The opposite inconsistency also survives: when preparation is correctly represented on the master key, several raw per-team consumers can omit the master's vassal coalition during strategic preparation. Current examples include area-strategy target scans, path-to-enemy-city gating, rival air power, enemy power/city/population-by-area helpers, and tech-monopoly war-plan context.
- Historical control: AdvCiv practical 1366 explicitly changed chosen-war/sneak-preparation logic so that a war plan against a master implies the corresponding strategic plan against its vassals. That makes the current mixed raw/master treatment an architectural inconsistency rather than a new policy proposal.
- Active-war negative control matters: once war has actually begun, real per-team active-war state can legitimately be raw. Therefore a global blind replacement of all `AI_getWarPlan(eTeam)` calls with `AI_getWarPlan(master)` would be unsafe and could erase meaningful actual-team state.
Repair direction:
- Define one canonical identity for undeclared strategic/preparation plans, most naturally the target master/locus representative.
- Make legacy target selection/setter use obey that identity.
- Make preparation-phase coalition consumers expand the target locus exactly once when they need actual cities/units/power.
- Preserve raw per-team active-war plans after declaration and keep tactical subtarget/front selection separate from strategic plan identity.

PENDING ROOT REPROOF - F048 / provisional KI#369
Permanent Alliance own-side merge omits absorbed-team SharedWarSuccess toward third parties.
- `AI_changeWarSuccess` feeds `AI_reportSharedWarSuccess`, which stores directional, accumulating relationship state about a war ally's contribution.
- `CvPlayerAI::AI_getShareWarAttitude` consumes our team's `AI_getSharedWarSuccess(eOtherTeam)` directly.
- In `CvTeam::addTeam`, the reverse outsider->newly merged team direction explicitly combines each outsider's SharedWarSuccess toward both pre-merge teams.
- In the own-side `CvTeam::shareCounters(eTeam)` direction, neighboring relationship histories are merged, including WarSuccess, but SharedWarSuccess is absent.
- Hence one directional half of the same live relationship state survives the Permanent Alliance while the absorbed team's own SharedWarSuccess toward third parties is discarded.
- The SharedWarSuccess feature is already present in the earliest tracked AdvCiv/UWAI source lineage, so this is not a recent SAS-only expectation.
Repair direction:
- Merge own-side SharedWarSuccess under the same relationship-history policy chosen for the corresponding directional state, taking care not to double count accumulated contribution.

PENDING ROOT REPROOF - F049 / provisional KI#370
Permanent Alliance outsider-side merge omits OpenBordersCounter toward the absorbed team.
- Own-side `CvTeam::shareCounters` explicitly preserves the larger OpenBordersCounter when combining the two allied teams' histories toward outsiders.
- The reverse outsider->merged-team loop in `CvTeam::addTeam` combines AtWar, AtPeace, HasMet, WarWeariness, DefensivePact, ShareWar, WarSuccess, SharedWarSuccess, peace-turns, enemy-trade/grant state and espionage, but omits OpenBordersCounter.
- `CvPlayerAI::AI_getOpenBordersAttitude` directly consumes the team OpenBordersCounter, so the omitted directional state has live diplomacy consequences after a Permanent Alliance.
- The asymmetry is inherited through Firaxis/K-Mod lineage; it is not caused by the recent SAS counter-cap repair.
Repair direction:
- Add the outsider-side OpenBordersCounter merge using the same team-size-weighted policy as the neighboring directional counters in `addTeam`.

PENDING ROOT REPROOF - F050 / provisional KI#371
`AI_getWarSuccessRating` mixes incompatible team and coalition populations.
- Its success scale is based on the caller team's own members' military-unit count only.
- Direct WarSuccess is keyed to the actual free enemy master-team IDs in the loop.
- Yet both `iThisTeamPower = getPower(true)` and `iOtherTeamPower = enemy.getPower(true)` include vassal power.
- Thus direct-team WarSuccess and an own-team unit-count scale are weighted by broader coalition power; a large or successful vassal can change the rating without supplying corresponding success/unit-scale state to the same population.
- The mismatch originates in inherited K-Mod war-success-rating logic (introduced around practical 116 in the local ancestry), not in KI#361's separate duplicate-coalition enumeration root.
Repair direction:
- Choose a coherent population: either evaluate direct team success against direct team power/unit scale, or explicitly aggregate the same master/vassal coalition's success and scale alongside coalition power. Avoid fixing only one operand.

PENDING ROOT REPROOF - F051 / provisional KI#372
Supported legacy `CvPlayerAI::AI_proposeWarTrade` can enter deal balancing with a price of -1.
- Current code initializes `iBestTeamPrice = -1`.
- The UWAI target-selection branch assigns that variable when it selects a target.
- The supported legacy target-selection branch can select `eBestTarget` and update `iBestValue`, but never assigns `iBestTeamPrice`.
- After either branch, common deal construction initializes `iWeReceive = iBestTeamPrice`; therefore a valid legacy selection starts balancing from -1 rather than the selected target's declaration price.
- Exact ancestry identifies the regression at AdvCiv practical 1341: the old common post-selection `AI_declareWarTradeVal(...)` call was replaced by the cached UWAI price, but the legacy branch was not given a corresponding assignment.
- Reachability is supported rather than theoretical: current configuration paths include Legacy Aggressive AI and the documented UWAI disable/background/nonaggressive switches.
- A suspected city-for-war fallback argument problem was adversarially checked and rejected; `kHireling.AI_cityTradeVal(city, kHireling.getID())` uses the correct acquiring-player valuation contract.
Repair direction:
- On the legacy selection path, compute/cache the selected target's war price before common deal balancing, or restore a common post-selection price computation with the correct legacy/UWAI semantics.

PENDING ROOT REPROOF - F052 / provisional KI#373
Inherited BtS TRADE_WAR item validity blocks an explicit AdvCiv/UWAI voluntary-vassal joint-war feature.
- Current `CvPlayer::canTradeItem(... TRADE_WAR ...)` rejects every target for which `GET_TEAM(eTarget).isAVassal()` is true.
- Current UWAI `AI_proposeWarTrade` intentionally distinguishes capitulated from voluntary vassals and states that an unpopular voluntary vassal should be a valid target even when its master would not be; it rejects only capitulated vassals before calling `canTradeItem`.
- Current `CvTeamAI::AI_declareWarTradeVal` and `AI_declareWarTrade` explicitly master-normalize a voluntary-vassal target for the UWAI valuation/denial contract.
- Core war rules provide the negative control: a foreign voluntary vassal is a supported eventual declaration target and declaration then aligns the master/vassal locus. The blocker is the trade-item gate, not impossibility of the war state.
- Ancestry is coherent:
  - original Civ4 did not have the later universal target-vassal TRADE_WAR exclusion;
  - Warlords used a narrower restriction;
  - BtS introduced/inherited the universal `!target.isAVassal()` style gate;
  - AdvCiv practical 1253 already carries the UWAI comment/selection intent for voluntary vassals;
  - practical 1263 explicitly master-normalizes the UWAI war-trade valuation for such a target.
- The descendant/policy search found no later AdvCiv statement intentionally retracting the voluntary-vassal feature.
- Important repair hazard: simply relaxing the gate for every AI mode would expose the legacy valuation path, which values the raw target and can underprice the wider master coalition. This root should therefore be repaired together with mode-appropriate valuation/canonicalization rather than by deleting one condition blindly.
Repair direction:
- Permit the explicit voluntary-vassal joint-war target where the UWAI contract supports it, while retaining capitulated-vassal exclusions and ensuring the price/denial calculation represents the target master/locus.
- If legacy mode is also meant to support the feature, repair its coalition valuation first or at the same time.

F047 CALLER-ORIENTED WAR-PLAN AUDIT - CLOSING SCOPE
- The final raw-war-plan callsite pass confirms that F047 should stay one architectural root rather than spawn sibling findings for every consumer.
- Raw reads in actual-war/UWAI tactical state are often legitimate and must not be mechanically rewritten.
- The defect boundary is the undeclared strategic/preparation identity: code that asks "are we strategically preparing to attack this political locus?" already tends to normalize to the master, while code that consumes the locus's cities/power/air/path information often iterates actual member teams.
- That producer/consumer split explains both failure directions under one invariant and is the appropriate unit for later repair/review.

- The candidate therefore remains strong enough for independent KI review rather than being downgraded to an uncertain policy suggestion.

NEW NORMALIZED ROOTS
--------------------

F053 / provisional KI#374 — nearby-enemy diagnostics pass the CENTER plot to CvUnit::isEnemy
instead of the nearby unit's ACTUAL plot; the same defect is duplicated in CvUnitAI diagnostics.

Primary evidence:
  SASGameRecordLog.cpp, countSASGameRecordVisibleEnemiesNearPlot (around current line 5419):

    for each pLoopPlot around kCenter
      for each pLoopUnit on pLoopPlot
        if (pLoopUnit == NULL ||
            !pLoopUnit->isEnemy(eTeam, kCenter) ||
            pLoopUnit->isInvisible(eTeam, false))
          continue;

The unit being classified is on pLoopPlot, but combat hostility is queried as though it occupied
kCenter. This matters because CvUnit::isEnemy(TeamTypes, CvPlot const&) deliberately routes through
getCombatOwner(eTeam, kPlot), and the supplied plot is semantically significant. Always-hostile /
hidden-nationality handling can change with the plot; in particular CvUnit::isAlwaysHostile(kPlot)
returns false on a friendly/base plot so that always-hostile units cannot attack into friendly cities.
Consequently a nearby unit can be classified according to the center city's diplomatic/combat-owner
context rather than its own location.

Affected GameRecord fields include:
  visibleEnemiesR2
  visibleCombatEnemiesR2
  nearestEnemyPlayer
  nearestEnemyUnit
  nearestEnemyDist

Independent duplicate:
  CvUnitAI.cpp contains SAS_countVisibleEnemiesNearPlot with the same
  pLoopUnit->isEnemy(eTeam, kCenter) call, used by SAS_logSettlerParking. Thus the BBAI/SAS settler
  diagnostic can be wrong for the same reason. This appears diagnostic-only, not a core AI decision.

Ancestry:
  Introduced in practical 6026 (commit 876158dff2..., "Add BBAI and SASGameSummary AI Settler
  security information") with the wrong plot argument already present; later carried into the
  GameRecord rename/expansion.

Repair direction:
  In both helpers, evaluate hostility at the loop/unit plot, e.g.
    pLoopUnit->isEnemy(eTeam, *pLoopPlot)
  subject to the local pointer/reference conventions. Do not globally replace other isEnemy calls:
  this root is specifically the nearby-unit helper using the wrong spatial context.

Impact:
  Diagnostic/logging accuracy; especially hidden-nationality/always-hostile edge cases. No evidence
  that this helper currently alters gameplay decisions.

Confidence: HIGH.


F054 / provisional KI#375 — the central structured GameRecord formatter silently truncates rows at
2047 bytes, while multiple machine-readable rows contain unbounded/large dynamic inventories.

Primary evidence:
  logSASGameRecordFormattedLine uses a single fixed char buf[2048], calls _vsnprintf with
  sizeof(buf)-1, then forcibly writes the final NUL. The KI#161.2-style hardening prevents an
  unterminated buffer/crash after MSVC truncation, but it cannot preserve content beyond the fixed
  capacity.

Why this is a data-integrity bug here rather than merely a cosmetic logger limitation:
  GameRecord rows are intended to be parsed as structured records, and several rows append lists
  whose size scales with mod/game state, including at least:
    GAME_RECORD_CITY_BUILDINGS ... buildings=%s
    GAME_RECORD_UNIT_COMPOSITION ... unitTypes=%s unitAI=%s
    GAME_RECORD_UNIT_PROMOTIONS ...
    city/unit composition and other type inventories
  A truncated row can therefore lose closing fields or cut a type/count token in half without an
  explicit truncation marker.

Current-tree scale check:
  166 BuildingInfos exist in the supplied SAS XML. Building type names alone average roughly 22
  characters and can reach roughly the high 30s. A mature/modded city inventory can therefore exceed
  the entire formatter capacity independently of the fixed fields surrounding it. Unit and promotion
  type inventories are also large enough to make the ceiling relevant.

Strong negative/control:
  Some intentionally huge coordinate/position rows are already manually chunked at roughly 1500
  bytes before logging. That shows the 2048-byte sink ceiling is real and was consciously avoided in
  selected producers, but the central formatter offers no equivalent protection to the many other
  dynamically growing records.

Bundled/sample logs checked so far do not prove a present truncation event (largest observed line in
one supplied/example log was below the ceiling); the defect is the reachable silent-loss condition,
not a claim that every normal game already hits it.

Ancestry:
  GameRecord inherited the fixed-buffer pattern when introduced around practical 6022; the earlier
  KI#161.2 hardening addressed NUL termination/safety, not structured-row completeness.

Repair direction:
  Prefer a central solution that preserves the complete formatted record (grow/retry or an equivalent
  old-MSVC-compatible formatting strategy). If that is awkward under the VC7.1-era va_list runtime,
  explicitly chunk all variable inventories and/or detect truncation and emit continuation records.
  Merely increasing 2048 to another arbitrary fixed number reduces but does not remove the invariant.

Impact:
  Structured log corruption/loss in sufficiently large rows; parsers can receive syntactically or
  semantically incomplete records.

Confidence: HIGH for the reachable bug; MEDIUM-HIGH for how often stock-sized games trigger it.


F055 / provisional KI#376 — ESPIONAGE stationarySpies/maxFortifyTurns are documented as
"stationary cost-reduction preparation" but count units whose fortify turns cannot represent an
active espionage discount, including home-idle spies and Great Spies.

Primary evidence:
  logSASGameRecordEspionage accepts a unit when either:
    pLoopUnit->isSpy()
    OR AI_getUnitAIType() == UNITAI_GREAT_SPY
  It then increments stationarySpies/maxFortifyTurns solely from getFortifyTurns() before checking
  foreign deployment/territory.

Engine semantics:
  CvUnit::doTurn increments FortifyTurns for every unmoved, non-cargo unit under the normal fortify
  mechanism; it is not an espionage-specific preparation counter.
  CvPlayer::getEspionageMissionCostModifier applies the stationary spy discount only when an actual
  pSpyUnit is supplied and uses that unit's current fortify turns at the mission location. A spy that
  accumulated fortify turns while sitting at home must move to reach a foreign target, which resets
  the preparation; those old home fortify turns therefore do not describe a usable mission discount.

Great Spy contradiction:
  Current UNIT_GREAT_SPY XML has bSpy=0 and its default AI is UNITAI_GREAT_SPY. The logger explicitly
  includes such Great-Spy-role units in stationarySpies/maxFortifyTurns even though they are not
  ordinary spy mission units receiving the normal stationary mission-cost discount.

This conflicts with the function's own explanatory contract, which describes the fields as
"stationary cost-reduction preparation," not merely "espionage-themed units that have stood still."

Ancestry:
  Introduced in practical 6046 (commit 559ee966cb..., espionage/army/GP diagnostic expansion) with
  this interpretation already present.

Repair direction:
  If the metric is meant to measure mission-discount preparation, restrict it to real isSpy() units
  that are stationary in a relevant foreign target context; continue reporting Great Spies through
  their separate Great-Spy/deployment metrics. Alternatively rename/re-document the metric if the
  desired meaning is only generic stationary espionage-role units, but that would abandon the current
  cost-reduction claim.

Impact:
  Diagnostic/logging accuracy; can make a player look as though it has prepared discounted espionage
  missions when it has only idle spies at home and/or stationary Great Spies.

Confidence: HIGH.


CHALLENGED BUT NOT PROMOTED
---------------------------

1. Shared player previous-state bValid / delta ordering:
   Checked. Level-2 player subrows all consume/update their own previous values before the common
   kPrevious.bValid flag is set at the end of logSASGameRecordPlayerSnapshot. No "first helper makes
   later helpers believe old state exists" bug in the fixed-level snapshot path.

2. City happiness / unhappiness / health source decomposition:
   Checked directly against CvCity::happyLevel, unhappyLevel, goodHealth and badHealth. The GameRecord
   helpers mirror the engine components essentially term-for-term. In particular, adding city and
   player ExtraHappiness/ExtraHealth is correct: their getters expose distinct stored fields.

3. globalWarming anger * 10:
   Not a units bug. CvCity::unhappyLevel contains K-Mod's explicit explanation: GwPercentAnger is a
   conventional percentage while Civ4's other anger-percent values are parts per thousand, so *10 is
   the intended conversion. GameRecord matches engine behavior.

4. tradeRoutes vs domesticTradeRoutes + foreignTradeRoutes:
   The city summary's tradeRoutes value comes from CvCity::getTradeRoutes(), i.e. route-slot capacity,
   while domestic/foreign counts enumerate populated getTradeCity slots. Therefore the three values
   need not add up in disorder/plunder/no-partner situations. This is potentially ambiguous naming,
   but "trade routes" is also the engine's established name for the capacity getter; no defect is
   promoted without a stronger parser/documentation contract.

5. revelation/resource hooks and known-area/BFC accounting:
   No independent defect found in the inspected paths. Full-map revelation callers tightly bracket
   the special state; ordinary plot reveal is invoked on the false->true transition; the old area-ID
   varargs issue in the bonus hook is already repaired in current source.

6. ASCII map arithmetic/palette/resampling:
   No root found in the inspected pass. Candidate divisors are guarded; palette validation accounts
   for derived aliases/player symbols; large map payloads are deliberately chunked in key producers.

7. direct literal log-format arity:
   Mechanical audit of 167 literal-format logSASGameRecord calls found zero format-specifier versus
   argument-count mismatches. This does not prove type correctness for every vararg, but it closes a
   broad class of accidental missing/extra arguments.

8. duplicate #include "CvInfo_Symbol.h":
   Harmless cleanup only; no normalized issue.

AdvCiv-SAS_light_source_20260824T132346.zip.

File 011 remains OPEN. Durable completed-file count remains 10/169.
This checkpoint intentionally freezes progress before the final closure tail.

Previously frozen roots F053-F055 remain live. The continuation work since
C011-WIP01 adds F056-F060. F061 remains UNUSED.

CURRENT NORMALIZED ROOT LEDGER
------------------------------

F053 / provisional KI#374 -- nearby-enemy diagnostics pass the wrong plot context to CvUnit::isEnemy

The helper that counts nearby visible enemies iterates a unit on pLoopPlot but
calls pLoopUnit->isEnemy(eTeam, kCenter), where kCenter is the city/settler
center plot rather than the unit's actual plot. CvUnit::isEnemy uses the supplied
plot to determine combat ownership / always-hostile behavior. Hidden-nationality
or otherwise always-hostile units can therefore be classified using the wrong
territorial context.

Affected diagnostic outputs include visibleEnemiesR2, visibleCombatEnemiesR2 and
nearestEnemy*; the same helper shape also exists in the CvUnitAI.cpp settler
logging path. This is a telemetry/diagnostic correctness issue, not gameplay AI
behavior by itself.

Confidence: HIGH.


F054 / provisional KI#375 -- fixed 2048-byte structured-row formatting ceiling can silently truncate rows

All structured rows are formatted through a fixed 2048-byte temporary buffer.
The KI#161.2 hardening guarantees termination / prevents unsafe overflow, but it
does not preserve data beyond the buffer; oversized rows are silently truncated.

This is materially relevant because several GameRecord rows intentionally contain
variable-length machine-readable inventories (CITY_BUILDINGS, unit type/AI
composition, promotion inventories, etc.). The supplied sample's longest observed
row is only about 1.64 KB, so that sample does NOT demonstrate truncation.
However a legality-aware upper-bound check makes the structural defect reachable:
SAS has 123 building classes, and a legal mature city can still carry roughly 117
distinct building entries after civilization UB mappings and ordinary national-
wonder constraints. The building type/count payload alone is roughly 2.8 KB for
several civs before fixed row fields are added.

The codebase itself contains a useful negative control: intentionally large map /
chunk payloads elsewhere are split around ~1500 bytes, showing the 2048 sink is a
real design ceiling rather than an unlimited formatter.

Confidence: HIGH for reachable structured-log truncation; NOT observed in the
provided sample run.


F055 / provisional KI#376 -- stationary-spy preparation metric counts states that do not represent usable mission discount

The espionage diagnostic describes stationarySpies/maxFortifyTurns as stationary
cost-reduction preparation, but it counts every unmoved non-cargo spy-like role
unit, including spies idling at home and Great-Spy-role units. The actual mission
stationary discount uses the acting ordinary spy's fortify turns in the relevant
mission context. UNIT_GREAT_SPY has bSpy=0, yet Great-Spy-role units are explicitly
included by this metric.

Thus the recorder can report substantial "stationary preparation" when no usable
ordinary spy discount is actually prepared against a target.

Ancestry: introduced in practical 6046 (559ee966cb...).

Confidence: HIGH.


F056 / provisional KI#377 -- attacker-loss combat is logged at attacker origin instead of attacked plot

CvUnit::updateCombat knows the actual combat target as pPlot. When the ATTACKER
dies, the combatResult callback runs while that losing attacker still resides on
its origin plot. logSASGameRecordCombatResult reconstructs battle location from
pLoser->plot(), so attacker-loss battles are recorded at the attacker start tile
rather than the tile attacked.

Defender-loss combat happens to be correct because the losing defender occupies
the battle target tile.

Downstream consequences include wrong battle coordinates and, critically, wrong
cityPlot classification for failed attacks against cities. That propagates into
interval cityBattleWins/Losses, per-war cityPlotWins*, Barbarian combat-location
rows, battle records and related diagnostic context.

Ancestry: present in the original practical 6083 GameRecord combat implementation;
later practical 6100/6103 flow and per-war summaries reused the derived cityPlot
and widened the diagnostic damage rather than creating the original error.

Confidence: HIGH.


F057 / provisional KI#378 -- battle-summary displayed interval does not track the real counter-reset boundary

GAME_RECORD_BATTLE_SUMMARY reconstructs its displayed start turn as
currentTurn - configuredInterval + 1 rather than remembering the turn at which its
battle counters were actually reset.

This is already wrong for the first new-game bucket: turn-0 combat can contribute
to the counters while a turn-10 summary can display range 1-10. It becomes more
obviously wrong when a save is loaded at an arbitrary turn or when victory forces
a snapshot/reset in the middle of the configured interval.

The newer production/military-flow bucket is a strong internal negative control:
it explicitly tracks g_iSASGameRecordFlowStartTurn to avoid exactly this class of
false interval label.

Confidence: HIGH.


F058 / provisional KI#379 -- session-local Golden Age / anarchy observations are emitted as unqualified lifetime totals

The recorder starts a fresh log session after loading a save and resets its local
arrays. totalGoldenAgeTurns and totalAnarchyTurns are among those reset values.
They then accumulate only turns observed since the new GameRecord session began,
but their names and emitted fields say totalGoldenAgeTurns / totalAnarchyTurns
without a logged/session validity qualifier.

Elsewhere the recorder is careful about this distinction: recorder-local city and
battle values are named loggedCities*/loggedBattle*, wars loaded midstream expose
unknown start state, and delta rows expose deltaValid=0 until a previous snapshot
exists. The GA/anarchy fields do not.

Therefore loading a turn-200 save can legitimately produce totalGoldenAgeTurns=0
for a civilization that had one or more earlier Golden Ages. Conservative repair
is schema/metadata/renaming (e.g. logged/session totals or validity/start fields),
unless true lifetime totals are deliberately persisted in save state.

Confidence: HIGH as telemetry/schema accuracy; not gameplay logic.


F059 / provisional KI#380 -- raw MAX_INT finite-production sentinels leak into structured city records

The city snapshot writes CvCity::getProductionTurnsLeft() and
CvCity::getProductionNeeded() directly as ordinary numbers. These getters use
MAX_INT (2147483647) as an internal sentinel when no finite answer exists.

Observed directly in the supplied GameRecord sample:
  * PROCESS production rows can emit productionTurns=2147483647 and
    productionNeeded=2147483647;
  * cities with no current production order can emit the same sentinel pair;
  * even ordinary UNIT/BUILDING production can emit productionTurns=2147483647
    during disorder/anarchy because getProductionDifference() is zero there,
    while productionNeeded remains finite.

So a machine-readable field that visually looks like a real ETA/requirement leaks
an implementation sentinel rather than an explicit unknown/infinite/not-applicable
state.

Ancestry: dates to practical 6022, the initial SASGameSummary city-detail row,
which already logged these getters directly.

Confidence: VERY HIGH; observed in supplied log and matched to engine getter contracts.


F060 / provisional KI#381 -- productionConversionX100 reports process output during disorder when CvCity::updateCommerce produces zero

The logger's process-conversion helper says it matches CvCity::updateCommerce, but
it computes process conversion whenever a process is selected and omits the
engine's !isDisorder() gate.

CvCity::updateCommerce zeros ordinary commerce and production-to-commerce process
conversion while the city is in disorder. A human city can retain a PROCESS while
in disorder because CvCity::doProduction handles isProductionProcess() before its
disorder early-return. Therefore the recorder can report Wealth/Research/Culture
conversion output that the city is not actually receiving on that turn.

Ancestry: introduced in practical 6075 / commit 22dc2f0abd... with the
productionConversionX100 field.

Confidence: HIGH.


F061 -- UNUSED


IMPORTANT NEGATIVE RESULTS / CHALLENGED CANDIDATES
--------------------------------------------------

* Shared per-player previous-state deltaValid ordering is correct. Level-2
  subrows consume/update their individual prior values before the common valid
  bit is set at the end of the snapshot.

* Player-history snapshot timing is valid: periodic recording occurs after each
  player has updated current-turn economy/industry/agriculture/power/culture/
  espionage history. Apparent missing-current-turn concerns are rejected.

* Golden Age/anarchy cumulative increment timing matches the turn played under
  those states; F058 is specifically the load-session lifetime-label problem,
  not an off-by-one decrement-timing bug.

* City happiness/unhappiness/health source decomposition matches CvCity engine
  accounting essentially term-for-term. The odd global-warming *10 is K-Mod's
  explicit percentage-to-parts-per-thousand conversion and is intentional.

* tradeRoutes is route-slot capacity while domesticTradeRoutes/foreignTradeRoutes
  count populated routes. This naming is somewhat ambiguous but follows the
  engine getter vocabulary; no defect promoted.

* War start/end hooks, spaceship launch/failure, capital victory-state logging,
  production overflow sampling and Great-Person action hooks are on defensible
  sides of the relevant state transitions.

* Combat Great-Person death is not double logged: lethal kill occurs while the
  unit is still in combat, suppressing the noncombat death hook; combatResult is
  the ordinary combat-death record.

* Buffered bombard sequences have a defensible flush/ordering mechanism and a
  recursion guard. No dropped/reordered-row root found.

* Production overflow is not double-counted. handleOverflow increments the
  interval overflow totals; completion hooks count completed objects and repeat
  overflow values only on detailed attribution rows.

* Worker buildTurnsLeft theoretically has a MAX_INT branch, but the recorder asks
  it only for a live currently executing build where the worker itself contributes
  work. No reachable zero-rate current-build state established; do not promote.

* Great-Person specialist indexing would be unsafe for NO_SPECIALIST in isolation,
  but the sole CvUnit::join caller reaches it only after canJoin validation rejects
  NO_SPECIALIST / invalid specialist state. Unreachable; rejected.

* visibleCombatEnemiesR2 excludes ordinary aircraft (base combat 0, air combat >0).
  After tracing its settler/worker diagnostic purpose, this is not strong enough
  to prove a bug: the field can reasonably mean nearby direct ground/naval danger,
  and ordinary air attacks cannot capture settlers. Keep as naming ambiguity only.

* researchTurns=-1 is explicitly AdvCiv's "infinitely many turns" contract;
  victory countdown -1 is the real inactive-state contract; vote-source timers
  are maintained nonnegative. No second accidental MAX_INT leak like F059 found.

NEW ROOT
--------

F061 / provisional KI#382 -- session rollover can discard pending buffered telemetry

The logger deliberately buffers some records until an ordering boundary rather
than writing them immediately:

* CITY_BOMBARD actions are accumulated in g_kSASGameRecordPendingCityBombard
  and emitted by flushSASGameRecordPendingCityBombard().
* batched plot-change coordinates are accumulated in
  g_aSASGameRecordPlotChanges with g_iSASGameRecordPendingPlotTurn.
* incremental revelation coordinates are accumulated in
  g_aaSASGameRecordRevealedPlots.

Ordinary ordering is sound: logSASGameRecord() flushes a pending bombard before
an unrelated row, flushSASGameRecordTurnChanges() flushes bombard plus the
turn-local coordinate buffers, and endGameTurn calls that turn flush.

The session-start paths are different. Both
startSASGameRecordLogForNewGame() and startSASGameRecordLogForLoadedSave() do:

    rollSASGameRecordLog(...);
    resetSASGameRecordState();

resetSASGameRecordState() then clears the pending bombard object, pending plot
turn, plot-change groups and revelation coordinate vectors. There is no flush
before that reset. Loading is particularly concrete because
CvGame::onAllGameDataRead() starts the new loaded-save log only after all saved
game state has already been read. Therefore an old-session pending buffer cannot
be reconstructed after the reset.

Reachable example: perform a city bombardment at GameRecord level 3 and quickload
before another ordinary GameRecord row/end-turn boundary. The bombard hook has
only accumulated the action in static pending state. The loaded-save start path
rolls to the new log and resets that state, so the final bombard sequence never
appears in the old session log. Batched map/revelation coordinates have the same
class of rollover loss when still pending.

This is recorder durability/history loss, not gameplay behavior. It should not
be overstated as corruption of already-written rows, and abrupt process exit may
need a different solution. The deterministic load/new-game rollover path is the
root established here.

Repair direction needs care: simply flushing after onAllGameDataRead is too late
for map-derived fields because the CvGame/CvMap object already represents the
newly loaded state. A robust fix should flush/finalize pending old-session
telemetry before destructive game-state replacement / before rolling the log, or
make each pending record self-contained enough to finalize safely at rollover.
CITY_BOMBARD itself is already largely self-contained; batched revelation rows
also derive revealedPctX100 at flush time, so those need special attention.

Ancestry: the pending plot-change architecture is already present when
SASGameRecord replaces/absorbs SASGameSummary at practical 6083
(cac3fa4d2b...). CITY_BOMBARD buffering is added at practical 6092
(c30e4e1ef0...) together with reset-time clearing and ordinary-row/end-turn
flushes, but no pre-session-rollover flush. Thus the common rollover gap is not
inherited BtS/AdvCiv gameplay code; it belongs to SAS telemetry history.

Confidence: HIGH for the deterministic pending-buffer loss; repair placement
requires design care.


VARARGS TYPE AUDIT -- COMPLETED
-------------------------------

A corrected whole-CvGameCoreDLL parser now recognizes comparison operators
without mistaking '<'/'>' expressions for C++ template nesting. Exact literal
logSASGameRecord corpus:

* 172 literal call sites
* 2,107 format conversions
* 1,675 %d
* 390 %s
* 38 %S
* 4 %u
* zero format/argument-count mismatches

The type-level follow-up is also clean:

* all 38 %S values are genuine wide-string inputs (CvWString/wchar data),
* all 4 %u values are unsigned values (CvRandom seed / uint timing values),
* no %d site passes a Cv object/reference/pointer in the manner of the historical
  pPlot->getArea() varargs crash; container .size() values are explicitly cast
  to int, and numeric/bool/enum values have valid default promotions,
* no %s/%S narrow/wide crossover was found; temporary CvString values whose
  GetCString() is passed remain alive through the complete call expression.

The old bonus-change CvArea& crash is therefore a useful negative control rather
than evidence of another surviving formatter-type bug. No F062 from varargs.


STATIC-STATE LIFECYCLE -- COMPLETED
-----------------------------------

All file-scope GameRecord state was inventoried again. Session identity/timing
state (timestamp/context, session and previous-snapshot timer, display-context
flag) is intentionally reset by rollSASGameRecordLog(). Recorder observation
state (battle/city totals, player/team/global previous snapshots, flow buckets,
war summaries, pending plot/revelation/bombard state, autoplay counters, etc.) is
reset by resetSASGameRecordState().

g_bSASGameRecordFlushingCityBombard is not persistent session data: it is a
short synchronous recursion guard set immediately around one synthetic write and
restored immediately afterward. No stale-session root was found there.

The one state-lifecycle failure is F061 above: valid pending observations are
cleared at rollover before being finalized.


F053-F060 FINAL-CHALLENGE NOTES SO FAR
--------------------------------------

F053 remains HIGH confidence. CvUnit::isEnemy explicitly documents that the
supplied plot controls combat ownership. getCombatOwner_bulk turns an
always-hostile unit into BARBARIAN_PLAYER when isAlwaysHostile(kPlot) is true,
while isAlwaysHostile(kPlot) deliberately becomes false in the unit owner's base
territory. Passing the observer center instead of pLoopPlot can therefore change
the answer. A concrete direction is an always-hostile/hidden-nationality unit on
its own base tile near a foreign observer center: the actual unit plot suppresses
always-hostile ownership, whereas the foreign center need not. The duplicated
CvUnitAI diagnostic helper has the same wrong-plot expression.

F054 remains HIGH confidence. The 2048-byte _vsnprintf sink silently truncates
rather than failing safely at the schema level. The current sample does not hit
it, but legality-aware city-building payloads can exceed it before fixed row
fields are added.

F055 remains HIGH confidence. The recorded field/comment specifically describes
stationary espionage cost-reduction preparation, but all fortified spy-like
units are counted regardless of deployment; ordinary mission cost reduction uses
the acting pSpyUnit's fortify turns, and UNIT_GREAT_SPY has XML bSpy=0 while this
metric explicitly includes UNITAI_GREAT_SPY.

F056 remains VERY HIGH confidence. CvUnit::updateCombat calls combatResult before
a surviving attacker advances. On attacker death, pLoser is therefore still on
the attacker's origin plot; on defender death, pLoser is on the attacked target.
Using pLoser->plot() cannot represent the battle tile symmetrically. This affects
coordinates and the derived cityPlot aggregates, plus detailed death/battle rows.
Simply substituting pWinner->plot() is not a universal repair because on a
defender death the winning attacker also has not advanced yet; the actual target
plot must be propagated/reconstructed from combat context.

F057 remains VERY HIGH confidence. Battle counters can be reset by a forced
victory snapshot mid-configured-interval and can begin observation only after an
arbitrary-turn save load, yet range start is always back-calculated from the
configured interval. The newer flow bucket's explicit g_iSASGameRecordFlowStartTurn
is the local correct-pattern control.

F058 remains HIGH confidence as a schema/session-accuracy root. The arrays are
reset on every loaded-save log and only count player turns observed afterward,
yet rows call them totalGoldenAgeTurns/totalAnarchyTurns without a session or
validity qualifier. Nearby recorder-local aggregates are explicitly named
logged* or carry validity/start metadata.

F059 remains VERY HIGH confidence and directly observed. CvCity exposes MAX_INT
for process/no-order production and for finite production with zero production
difference; GameRecord writes 2147483647 as an ordinary productionTurns /
productionNeeded value. The supplied example demonstrates the sentinel.

F060 remains HIGH confidence. getSASGameRecordCityProductionConversion claims to
match CvCity::updateCommerce but omits updateCommerce's !isDisorder() guard.
CvCity::doProduction handles a retained production process before its own
disorder early-return, so PROCESS+disorder is reachable while actual process
commerce is zero.

* Static logger state is otherwise partitioned correctly between roll/reset.
* Full-map revelation begin/end assertions are backed by the sole CvTeam caller,
  which supplies a real acquired map-visible tech and a valid civ team; no
  release-assert-only mutation/root found.

  flushes an older pending turn before beginning the new one.
* Spaceship arrivalTurn = launchTurn + countdown survives timing reconstruction:
  the first countdown decrement is on the next game turn, so N reaches zero at

  distinct accidental raw MAX_INT exposure.

C011-WIP03 is preserved byte-for-byte as the cumulative prefix of this C012
artifact. No repository source was modified by the audit.

Nine independent current-tree SASGameRecord roots survive final adversarial
