FINAL NORMALIZED ROOT LEDGER -- FILE 011
----------------------------------------

F053 / provisional KI#374
Nearby-enemy diagnostics evaluate combat ownership at the observer center plot,
not the unit's actual plot.

countSASGameRecordVisibleEnemiesNearPlot iterates pLoopUnit on pLoopPlot but calls
pLoopUnit->isEnemy(eTeam, kCenter). CvUnit::isEnemy explicitly uses the supplied
plot to determine combat ownership. getCombatOwner_bulk can map an always-hostile
unit to BARBARIAN_PLAYER depending on isAlwaysHostile(kPlot), while
isAlwaysHostile(kPlot) deliberately becomes false in the unit owner's base
territory. Therefore replacing pLoopPlot by kCenter can change enemy status.

A concrete direction is an always-hostile/hidden-nationality unit standing on
its own team's base tile near a foreign center: its actual tile suppresses
always-hostile ownership, while the foreign center need not. The same wrong-plot
expression is duplicated in CvUnitAI.cpp's settler diagnostic helper.

Affected telemetry includes visibleEnemiesR2, visibleCombatEnemiesR2 and
nearestEnemy*; no direct gameplay AI behavior is changed by the GameRecord copy,
although the duplicated CvUnitAI diagnostic also becomes misleading.

Repair direction: call isEnemy(eTeam, *pLoopPlot) / use the unit's actual plot.
Confidence: HIGH.


F054 / provisional KI#375
The fixed 2048-byte structured formatter can silently truncate valid rows.

logSASGameRecordFormattedLine formats every structured row through a static
char[2048] using MSVC _vsnprintf. KI#161.2 correctly forces the final NUL byte,
preventing the historical unterminated-buffer/heap-crash class, but oversized
rows are still silently cut and then written as if complete.

This is reachable for variable-length machine-readable inventories. The supplied
example log does not hit the ceiling (largest observed row about 1.64 KB), which
is preserved as an important negative observation. However SAS has 123 building
classes; after civilization-specific building mappings and ordinary national-
wonder constraints, a legal mature city can still hold roughly 117 distinct
building entries. Their TYPE:count payload alone can be about 2.8 KB for several
civilizations before the fixed GAME_RECORD_CITY_BUILDINGS fields are included.
Other intentionally large map producers already chunk around ~1500 bytes, a
useful local control showing that bounded output is part of the logger design.

Repair direction: use a dynamically sized formatting path, or make every
unbounded structured payload use explicit chunking with part/parts metadata.
Confidence: HIGH (reachable structural data loss, not demonstrated in the sample).


F055 / provisional KI#376
The stationary espionage-preparation metric counts states that cannot provide the
claimed ordinary-spy stationary cost reduction.

logSASGameRecordEspionage describes foreign deployment, city infiltration and
"stationary cost-reduction preparation". It counts any included unit with
getFortifyTurns()>0, independent of where it is standing, and explicitly includes
UNITAI_GREAT_SPY. Actual espionage mission cost reduction uses the acting
pSpyUnit->getFortifyTurns(); a spy fortified at home is not presently positioned
to use that target discount, and UNIT_GREAT_SPY has XML bSpy=0 despite the
logger's UNITAI_GREAT_SPY inclusion.

Thus stationarySpies/maxFortifyTurns can report apparent mission preparation
that consists only of home-idle ordinary spies or Great Spies rather than a
usable stationary infiltration state.

Repair direction: define the metric explicitly and count only ordinary spy units
in an eligible foreign/mission context if it is intended to measure current
stationary mission discount. Alternatively rename/split the raw fortified-unit
metric so it does not claim cost-reduction readiness.
Ancestry: introduced in practical 6046 (559ee966cb...).
Confidence: HIGH.


F056 / provisional KI#377
Lethal attacker losses are recorded at the attacker's origin, not the battle
target, causing direction-dependent combat-location and city-battle accounting.

CvUnit::updateCombat has the true target as pPlot. When the attacker dies it
calls combatResult(pDefender, this) before the dead attacker has moved from its
origin plot. When the defender dies it calls combatResult(this, pDefender), and
the defender naturally resides on the target plot. logSASGameRecordCombatResult
then reconstructs the battle tile as pLoser->plot(). The same expression is
therefore correct only when the defender loses.

Consequences for attacker-loss combat include wrong x/y, failed attacks against a
city becoming cityPlot=0, wrong interval/per-war cityPlot wins/losses, and wrong
locations on detailed BARBARIAN_COMBAT / GAME_RECORD_BATTLE rows. Combat Great
General/Great Person death rows also read the losing unit's coordinates and can
inherit the same attacker-origin location.

Simply switching to pWinner->plot() is not a general repair because a winning
attacker also has not advanced at the defender-death callback. The actual combat
target plot (or equivalent attacker/defender context) should be propagated to the
GameRecord hook.

Ancestry: present in the original practical-6083 GameRecord combat implementation;
6100/6103 later reused the derived cityPlot in additional flow/war summaries.
Confidence: VERY HIGH.


F057 / provisional KI#378
GAME_RECORD_BATTLE_SUMMARY fabricates the displayed observation interval instead
of tracking the actual counter-reset boundary.

logSASGameRecordBattleBuckets derives startTurn as
currentTurn - configuredInterval + 1. But its counters can start/reset at other
boundaries: a loaded-save session can begin on an arbitrary turn, and victory can
force a full snapshot/battle-bucket reset in the middle of the configured
interval. The first new-game bucket also can contain turn-0 combat while a
turn-10 / interval-10 row displays range=1-10.

The newer production/military flow bucket is the local correct-pattern control:
it carries g_iSASGameRecordFlowStartTurn, initializes it to the actual log-session
turn, and advances it to iGameTurn+1 after a flush.

Repair direction: track an explicit battle-bucket start turn and update it at
every reset, analogous to the flow bucket.
Confidence: VERY HIGH.


F058 / provisional KI#379
Golden Age/anarchy recorder-session observations are emitted as unqualified
lifetime totals after a loaded save.

g_aiSASGameRecordTotalGoldenAgeTurns and
 g_aiSASGameRecordTotalAnarchyTurns are reset to zero whenever a new loaded-save
GameRecord session starts, then increment once for each player turn observed in
that session. Nevertheless GAME_RECORD_PLAYER and related action rows expose them
as totalGoldenAgeTurns / totalAnarchyTurns without a validity/session qualifier.
A turn-200 load can therefore report zero lifetime total despite earlier Golden
Ages/anarchy that occurred before the load.

This differs from nearby recorder-local quantities whose schema is explicit:
loggedBattle*, loggedCities*, deltaValid, unknown loaded-war start baselines, and
flow start turns.

Repair direction: rename/qualify as logged/session totals and expose session start
or validity, or persist/reconstruct true lifetime counters if lifetime semantics
are desired.
Confidence: HIGH (schema/telemetry accuracy, not gameplay logic).


F059 / provisional KI#380
Raw CvCity MAX_INT production sentinels leak into structured city rows as ordinary
numeric values.

GAME_RECORD_CITY writes CvCity::getProductionTurnsLeft() and
getProductionNeeded() directly. CvCity returns MAX_INT for no finite current
order / ORDER_MAINTAIN process, and getProductionTurnsLeft can also return MAX_INT
for an ordinary unit/building/project whenever current production difference is
zero, e.g. during disorder. The supplied example log directly contains
2147483647 in these fields, including process/empty-production states and finite
production under anarchy/disorder.

GAME_RECORD_BARBARIAN_CITY also writes getProductionTurnsLeft() directly and
shares the productionTurns sentinel exposure.

Repair direction: translate MAX_INT to an explicit schema sentinel such as -1
(or a textual state/validity field) and keep finite productionNeeded only where a
finite train/construct/create order exists.
Ancestry: dates to practical 6022's initial SASGameSummary city-detail row.
Confidence: VERY HIGH and directly observed.


F060 / provisional KI#381
productionConversionX100 reports process output during disorder although
CvCity::updateCommerce gives the city zero process commerce then.

getSASGameRecordCityProductionConversion says it matches CvCity::updateCommerce,
but if a process is selected it always multiplies production yield by the
production-to-commerce modifier. The real CvCity::updateCommerce performs both
ordinary commerce and process conversion only inside if (!isDisorder()).
CvCity::doProduction can retain a production process during disorder because the
process branch returns before doProduction's later disorder early-return.
PROCESS + disorder is therefore a reachable state with actual conversion zero
but positive GameRecord productionConversionX100.

Repair direction: return '-' / zero conversion while isDisorder(), mirroring the
engine contract the helper explicitly claims to reconstruct.
Ancestry: introduced practical 6075 (22dc2f0abd...).
Confidence: HIGH.


F061 / provisional KI#382
Starting a loaded/new game can discard still-buffered telemetry from the old log
session before it is ever written.

CITY_BOMBARD actions, batched plot-change coordinates and incremental revelation
coordinates are intentionally buffered. During ordinary play, the generic logger
and end-turn flush preserve ordering and eventually write them. However both
startSASGameRecordLogForNewGame() and startSASGameRecordLogForLoadedSave() roll the
log and then call resetSASGameRecordState(), which clears all pending buffers;
there is no pre-roll/pre-reset finalization.

Concrete deterministic example: perform a city bombard at log level 3, then
quickload before another ordinary GameRecord row/end-turn. The bombard exists
only in g_kSASGameRecordPendingCityBombard. CvGame::onAllGameDataRead starts the
new loaded-save log after the new game state has been read, and reset discards the
old pending action, so it never reaches the old session file. Pending map/reveal
batches have the same class of tail loss.

Project documentation describes SASGameRecord as a compact factual whole-game /
autoplay record, and load sessions deliberately roll to distinct loadN logs; no
contract was found that permits dropping the old branch tail. Victory explicitly
flushes pending turn changes before its final snapshot, reinforcing that pending
history is meant to be finalized at important boundaries.

Repair placement needs care. Flushing map-derived rows only after
onAllGameDataRead is too late because the game/map state already belongs to the
newly loaded save; either finalize the old session before destructive state
replacement/log roll or make buffered rows self-contained enough for safe late
finalization.

Ancestry: the pending plot architecture is already present when GameRecord is
introduced/renamed at practical 6083 (cac3fa4d2b...); CITY_BOMBARD buffering is
added at practical 6092 (c30e4e1ef0...) with normal-boundary flushes and
reset-time clearing but no session-rollover flush.
Confidence: HIGH for deterministic pending-buffer loss; repair architecture is
less trivial than the diagnosis.


FINAL WHOLE-FILE CLOSURE EVIDENCE
---------------------------------

1. Definition/header/helper inventory

* 284 live top-level definitions / 280 unique callable names reconciled.
* 209 static helpers; every one has at least one live use.
* Every SASGameRecordLog.h declaration has a definition.
* Every externally intended public hook has a live external caller. The two
  apparent getter exceptions are consumed through header macros;
  logSASGameRecordRunStatus is intentionally internal to snapshot generation.

No orphan hook/helper root remains.

2. Structured varargs audit

A corrected parser over the entire CvGameCoreDLL finds exactly:

* 172 literal logSASGameRecord call sites
* 2,107 conversions total
* 1,675 %d
* 390 %s
* 38 %S
* 4 %u
* zero format/argument-count mismatches

Type challenge also closes cleanly: all %S arguments are wide strings, all %u
arguments are unsigned values, narrow/wide %s crossover was not found, temporary
CvString lifetimes survive the full call expression, container .size() arguments
are explicitly int-cast, and no remaining %d expression passes a class/object
reference like the already-fixed CvArea& bonus-change crash.

3. Static state/reset lifecycle

All session identity/timing state is deliberately owned by rollSASGameRecordLog;
all recorder observation/counter/previous-state state is reset by
resetSASGameRecordState. The bombard flushing bool is a synchronous recursion
guard and cannot become ordinary stale session data. F061 is the one surviving
lifecycle defect: valid pending observations are cleared rather than finalized.

4. Index/enum/nullability/sentinel/divisor sweep

No additional live invalid index or caller-nullability root survived. Several
apparently unsafe helpers become safe under their sole validated callers (e.g.
Great-Person join specialist indexing). Direct division sites have positive
construction or explicit guards; landmass divisions run only for positive-sized
landmasses and team percentages guard world totals. Worker build ETA, research
-1, victory countdown -1 and vote timers follow valid engine contracts. F059 is
the distinct accidental raw MAX_INT schema leak.

5. Snapshot and event timing

Player-history timing, Golden Age/anarchy per-turn sampling, city health/happiness
reconstruction, production overflow ownership, war start/end, spaceship launch /
failure, capital victory-progress removal, Great-Person actions, air strike /
interception/bombing and player-elimination reconciliation were traced to their
mutation points. No additional event-order root survived. Ground lethal combat
location remains the asymmetric F056 exception.

6. Rejected semantic candidates retained as negative controls

* deltaValid common-bit ordering is correct.
* tradeRoutes capacity versus populated domestic/foreign route counts is naming
  ambiguity consistent with engine getter vocabulary, not a promoted defect.
* ordinary bombard sequence ordering/recursion is correct; only rollover
  durability is F061.
* aircraft omission from visibleCombatEnemiesR2 was not strong enough to call a
  bug under the settler/worker direct-danger diagnostic semantics.
* combat Great-Person deaths are not normally double logged.
* overflow totals are not double counted by completion rows.
* full-map revelation assertion contracts are guaranteed by the sole CvTeam
  caller in supported current flow.
* spaceship arrivalTurn arithmetic matches countdown timing.
* no second MAX_INT-like sentinel leak was established.

7. Base / ancestry classification

SASGameRecordLog is SAS-specific, so most roots are telemetry/schema issues rather
than inherited AdvCiv/Civ4CE behavior. Base/AdvCiv engine code was used to prove
contracts where necessary (CvUnit combat ownership, CvCity production/commerce,
combat callback timing). Snapshot commit-diff archaeology was used to date the
SAS regressions/features where useful. No root is being mislabeled as a base
AdvCiv gameplay bug merely because an engine getter participates in it.

SASGameRecordLog.cpp is defensibly exhausted for this pass. The nine roots above
remain independently actionable after explicit attempts to disprove them; late
whole-file passes killed several weaker candidates instead of manufacturing
F062. No uncovered method/helper/caller/state/index/format/divisor/event family
remains that justifies keeping file 011 open.

RECOVERY CURSOR

CONFIRMED CURRENT ROOT
----------------------

F062 / provisional KI#383
The SAS vassal-willingness helper refactor accidentally removed the existing
War-Trades hover explanation from WIDGET_LH_GLANCE.

Current CvForeignAdvisor.py deliberately selects WIDGET_LH_GLANCE for a Glance
matrix relation cell when Advisors__ShowGlanceWarTrades is enabled. The visible
fist icon itself is assembled in Python via AttitudeUtil.getAttitudeText(...,
AdvisorOpt.isShowGlanceWarTrades()). In base AdvCiv 1.14, the matching C++
WIDGET_LH_GLANCE tooltip path does two things:

  1. parseLeaderheadRelationsHelp(...)
  2. GAMETEXT.parseWarTradesHelp(szBuffer, data1, data2)

The second call supplies the textual explanation for the special fist state --
"willing to start war" against the relevant third party -- using the same
eligibility checks as the Python icon.

SAS current source still routes Show War Trades cells to WIDGET_LH_GLANCE, but
its C++ case now performs parseLeaderheadRelationsHelp(...) followed only by
parseVassalWillingnessHelp(...). The parseWarTradesHelp call is absent. Result:
the War Trades option can still show its fist icon while hovering that cell no
longer receives AdvCiv's dedicated war-trade explanation; the newly added vassal
willingness line replaced rather than augmented that portion of the tooltip.

Ancestry is exact:

* practical 5178 / 39fcce8ec0... added the vassal-willingness tooltip while
  preserving GAMETEXT.parseWarTradesHelp in WIDGET_LH_GLANCE.
* practical 5179 / daa528a557... refactored the repeated vassal code into
  CvDLLWidgetData::parseVassalWillingnessHelp and, in that refactor hunk, deleted
  GAMETEXT.parseWarTradesHelp without replacing it.
* AdvCiv 1.14 still contains the call, confirming this is a SAS regression rather
  than an upstream policy change.

Repair direction: restore GAMETEXT.parseWarTradesHelp for WIDGET_LH_GLANCE and
then append parseVassalWillingnessHelp; the two describe independent status icons
and are meant to coexist.
Confidence: VERY HIGH.

INITIAL NEGATIVE CONTROL
------------------------

The inherited WIDGET_MENU_ICON -> WIDGET_CONSCRIPT switch fallthrough looks
alarming in both parseHelp and executeAction and exists all the way back through
AdvCiv and vanilla Civ4CE. It was challenged before promotion:

* CvMainInterface.py explicitly hides MainMenuButton whenever the city screen is
  up, i.e. the ordinary state in which a city is selected and conscription UI is
  meaningful.
* Outside the city screen the button is shown only in ordinary map-interface
  state; the current UI contract does not establish a reachable selected-city
  list there that would make the fallthrough's selectedCitiesGameNetMessage
  actually draft a city.
* parseConscriptHelp likewise needs a head-selected city to add conscription help.

Therefore this is retained as suspicious inherited dead/redundant fallthrough,
not promoted as F063 without a reachable side effect. If later caller/UI evidence
shows selected cities can coexist with the visible MainMenuButton, reopen it.

INITIAL SAS-DELTA MAP
---------------------

Relative to current AdvCiv 1.14, substantive SAS changes in this file are small
compared with the 6.3k-line inherited body. Current differences are chiefly:

* cached immutable XML/global-define lookups for several hover helpers;
* optional whole-tooltip <font=N> wrapping through SAS_UI_FONT_HOVER;
* tech-advisor right-click bonus-ID fixes for reveal vs obsolete bonus widgets;
* Foreign Advisor vassal-willingness tooltip support;
* comment/signature formatting and the unified CvForeignAdvisor naming update.

This makes a two-track audit useful: first prove all SAS deltas, then inspect the
much larger inherited dispatch/tooltip body for older AdvCiv/BtS defects.

C012-WIP02 — file 012 CvGameCoreDLL/CvDLLWidgetData.cpp — INCOMPLETE CHECKPOINT
================================================================================
Baseline: AdvCiv-SAS practical 6244 / HEAD 9f56202d8b...
Durable completion remains 11/169 files. This file remains OPEN.
This checkpoint extends C012-WIP01; no source files were modified.

ROOT LEDGER AT THIS CHECKPOINT

F062 / provisional KI#383 — CONFIRMED — SAS-specific regression
WIDGET_LH_GLANCE lost AdvCiv/BULL parseWarTradesHelp when SAS refactored the
vassal-willingness hover into parseVassalWillingnessHelp. Practical 5178 still
called both; practical 5179 (daa528a557...) removed parseWarTradesHelp while
extracting the vassal helper. With BUG "Show War Trades" enabled, Python still
uses WIDGET_LH_GLANCE and can still draw the war-trade fist, but the specialized
"willing to start war" hover explanation is no longer appended. Repair should
restore GAMETEXT.parseWarTradesHelp(...) and then append vassal willingness;
the two statuses are independent.

F063 / provisional KI#384 — STRONG — inherited AdvCiv regression
CvDLLWidgetData::executeAltAction unconditionally executes:
    CvCivilization const& kActiveCiv = *GC.getGame().getActiveCivilization();
before switching on the widget type. CvGame::getActiveCivilization explicitly
returns NULL when getActivePlayer()==NO_PLAYER. The opening-menu Civilopedia is
an intended such state: Sevopedia stores iActivePlayer=-1, and CvGameTextMgr has
explicit comments/branches for "NULL when in opening-menu Civilopedia". The
right-click dispatcher contains many Pedia cases (tech/unit/project/religion/
corporation/etc.) that require no civilization context, but all now encounter
the unconditional dereference first. This widening dates to AdvCiv practical
1648 (553b1a0f8d...), when old per-case doPedia* helpers were replaced by direct
CvPythonCaller jumps and the active-civilization reference was hoisted for only
the WIDGET_TRAIN/WIDGET_CONSTRUCT class-to-civ mappings. Vanilla/Civ4CE keeps
civilization lookup local to those two helpers. Conservative repair: do not
hoist/dereference the pointer; resolve active civ lazily only in cases that need
it, with an opening-menu fallback/default-unit/default-building policy if those
cases can themselves appear there.

F064 / provisional KI#385 — CONFIRMED DIAGNOSTIC DRIFT — inherited, widened by SAS
parseScoreboardCheatText contains a duplicate reconstruction of the legacy
K-Mod/BBAI AI_doWar decision machinery, but it no longer matches the real
CvTeamAI::AI_doWar. Concrete mismatches include:
  * current SAS AI_doWar KI#356 counts non-Dagger members, non-Dagger financial
    trouble, and non-Dagger GET_BETTER_UNITS intersections explicitly; the
    tooltip still uses (financialTroubleCount-daggerCount) and
    (getBetterUnitsCount-daggerCount) against the total team population.
  * actual target loops require AI_haveSeenCities(eTarget); tooltip candidate
    generation does not.
  * actual target defensive power is getDefensivePower(getID()), excluding the
    evaluating team from defensive-pact coalition power; tooltip uses
    getDefensivePower() with no exclusion.
  * actual total-war comparison additionally scales target defensive power by
    2/3; the tooltip uses the unscaled value.
  * actual dogpile support iterates MAJOR_CIV enemies and filters unseen teams;
    the tooltip uses a broader CIV_ALIVE enemy set without the same knowledge
    filter.
Several of these discrepancies already exist in AdvCiv 1.14; SAS KI#356 makes
the first two gate formulas newly/starkly wrong as a mirror of current SAS AI.
This affects debug/diagnostic truth, not gameplay. Because the function presents
war percentages, possible passes and candidates specifically as AI-debug info,
its duplicate logic should either be synchronized with AI_doWar or replaced by
shared query/helper calculations so future AI repairs cannot silently drift.

F065 / provisional KI#386 — CONFIRMED — inherited BtS tooltip defect
parseActionHelp_Mission's MISSION_INFILTRATE branch searches selected units with:
    if (!kSelectedUnit.canEspionage(&kMissionPlot)) continue;
but the mission being explained is Infiltration and CvUnit::infiltrate gates on
canInfiltrate(plot()). CvUnit::canEspionage rejects !isSpy(), whereas SAS
UNIT_GREAT_SPY has bSpy=0 and iEspionagePoints=3000; canInfiltrate instead
requires positive espionage points and a foreign non-Barbarian city. Therefore
a normal Great Spy that can perform MISSION_INFILTRATE fails the tooltip's
predicate, so the intended "+N espionage" yield line is omitted. Civ4CE/BtS
contains the same canEspionage mistake and AdvCiv 1.14 retains it. Repair: use
canInfiltrate(&kMissionPlot, true) (matching the tooltip/test-visible context)
before getEspionagePoints.

REJECTED / DEFERRED IN THIS TRANCHE
- WIDGET_MENU_ICON -> WIDGET_CONSCRIPT missing breaks remain suspicious inherited
  fallthroughs, but MainMenuButton is hidden while city screen is up and no
  verified visible-menu + selected-city path has been established. Do not call
  this a bug without reachability evidence.
- Debug war-percentage division by zero: XML literal zero war-rands belong only
  to LEADER_BARBARIAN and fake LEADER_DEFAULTS. Extremely high vassal counts can
  theoretically integer-reduce max-war rand to zero under FINAL_WAR, but this
  is not being promoted independently absent a realistic/reproduced path.
- SAS bonus right-click data routing (reveal=data2, obsolete=data1) matches the
  actual CvTechChooser widget payloads and is accepted.
- SAS global hover-font wrapper and cached immutable defines have no demonstrated
  defect so far.

NEXT CURSOR
F066 UNUSED.
Continue inside parseActionHelp_Mission after MISSION_INFILTRATE and then audit
remaining action helpers, trade/deal/city/economic hover functions, dispatch
symmetry, enum/index/null/divisor/fallthrough/static-state sweeps, full
header/definition/caller inventory, ancestry and Known-Issue collision pass.
Keep CvDLLWidgetData.cpp open until those closure gates are complete.

ROOT LEDGER AT THIS CHECKPOINT

Unchanged from WIP02: executeAltAction hoists and dereferences

an explicit NO_PLAYER/NULL-active-civilization state and most right-click Pedia
cases need no civilization context. Introduced by practical 1648 refactor.

Unchanged from WIP02: parseScoreboardCheatText's duplicated AI_doWar model has
multiple stale gates/power/knowledge semantics; KI#356 widened the mismatch.

F066 / provisional KI#387 — CONFIRMED — inherited AdvCiv regression
CvDLLWidgetData::parseTradeItem stores eWhoFrom as PlayerTypes but renders the
TRADE_PEACE, TRADE_WAR and TRADE_EMBARGO labels through:
    GET_TEAM(eWhoFrom).getName()
This treats the player ID as though it were a team ID. Vanilla/Civ4CE BtS uses
    GET_TEAM(GET_PLAYER(eWhoFrom).getTeam()).getName()
and AdvCiv originally preserved that semantics through its TEAMREF(eWhoFrom)
macro, where TEAMREF(ePlayer) was defined as GET_TEAM(TEAMID(ePlayer)).

The regression can be pinned exactly to AdvCiv practical 1679
(1c56cc6ac1...), whose broad TEAMREF-removal refactor mechanically changed:
    TEAMREF(eWhoFrom)
to:
    GET_TEAM(eWhoFrom)
for all three trade-item labels. That loses the required PlayerTypes ->
TeamTypes conversion. In common one-player-per-team games player IDs often
match team IDs and mask the problem. In real team games they need not: hovering
'Make peace with', 'Declare war on' or 'Stop trading with' trade items can name
the unrelated team whose team index happens to equal eWhoFrom, rather than the
from-player's actual team. This is tooltip/diplomacy UI truth, not gameplay
trade execution. Conservative repair: GET_TEAM(GET_PLAYER(eWhoFrom).getTeam())
(or TEAMID(eWhoFrom)) in all three labels.

ADDITIONAL REJECTED / CLEARED ITEMS SINCE WIP02
- MISSION_BUILD feature-tech lookup is safe for builds whose feature prereq is
  NO_TECH: CvTeam::isHasTech(NO_TECH) deliberately returns true, so the branch
  never calls GC.getInfo(NO_TECH).
- MISSION_BUILD getBuildTurnsLeft can theoretically return MAX_INT for zero
  future work rate, but no normal reachable build-action hover with that state
  has yet been established; do not conflate it with the proven GameRecord
  production sentinel root.
- MISSION_DISCOVER validates canDiscover before indexing the discovery tech;
  canDiscover explicitly rejects NO_TECH and zero research.
- MISSION_HURRY's production key is protected by the surrounding production-
  building/action semantics; no independent null-string defect established.
- The potential MISSION_LEAD queued-destination discrepancy is not promoted:
  the actual warlord experience action resolves the units present at execution
  time, and there is not yet a defensible contract that the hover should
  predict a queued destination stack.

ANCESTRY DETAIL FOR F066
Tracked commit-diff evidence is local in:
  _SNAPSHOT_CONTEXT/commit_diffs/AdvCivPreSAS_1679_1c56cc6ac1.diff
The same diff explicitly removes:
  #define TEAMREF(ePlayer) GET_TEAM(TEAMID(ePlayer))
and replaces the three
  TEAMREF(eWhoFrom)
expressions in CvDLLWidgetData with raw GET_TEAM(eWhoFrom). The prior practical
1417 trade-item refactor still used TEAMREF correctly, and Civ4CE/BtS retains
the original explicit GET_PLAYER(eWhoFrom).getTeam() conversion.

WIP03 CORRECTION — FORMER F066 / KI#387 IS RETRACTED
----------------------------------------------------
WIP03 claimed that parseTradeItem's GET_TEAM(eWhoFrom), where eWhoFrom is a
PlayerTypes value, treated the player's integer ID as a TeamTypes value. That
claim is false in current AdvCiv/SAS.

The relevant accessor is deliberately overloaded:
  CvGamePlay.h:
    getTeam(TeamTypes eTeam)      -> CvTeam::getTeam(eTeam)
    getTeam(PlayerTypes ePlayer)  -> CvTeam::getTeam(TEAMID(ePlayer))
  CoreAI.h has the analogous pair for CvTeamAI.
And GET_TEAM(x) expands through the overloaded CvGamePlay/CoreAI getter in this
translation-unit environment. Therefore GET_TEAM(eWhoFrom) with a statically
PlayerTypes expression already performs the required PlayerTypes -> TeamTypes
conversion. AdvCiv practical 1679's TEAMREF removal preserved semantics via this
overload; comparing the call text mechanically to vanilla BtS was insufficient.

The new staged explicit conversion:
  GET_TEAM(GET_PLAYER(eWhoFrom).getTeam())
is behaviorally equivalent and harmless, but it does NOT repair a team-game bug.
KI#387's current documentation therefore should be retracted/corrected rather
than preserved as a fixed defect. If the explicit conversion is retained, it is
only a readability/defensive-explicitness change.

This directly repeats the accessor lesson already recorded by rejected KI#311
in the completed historical_source_archaeology.txt sweep. KI#311 traced the AI
translation-unit form, CoreAI::getTeam(PlayerTypes), while this file uses the
parallel non-AI CvGamePlay::getTeam(PlayerTypes) overload. Both resolve through
TEAMID(ePlayer); KI#311 should therefore be cross-linked from KI#387 so either
overload family leads future audits to the shared negative-control evidence.

This is a useful negative control: ancestry can look compelling while a type-
overloaded API preserves the old semantics invisibly at the call site. Future
GET_TEAM(PlayerTypes) findings must inspect overload resolution before promotion.

NEW F066 / provisional KI#388 — INFO-SCREEN POWER HOVER IMPORTS SCOREBOARD-ONLY
EXPANSION STATE/TIMER SIDE EFFECT
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Current SAS adds WIDGET_POWER_RATIO cells to the Info Screen's custom Score tab:
  Assets/Python/Screens/CvInfoScreen.py
    SASTextScale.setTableTextLabel(...,
      WidgetTypes.WIDGET_POWER_RATIO, ePlayer, iPowerColorForWidget, ...)

CvDLLWidgetData::parseHelp handles WIDGET_POWER_RATIO in two independent ways:
  1. parsePowerRatioHelp(...) renders the intended ratio tooltip;
  2. the post-switch scoreboard expansion block unconditionally calls
       GET_PLAYER(getActivePlayer()).setScoreboardExpanded(true)
     for WIDGET_POWER_RATIO regardless of iData2.
The AdvCiv comment immediately above this special case explicitly says that
WIDGET_POWER_RATIO needs iData2 for another purpose and therefore this WidgetType
"must only" be used on the scoreboard.

SAS practical 5483 / commit e92a36c49dca11cdb4f28526d85887a2c63012f0
introduced the new Info Screen Score tab and reused WIDGET_POWER_RATIO there.
The commit's stated purpose is the sortable Score tab/power display; there is no
scoreboard-expansion intent.

The side effect is real rather than merely a dirty-bit nuisance. In
CvPlayer::setScoreboardExpanded, when the UI is focused (as with an advisor
screen) and Scores__ExpandOnHover is enabled, b=true can set
m_bScoreboardExpanded and schedules UPDATE_COLLAPSE_SCORE_BOARD. A collapse
callback while UI focus remains active calls setScoreboardExpanded(false), but
that focused branch does not clear the state (except diplomacy-specific logic);
it schedules another collapse callback and returns. Thus hovering an Info Screen
power cell can start/maintain the scoreboard's hidden expansion state and timer
loop until focus ends, and can leave the scoreboard briefly/stalely expanded as
the advisor closes before a later callback collapses it.

This is SAS-specific cross-caller contract drift: the tooltip widget was reused
outside the only context its C++ side-effect code says it supports. Gameplay is
not affected, but UI state/timer behavior is. Conservative repair directions
include a context-distinct power-ratio widget/helper or removing/guarding the
scoreboard-expansion side effect for non-scoreboard callers; simply using
WIDGET_GENERAL would avoid the side effect but would also lose the useful power
hover.

Negative control: BUG/Scoreboard.py is the intended original caller and uses
WIDGET_POWER_RATIO on the scoreboard, where expansion-on-hover is desired.

NEW F067 / provisional KI#389 — SPECIALIST "EXTRA SLOTS" REUSES A TECH-CONTEXT
SPECIAL-BUILDING WIDGET WITH NO_TECH, PRODUCING FALSE HOVER TEXT
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SAS practical 5331 / commit d0360f3c4757a6b7a13181029bddd8fa2474ec3d
added SevoPediaSpecialist's Extra Slots panel. For grouped special-building
sources (Temple/Cathedral/Monastery), it constructs the button as:
  WIDGET_HELP_SPECIAL_BUILDING, data1=-1, data2=iSpecialBuilding

But CvDLLWidgetData::parseSpecialBuildingHelp forwards data1 as a TechTypes:
  GAMETEXT.buildSpecialBuildingString(buffer,
      (TechTypes)data1, (SpecialBuildingTypes)data2)
and CvGameTextMgr::buildSpecialBuildingString is explicitly a tech-unlock
formatter. It emits "Can Construct ..." when TechPrereq == eTech and
"Any player can Construct ..." when TechPrereqAnyone == eTech.

Current SAS CIV4SpecialBuildingInfos.xml has Temple, Cathedral and Monastery
TechPrereqAnyone=NONE. NONE is -1, exactly the value supplied by the Specialist
page. Therefore the sentinel is not neutral: it matches TechPrereqAnyone and can
make an Extra Slots source button claim "Any player can Construct Temple /
Cathedral / Monastery". That text does not describe why the building class is
shown and is false/misleading in this context.

Controls: CvTechChooser.py and SevoPediaTech.py also use
WIDGET_HELP_SPECIAL_BUILDING, but correctly pass a real tech ID in data1. The
C++ helper therefore behaves according to its intended contract; the defect is
the SAS Specialist-page caller's widget/payload choice, discovered through this
C++ contract audit.

Conservative repair: do not feed NO_TECH to this tech-context widget for the
Extra Slots grouping. Use a neutral/non-tech widget if the generic special-
building icon is only representative, or introduce a dedicated Pedia/source
hover that describes the special-building family/slot relationship.

F066 / provisional KI#388 — BROADENED: INFO SCREEN SCORE TAB REUSES THREE
SCOREBOARD-ONLY EXPANSION WIDGETS, IMPORTING HIDDEN SCOREBOARD STATE/TIMERS
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WIP05 first isolated this through the custom Score tab's WIDGET_POWER_RATIO
cells. The caller audit now proves the same root at three independent Score-tab
cell families in Assets/Python/Screens/CvInfoScreen.py:

  active score cell:  WIDGET_SCORE_BREAKDOWN with data2=0
  power ratio cell:   WIDGET_POWER_RATIO
  trade-network cell: WIDGET_TRADE_ROUTES_SCOREBOARD

CvDLLWidgetData::parseHelp's post-switch expansion block treats all three as
scoreboard controls:
  - WIDGET_SCORE_BREAKDOWN expands when data2==0;
  - WIDGET_POWER_RATIO expands unconditionally;
  - WIDGET_TRADE_ROUTES_SCOREBOARD expands unconditionally.

The surrounding AdvCiv comment explicitly documents the scoreboard-only data
contract for this machinery (notably WIDGET_POWER_RATIO's occupied data2).
CvPlayer::setScoreboardExpanded then mutates the active player's scoreboard
expansion state and schedules UPDATE_COLLAPSE_SCORE_BOARD callbacks. While an
advisor owns UI focus, collapse attempts can reschedule rather than clear the
state, so hovering these Info Screen cells can create/maintain hidden scoreboard
state and timer churn until focus ends.

This is best kept as ONE cross-context root, not three findings: the custom Info
Screen Score tab reused scoreboard UI widgets for their useful text hovers but
also inherited their unrelated scoreboard-expansion behavior. The power cell
was simply the first observable instance. Repair should separate tooltip
semantics from scoreboard-only state mutation or guard the expansion side effect
to actual scoreboard context.

NEW F068 / provisional KI#390 — MILITARY-ADVISOR "TOTAL COST + SUPPLY" ROW USES
THE GLOBAL-INFLATED-EXPENSES TOOLTIP, SO ITS HOVER EXPLAINS UNRELATED COSTS
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The custom Military Advisor Summary tab displays:
  Total Cost + Supply = post-inflation Unit Cost + post-inflation Unit Supply.
The row binds:
  WIDGET_HELP_FINANCE_INFLATED_COSTS, data1=self.iActivePlayer, data2=1.

CvDLLWidgetData::parseFinanceInflatedCosts does NOT describe that subtotal. It
calls CvGameTextMgr::buildFinanceInflationString, which obtains:
  CvPlayer::calculatePreInflatedCosts()
and the engine definition is:
  calculateUnitCost() + calculateUnitSupply() + getTotalMaintenance()
    + getCivicUpkeep() + Python extraExpenses().
Thus the hover's pre-inflation base and inflation amount can include city
maintenance, civic upkeep and other expenses that are absent from the visible
"Total Cost + Supply" value.

The original/current Domestic Finance screen is the clean control: it attaches
WIDGET_HELP_FINANCE_INFLATED_COSTS to totalInflatedCosts / the overall Expenses
row, where the helper's global expense-base contract matches the displayed
number. The Summary tab's own introductory comment says rows bind the matching
finance widget "where applicable", and its legend defines Total Cost + Supply
specifically as Unit Cost + Unit Supply. Therefore this is not an intentional
whole-economy hover; it is a cross-caller widget mismatch introduced with the
Military Advisor Summary tab (initial implementation practical 5800).

This is diagnostic/UI accuracy only, not gameplay. Conservative repair: use no
hover on this subtotal, combine the already-correct Unit Cost and Away Supply
breakdowns in a dedicated helper, or add a purpose-specific Cost+Supply tooltip.
Do not use the global inflation/total-expenses helper for this row.

ADDITIONAL NEGATIVE CONTROL
---------------------------
- The Summary tab's WIDGET_HELP_FINANCE_UNIT_COST and
  WIDGET_HELP_FINANCE_AWAY_SUPPLY reuses are legitimate: their C++ helpers call
  the exact player Unit Cost and Unit Supply breakdown builders. The mismatch is
  specific to the combined Total Cost + Supply row.
- WIDGET_HELP_FINANCE_NUM_UNITS is intentionally disabled by AdvCiv and therefore
  produces no tooltip; using it on the Summary Total Units row is a no-op rather
  than a false breakdown, so it is not promoted as a separate bug at this point.

NEW F069 / provisional KI#391 — OPTIONAL BUG DETAILED/WIDE TECH SPLASH PASSES
BUILD ID AS TECH ID AND HARD-CODES BUILD ID 1 TO WIDGET_HELP_IMPROVEMENT
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Both live optional BUG tech-splash implementations contain the same caller:

  Assets/Python/Contrib/TechWindow.py
  Assets/Python/Contrib/TechWindowWide.py

For a build enabled by the displayed technology whose BuildInfo has no
ImprovementType, they attach the build button as:

  WIDGET_HELP_IMPROVEMENT, j, 1

where j is the BuildTypes index.

The C++ widget contract is the opposite kind of payload:

  CvDLLWidgetData::parseBuildHelp
    data1 -> TechTypes
    data2 -> BuildTypes

and forwards them to:

  CvGameTextMgr::buildImprovementString(szBuffer, eTech, eBuild)

which tests the supplied build's technology prerequisite/feature technologies
against the supplied technology and, on a match, prints "Can build <Build>".

The normal/current CvTechChooser is the control: it passes (iTech, j), i.e. the
actual technology in data1 and actual BuildTypes index in data2.

Concrete current-SAS reachability:
  Build 0  BUILD_ROAD          -> TECH_THE_WHEEL
  Build 1  BUILD_RAILROAD      -> TECH_STEAM_POWER
  Build 18 BUILD_REMOVE_JUNGLE -> feature tech TECH_METAL_CASTING
  Build 19 BUILD_REMOVE_FOREST -> feature tech TECH_MASONRY
  Build 20 BUILD_SCRUB_FALLOUT -> feature tech TECH_ENVIRONMENTALISM

All five have ImprovementType=NONE and therefore use this erroneous TechWindow
branch. data2 is always 1, i.e. BUILD_RAILROAD. data1 becomes raw build IDs
0/1/18/19/20, which in current TechInfos correspond to unrelated technologies
(Mysticism, Meditation, Liberalism, Democracy, Corporation). None is Steam
Power, so buildImprovementString finds no match and the intended hover is empty.
The same payload also prevents executeAltAction's WIDGET_HELP_IMPROVEMENT path
from jumping anywhere useful because Build 1 has no ImprovementType.

This is not SAS-specific. The exact bad pair is already present when the BUG
TechWindow assets enter reachable K-Mod history in practical 4 / commit
593c549b51b5726302484ad8d0e08602b010d4a9 ("assets to go with the initial
commit"). Both Detailed and Wide variants contain it there. The UI remains live:
Assets/Config/TechWindow.xml exposes View = Original|Detailed|Wide and
CvScreensInterface.createTechSplash imports TechWindow/TechWindowWide when the
corresponding user option is selected.

Classification: inherited BUG/K-Mod UI tooltip/payload bug; no gameplay effect.
Conservative repair: pass self.iTech (or the displayed technology ID) as data1
and j as data2 in both files, matching CvTechChooser's established contract.

ADDITIONAL NEGATIVE CONTROLS / PROGRESS
---------------------------------------
- Shifted mission-help plot dereference is safe: CvSelectionGroup::lastMissionPlot
  falls back to the group's current plot when no queued movement target exists.
- WIDGET_HELP_OBSOLETE / _BONUS / _SPECIAL casts in CvDLLWidgetData look odd
  locally, but their CvGameTextMgr functions intentionally accept an int item ID
  and reinterpret it as BuildingTypes / BonusTypes / SpecialBuildingTypes; the
  current TechChooser callers therefore match the real callee contract.
- WIDGET_HELP_TERRAIN_TRADE's -1 hazard is not reached by current callers:
  terrain callers pass a real TerrainTypes index and river trade deliberately
  uses getNumTerrainInfos() as its sentinel, which parseTerrainTradeHelp handles.

    WidgetTypes.WIDGET_PEDIA_JUMP_TO_UNIT; no broader behavior change is needed.

ADDITIONAL CLOSURE / NEGATIVE-CONTROL RESULTS SINCE WIP07

1. Header inventory discrepancy is old dead declaration residue, not broken wiring.
   - Current CvDLLWidgetData.cpp: 168 live out-of-line definitions.
   - Header: 170 declarations.

   - No callers/definitions exist in current CvGameCoreDLL, base AdvCiv or Civ4CE.
   - K-Mod practical 3 / KMod_3_2414225e64.diff already introduced these two header
     declarations without implementations. Keep as inherited dead residue unless a

2. WIDGET_CHOOSE_EVENT active-player use is valid.
   - Its live caller is CvDLLButtonPopup's in-game triggered-event popup; this is not an
     opening-menu/Pedia path. The unguarded active player in parseEventHelp therefore
     has the intended caller contract.

3. Shifted mission plot dereference is valid.
   - CvSelectionGroup::lastMissionPlot() falls back to the group's current plot, so
     parseActionHelp_Mission does not obtain NULL merely because there is no queued
     shifted destination.

   - CvTeam::isHasTech(NO_TECH) intentionally succeeds for the no-prerequisite case.
   - The Sevopedia WIDGET_HELP_IMPROVEMENT NO_TECH caller is also intentional:
     CvGameTextMgr::buildImprovementString handles feature-specific prerequisite techs
     when BuildInfo::getTechPrereq()==NO_TECH.

   - Obvious non-scoreboard Tech/Pedia callers inspected so far pass -1 or 1, not zero.

F071 / provisional KI#393 — MULTI-UNIT BOMBARD PREVIEWS COUNT INELIGIBLE UNITS

CvDLLWidgetData.cpp MISSION_BOMBARD help (current ~2914):
  for each UI-selected unit:
      iDamage += kSelectedUnit.damageToBombardTarget(kMissionPlot);

The preview does not require kSelectedUnit.canMove() or
kSelectedUnit.canBombard(kMissionPlot). By contrast, actual execution in
CvSelectionGroup::startMission repeatedly calls
AI_bestUnitForMission(MISSION_BOMBARD), and that helper explicitly skips units
that cannot move or fail canBombard(kAt).

Reachable example: a selected siege group contains one fresh siege unit and one
unit that has already spent its attack/bombard for the turn. At least one unit
can still bombard, so the action remains available. The tooltip sums nominal
damage from both; execution skips the spent unit. The preview can therefore
promise more city-defense reduction than the mission can deliver.

Sibling in CvGameTextMgr::getAirBombPlotHelp:
  - the helper first establishes that one best unit can air-bomb the hovered
    target;
  - for revealed city targets it then loops over every selected unit and adds
    airBombDefenseDamage without checking canMove/canAirBombAt(target).
Thus a fresh eligible aircraft plus an exhausted or out-of-range selected
companion can similarly overstate city-defense damage.

Concrete SAS XML range control: Fighter range 6 / bomb rate 8; Bomber range 8 /
bomb rate 16; Jet Fighter range 10 / bomb rate 12; Stealth Bomber range 12 /
bomb rate 20; Guided Missile range 5 / bomb rate 16. For a city 7 tiles away,
a Bomber can be valid while a selected Fighter is out of range; once the valid
best-unit gate passes, the city preview still adds the Fighter's nominal
airBombDefenseDamage.

Ancestry: the ground and air multi-unit previews were introduced together in
AdvCiv practical 2778 / commit 4098fbb24851e4cdc6f2f615f4458adedf97da75,
"Various UI improvements for bombard and air bomb missions." The same commit
also added per-unit eligibility-aware execution, making the omitted tooltip
filters especially clear.

Conservative repair direction:
  - ground preview: add damage only for units that can move and
    canBombard(kMissionPlot) (or otherwise mirror the actual mission selector);
  - air-city preview: add damage only for units that can move and
    canAirBombAt(kPlot).
Do not merely test bombard/air-bomb base rate: target/move-state eligibility is
the mismatch.

F072 / provisional KI#394 — AIR BOMB BEST-UNIT SELECTION CAN ABORT A VALID GROUP ORDER

Current CvSelectionGroupAI::AI_bestUnitForMission(MISSION_AIRBOMB, pMissionPlot):
  - common loop filters !pUnit->canMove();
  - city branch tests pUnit->canAirBomb(pMissionPlot), NOT canAirBombAt;
  - CvUnit::canAirBomb's pFrom parameter is explicitly unused and it tests only
    generic air-bomb ability / attack-spent state;
  - non-city branch has no target-eligibility test at all.

Actual group execution in CvSelectionGroup::startMission is:
  while ((pUnit = AI_bestUnitForMission(MISSION_AIRBOMB, &kMissionPlot)) != NULL &&
         pUnit->airBomb(kMissionPlot, ...))
      ...
CvUnit::airBomb begins with canAirBombAt(kTarget) and returns false if the chosen
unit cannot attack that target. Because the while condition then fails, the
mission loop stops instead of trying another eligible selected aircraft.

CvSelectionGroup::canDoMission(MISSION_AIRBOMB) proves normal reachability:
it returns true if ANY selected unit canAirBombAt(target), so invalid companion
units are allowed to remain in a group whose Air Bomb order is valid.

Concrete current-SAS example from CIV4UnitInfos.xml:
  UNIT_BOMBER: iBombRate=16, iAirRange=8, cost=180
  UNIT_JET_FIGHTER: iBombRate=12, iAirRange=10, cost=170
Against a city 9 tiles away, the Jet Fighter is target-valid and makes the
mission available; the Bomber is out of range. City prioritization nevertheless
uses nominal airBombDefenseDamage and can prefer the higher-rate Bomber. The
chosen Bomber's airBomb(target) then rejects range 9 and the while loop stops,
so the valid Jet Fighter never acts. The same eligibility omission exists for
non-city air-bomb targets.

Ancestry:
  - practical 2778 / 4098fbb248... introduced the smarter grouped Air Bomb
    ordering with the same generic canAirBomb test for city targets and no
    target filter in the non-city ordering;
  - practical 2784 / 160fa499a896cf595f74bcbde68b7ce41bedc466 factored the
    ordering into CvSelectionGroupAI::AI_bestUnitForMission and preserved the
    missing target-eligibility check exactly;
  - later bombard-priority work (e.g. practical 2809) retained it.

Conservative repair direction: in MISSION_AIRBOMB selection, reject any unit
that does not pUnit->canAirBombAt(*pMissionPlot) (using the proper current
signature/origin semantics) before ranking it. That aligns selection with
canDoMission, CvUnit::airBomb, and the target-specific mission contract.

  defect also affects Paradrop interception help. Multi-unit special-mission
  previews choose/aggregate selected units without first filtering to the units

F071 PARADROP SIBLING (ADDED AFTER WIP09)
CvGameTextMgr::getParadropPlotHelp chooses the selected unit with the greatest
interception-evasion value before applying target/move eligibility. It then
emits interception help only if that chosen unit canParadropAt the target.

This differs from actual group execution: CvSelectionGroupAI::AI_bestUnitForMission
(MISSION_PARADROP) first requires canMove and canParadropAt(target), then ranks
eligible units. Current SAS has UNIT_PARATROOPER with equal drop/evasion stats,
so a normal mixed-move-state selection is enough: one tied Paratrooper has
already moved, another is fresh. The fresh unit keeps the Paradrop order valid
and is the unit execution can use, but the help loop can retain the moved tied
unit and then suppress interception information when its final canParadropAt
check fails.

This is normalized under F071 because its cause and repair principle are the
same as the already-recorded Ground Bombard and Air Bomb preview cases: first
restrict the help population to mission-executable units at the target, then
aggregate/rank them. Do not create a separate finding merely for each mission.

REJECTED F073 CANDIDATE — MISSION_HEAL MAX_INT IS SEMANTICALLY REAL
parseActionHelp_Mission computes the group Heal ETA as the maximum healTurns()
of all selected units, and CvUnit::healTurns returns MAX_INT when a hurt unit's
net heal rate at the plot is <= 0. At first this looked like another raw sentinel
leak.

Engine semantics show otherwise:
- CvUnit::canHeal rejects healTurns()==MAX_INT, so such a unit alone cannot make
  the Heal mission available.
- If another selected unit can heal, the group can nevertheless enter
  ACTIVITY_HEAL.
- CvUnit::doTurn applies feature turn-damage and then doHeal to hurt stationary
  units; the group remains ACTIVITY_HEAL while any member remains hurt (for a
  human group, absent the separate sentry-danger wake condition).
- Therefore a hurt selected unit whose healing is exactly cancelled/exceeded by
  feature turn damage really can keep the group from ever becoming fully healed
  at that plot. MAX_INT is meaningful as an infinite group ETA in that state,
  not a finite-production-style sentinel masquerading as ordinary data.

Result: reject this candidate. F073 stays unused.

OTHER POST-WIP09 CONTROLS
- Airlift, Recon and Range Strike group execution re-validates target eligibility
  per selected unit; one invalid companion does not abort later valid units.
- Paradrop execution is properly eligibility-filtered; only its interception
  help selection shares F071's preview-population defect.
- The apparent WIDGET_HELP_OBSOLETE parse/alt payload mismatch is not a bug:
  buildObsoleteString takes an int item/building identifier despite the local
  TechTypes cast annotation, and the callers/alt-action consistently encode a
  BuildingTypes value.
- WIDGET_HELP_FREE_UNIT intentionally dual-encodes unit in data1 and tech in
  data2; parse and alt-action consume the appropriate half.

C012-WIP14 RECONSTRUCTED CONTINUATION — file 012 CvDLLWidgetData.cpp — OPEN
Authoritative current source: AdvCiv-SAS practical 6250 / HEAD 2ac3faa2f6cb45cee0d417728410d325c32668ea
Reconstruction note: the attached C012-WIP10 artifact above is preserved exactly as the physical prefix. The original WIP11-WIP13 files were not re-uploaded into this new chat; their deltas below are reconstructed from the preserved previous-thread transcript and rechecked against the current practical-6250 source where relevant. This avoids pretending byte-for-byte possession of artifacts that are no longer mounted while preserving the audit state faithfully. No source files modified.

RECOVERED WIP11 DELTA
F073 / provisional KI#395 — BUG/AdvCiv Chevron angry-citizen overlay uses the specialist widget contract
Status: inherited current UI defect; source/caller contract strong.
Evidence:
- The Chevron city-specialist display creates the ordinary angry-citizen button with WIDGET_ANGRY_CITIZEN.
- It then draws a 10x10 DDS chevron directly over that angry-citizen button but tags the overlay WIDGET_CITIZEN with data1=j.
- The neighboring specialist chevrons intentionally use WIDGET_CITIZEN because they really are specialist overlays; the angry-citizen branch is the copy/paste outlier.
- addDDSGFC carries its own widget/data payload. Hovering the overlay therefore routes through parseCitizenHelp with j interpreted as a SpecialistTypes value; right-click routes to that specialist's Pedia instead of the angry-citizen contract.
Repair direction:
- Tag the angry-citizen chevron overlay with WIDGET_ANGRY_CITIZEN and the same payload convention as the underlying angry-citizen button, or make the overlay non-interactive if the engine permits that without breaking rendering.
Rejected neighboring smell:
- WIDGET_HELP_OBSOLETE's local TechTypes cast is misleading but not a runtime payload bug; buildObsoleteString consumes the integer as a Building ID, matching the caller/right-click contract.

RECOVERED WIP12 DELTA
F074 / provisional KI#396 — native Bonus/Tech trade-denial widgets lost their old Pedia fallback click/link behavior
Status: inherited AdvCiv native-widget migration regression; pre/post behavior source-provable.
Evidence:
- Before AdvCiv practical 1419, the Foreign Advisor used BugDll.widget(... fallback=WIDGET_PEDIA_JUMP_TO_BONUS/TECH ...). Because the custom enum was not native, the fallback widgets supplied ordinary clickable Pedia navigation and isLink behavior.
- Practical 1419 made WIDGET_PEDIA_JUMP_TO_BONUS_TRADE and WIDGET_PEDIA_JUMP_TO_TECH_TRADE native and added their richer denial-help parsing.
- The native migration did not add the corresponding executeAction/isLink Pedia behavior, so selecting the custom widget stops using the clickable fallback.
- WIDGET_DEAL_KILL is a clean migration control: when AdvCiv made that custom behavior native, it retained action/link machinery rather than assuming every rich-hover widget should be parse-only.
- The bonus import column may encode its BonusTypes payload with +1000; any native Pedia action needs to normalize that encoding before jumping.
Repair direction:
- Restore Pedia click/link semantics for the native trade-denial widgets while preserving their denial hover text and payload encoding.

RECOVERED WIP13 DELTA + POST-WIP13 NORMALIZATION
F075 / provisional KI#397 — practical-1481 native BULL hover migration dropped contact fallback actions
Status: inherited AdvCiv native-widget migration regression; two sibling manifestations, one root.
Manifestation A — WIDGET_LEADERHEAD_RELATIONS:
- Before practical 1481 (4a65bf5c0f...), callers were wrapped in BugDll.widgetVersion(... fallback=WIDGET_LEADERHEAD ...).
- The fallback's C++ alt action contacted the civilization.
- The Military/Foreign Advisor Python right-click handling still hides the advisor in preparation for that contact action.
- Practical 1481 made WIDGET_LEADERHEAD_RELATIONS native and added rich hover parsing but no matching executeAltAction. A right-click can therefore hide the advisor and then do nothing instead of contacting the civ.
Manifestation B — scoreboard trade-network widget:
- Before practical 1481, the scoreboard trade icon used BugDll.widget("WIDGET_TRADE_ROUTES", ..., fallback=WIDGET_CONTACT_CIV, rival, -1), inheriting ordinary click-to-contact behavior.
- Practical 1481 made the rich trade-routes hover native but added no execute action, dropping the fallback contact.
- Practical 3396 (cf04fab335...) later split the scoreboard form to WIDGET_TRADE_ROUTES_SCOREBOARD only to stop Foreign Advisor hovers from expanding the scoreboard; it did not restore or intentionally revoke contact behavior.
Repair direction:
- Restore the lost contact action(s) explicitly on the native widgets, preserving the current rich hover behavior and the later scoreboard-only expansion distinction.
Negative control:
- WIDGET_DEAL_KILL shows that native migration can and does retain explicit click/link semantics when intended.

POST-WIP13 STRICT-SWEEP CONTROL — WIDGET_MENU_ICON FALL-THROUGH REJECTED AS CURRENT BUG
- parseHelp has WIDGET_MENU_ICON fall through into WIDGET_CONSCRIPT, whose parser uses assign() and would overwrite "Main Menu" help with conscription help when a selected city exists.
- executeAction likewise calls doMenu() and then falls through to doConscript(). This shape is inherited and also present in the local controls.
- Current normal UI hides the Main Menu button while the city screen / selected-city context is active, which masks the harmful city-dependent side of the fall-through in supported ordinary use.
- Keep as ugly inherited control-flow / robustness debt, not F076, unless a normal reachable selected-city + visible-menu path is later proven.

F076 / provisional KI#398 — group Espionage can abort a valid order by trying the first movable non-Spy
Status: NEW INHERITED BTS GAMEPLAY / GROUP-MISSION CONTRACT DEFECT ROOT; current SAS reproduction and vanilla/BtS control proof strong.

Mission-availability contract:
- CvGame::canHandleAction delegates mission legality to the selected interface group's canStartMission.
- CvSelectionGroup::canDoMission(MISSION_ESPIONAGE) iterates the group and returns true when ANY unit satisfies canEspionage(target, bTestVisible) and, when required, canMove().
- Therefore a mixed same-domain group legitimately exposes/enables Espionage when one selected Spy can act, even if other selected units cannot.

Execution contract failure:
- CvSelectionGroup::startMission iterates units in group order, skipping only units that cannot move.
- MISSION_ESPIONAGE calls pUnit->espionage(...) on the first movable unit it reaches and then unconditionally jumps to exit_unit_loop, explicitly to allow one unit at a time to do espionage.
- CvUnit::espionage immediately returns false when canEspionage(plot()) is false; CvUnit::canEspionage rejects every non-Spy through !isSpy().
- Consequently the executor stops after a failed attempt by an ineligible movable unit rather than continuing until it reaches the Spy that made the group mission legal.

Concrete current SAS reproduction:
- CvUnit::canJoinGroup allows same-owner units on the same plot to group when their domains match; it does not forbid Spy + combat-unit groups.
- Current UNIT_AXEMAN is DOMAIN_LAND, DefaultUnitAI=UNITAI_ATTACK, bSpy=0.
- Current UNIT_SPY is DOMAIN_LAND, DefaultUnitAI=UNITAI_SPY, bSpy=1.
- CvSelectionGroup::addUnit orders units by AI_groupFirstVal (then second value). UNITAI_ATTACK has first value 13; UNITAI_SPY has 9. Thus a normal Axeman + Spy group deterministically puts the Axeman before the Spy.
- On a rival-owned plot where the fresh Spy is legally able to perform espionage and the fresh Axeman can move, canDoMission returns true because of the Spy.
- startMission reaches the Axeman first; Axeman::espionage returns false because it is not a Spy; the unconditional exit prevents the eligible Spy from acting. A valid Espionage order can therefore produce no espionage action/popup.

Help sibling:
- parseActionHelp_Mission(MISSION_ESPIONAGE) uses the head selected unit kUnit and passes only that unit to setEspionageMissionHelp.
- setEspionageMissionHelp immediately returns for !isSpy(). In the same Axeman + Spy ordering, the action is enabled by the Spy but Spy-specific mission help is omitted because the head is the Axeman.
- This reinforces the same group-selection assumption, though the executor failure is independently sufficient for F076.

Ancestry/control:
- The local Civ4CE BtS source has the same contract split: canDoMission iterates until any pLoopUnit->canEspionage succeeds, while execution attempts espionage on the current loop unit and then sets pUnitNode=NULL regardless of success to stop after one unit.
- The AdvCiv/SAS goto exit_unit_loop is a readability/control-flow rewrite of that inherited BtS behavior, not the birth of the defect.

Conservative repair direction:
- Preserve the "one Spy per Espionage command" rule, but exit the unit loop only after selecting/attempting an actually eligible Spy.
- With canMove already filtered by the outer loop, the MISSION_ESPIONAGE case can continue past units that fail canEspionage(plot()) and stop after the first eligible Spy performs/opens the espionage action.
- Keep the two-phase/popup semantics intact; do not allow multiple Spies to execute one user command.
- For help, derive the espionage-help subject from an eligible selected Spy rather than blindly from the group head.

CURRENT REJECTION / CLOSURE NOTES CARRIED FORWARD
- The two extra CvDLLWidgetData.h declarations doCancelCivics and applyCityEdit remain dead inherited header residue: no live definitions/callers established in current, base AdvCiv, or Civ4CE controls.
- Generic enum casts without reachable bad widget producers remain non-findings; theoretical forged payloads are not being promoted.
- WIDGET_EXAMINE_CITY's direct city lookup remains protected by ordinary live-owned-city callers; no supported stale-city path has been proven.
- parseTradeRoutes' player IDs remain valid across all live callers inspected.
- plot-list static iUnitsPerRow is refreshed on each forward-shift sequence rather than acting as a permanently stale layout cache.
- MISSION_BUILD worker ETA does not double-count workers already performing the same build: getBuildTurnsLeft accounts for active builders while widget help adds selected workers only when their current build differs.
